P·L MARTYROLOGIUM 2 124

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 124 ·

MARTYROLOGIUM 2

PL 124, coll. 9–857  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  First English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Usuard of Saint-Germain

Usuardus Sangermanensis

d. c. 875

Monk of the abbey of Saint-Germain-des-Prés at Paris. In 858 he travelled to Spain to bring back relics for his house, and came home with those of martyrs of Córdoba. At the request of Charles the Bald he compiled a martyrology for the whole year, drawing on the Hieronymian lists, Bede, Florus of Lyon and Ado of Vienne, and dedicated it to the king. Its brevity made it the most widely copied martyrology of the Middle Ages, and it is the direct ancestor of the Roman Martyrology of 1584.

SEQUITUR USUARDI MARTYROLOGIUM.

THE MARTYROLOGY OF USUARDUS FOLLOWS.

MENSIS MAIUS HABET DIES XXXI.

MAY HAS 31 DAYS.

Kalendis Die 1.

  • 9

In Aegypto, Hieremiae prophetae, qui a populo lapidibus obrutus, apud Taphnas occubuit. Item, natalis beatorum apostolorum Philippi et Jacobi, ex quibus Philippus, postquam pene Schytiam ad fidem Christi convertisset, apud Hierapolim Asiae civitatem glorioso fine quievit. Jacobus vero, qui et frater Domini legitur, juxta templum Hierosolimorum, ubi et praecipitatus fuerat, sepultus est. In Galliis territorio Vivariensi, beati Andeoli subdiaconi, quem misit ab Oriente sanctus Policarpus una cum aliis, ad praedicandum verbum Dei, in Galliam, cujus praedicatione imperator Severus comperta, spinosis fustibus crudelissime flagellatum, in carcerem trudi praecepit, et sic demum ab ense lignato, in crucis modum caput ejus scindi. Civitate Sedunensi, natalis Sigismundi regis. Apud civitatem Auscium, sancti Orientis episcopi, qui virtutibus ornatus sancto fine quievisse scribitur. Item Autisiodoro, sancti Amatoris episcopi et confessoris.

On the Kalends · 1 May

  • 9

In Egypt, of Hieremias the prophet, who, overwhelmed with stones by the people, fell at Taphnae. Also, the birthday of the blessed apostles Philip and James, of whom Philip, after he had converted almost all Schytia to the faith of Christ, rested with a glorious end at Hierapolis, a city of Asia. But James, who is also read to be the brother of the Lord, was buried beside the temple of Jerusalem, where also he had been cast down headlong. In the Gauls, in the territory Vivariensis, of blessed Andeolus, subdeacon, whom Saint Policarpus sent from the East together with others to preach the word of God in Gaul; and the emperor Severus, having learned of his preaching, ordered him, most cruelly scourged with thorny cudgels, to be thrust into prison, and so at last his head to be split in the form of a cross with a wooden sword. In the city Sedunensis, the birthday of Sigismundus, king. At the city of Auscium, of Saint Orientis, bishop, who, adorned with virtues, is written to have rested in a holy end. Also at Auxerre, of Saint Amator, bishop and confessor.

VI Non. Die 2.

  • 13

Natalis sancti Patris nostri Athanasii, Alexandrinae urbis episcopi, et confessoris. In hujus persecutionem, universus pene conjuravit orbis, et commoti sunt principes terrae, gentes, regna, et exercitus cojerunt adversus eum, multaque Arianorum perpessus insidiis, toto orbe profugus agitur, nec ei ullus tutus ad latendum supererat locus, dum ad investigandum eum omnes moventur. Moritur autem quadragesimo et sexto anno sacerdotii sui, post multos agones, multasque patientiae coronas, tempore Valentiniani et Valentis. Eodem die, sanctorum Saturnini et Neopolis, qui in carcere requieverunt.

The sixth day before the Nones · 2 May

  • 13

The birthday of our holy Father Athanasius, bishop of the city of Alexandria, and confessor. For his persecution almost the whole world conspired, and the princes of the earth were moved, and nations, kingdoms and armies came together against him; and having suffered many plots of the Arians, he is driven a fugitive through the whole world, nor was any safe place left him for hiding, while all are set in motion to track him down. He dies, however, in the forty-sixth year of his priesthood, after many contests and many crowns of patience, in the time of Valentinian and Valens. On the same day, of Saints Saturninus and Neopolis, who rested in prison.

V Non. Die 3.

  • 15

Hierosolimis, inventio sanctae Crucis ab Helena regina, sub Constantino principe. Romae via Numentana, passio beati Alexandri papae, Eventii et Theodoli presbiterorum, sub Adriano principe, quorum primus post vincula, carceres, equuleum, ungula et ignes, punctis creberrimis per tota membra peremptus est. 17Sequentes quoque, post longam carceris sustinentiam, ignibus examinati, et ad ultimum decollati sunt. Ipso die, sancti Juvenalis episcopi et confessoris.

The fifth day before the Nones · 3 May

  • 15

At Jerusalem, the finding of the holy Cross by Queen Helena, under the emperor Constantine. At Rome, on the Numentan Way, the passion of blessed Alexander, pope, and of Eventius and Theodolus, presbyters, under the emperor Hadrian; of whom the first, after chains, prisons, the rack, the claw and fires, was slain by very frequent piercings over all his limbs. 17The following ones also, after a long endurance of prison, were tried by fire, and at the last beheaded. On this same day, of Saint Juvenalis, bishop and confessor.

IV Non. Die 4.

  • 19

In Palaestina civitate Gaza, natalis beati Silvani ejusdem urbis episcopi: qui persecutione Diocletiani, cum plurimis clericorum suorum, martyrio coronatus est. Hierosolymis, passio sancti Quiriaci episcopi, sub Juliano imperatore. Item in metalla Fanensi, sanctorum martyrum quadraginta, qui simul capite caesi sunt. Nicomediae, natalis Antoniae martyris, quae nimium torta et variis afflicta cruciatibus, ab uno brachio tribus diebus suspensa et in carcerem biennio detrusa, ad ultimum in confessione Domini flammis exusta est. In Norico Ripensi, sancti Floriani, qui praesidis jussu, ligato ad collum saxo in flumen Anisii praecipitatus est. Autisiodoro, sancti Curcodemi diaconi.

The fourth day before the Nones · 4 May

  • 19

In Palestine, in the city of Gaza, the birthday of blessed Silvanus, bishop of the same city: who, in the persecution of Diocletian, was crowned with martyrdom with very many of his clergy. At Jerusalem, the passion of Saint Quiriacus, bishop, under the emperor Julian. Also in the mines Fanensis, of the forty holy martyrs, who were beheaded together. At Nicomedia, the birthday of Antonia, martyr, who, exceedingly tortured and afflicted with various torments, hung by one arm for three days and thrust into prison for two years, at the last was burned up with flames in the confession of the Lord. In Noricum Ripense, of Saint Florianus, who by the governor's order was cast headlong into the river Anisius with a stone tied to his neck. At Auxerre, of Saint Curcodemus, deacon.

III Non. Die 5.

  • 23

Apud Alexandriam, sancti Euthimii diaconi, in carcere quiescentis. Thessalonicae, natalis sanctorum Hirenei, Peregrini et Hirenis, ignibus combustorum. In Galliis civitate Arelatensi, beati Hilarii episcopi et confessoris, praeclarissimi viri et nobilissimi. Item Viennae, sancti Niceti episcopi, venerabilis sanctitatis viri. Autisiodoro, passio sancti Joviniani lectoris et martyris.

The third day before the Nones · 5 May

  • 23

At Alexandria, of Saint Euthimius, deacon, resting in prison. At Thessalonica, the birthday of Saints Hireneus, Peregrinus and Hirene, burned with fires. In the Gauls, in the city of Arles, of blessed Hilary, bishop and confessor, a most illustrious and most noble man. Also at Vienne, of Saint Niceti, bishop, a man of venerable holiness. At Auxerre, the passion of Saint Jovinianus, reader and martyr.

Pridie Non. Die 6.

  • 27

Natalis beati Joannis apostoli, quando ab Epheso, jussu Domitiani Romam perductus, et praesente senatu ante portam Latinam in olei ferventis dolium missus est, sed minime laesus. Ubi festum eadem die concursum fideles populi usque hodie faciunt. Antiochiae, sancti Evodii qui, ut beatus Ignatius scribit, primus ibidem episcopus ab Apostolis ordinatus est. Hic glorioso martyrio apud eamdem urbem vitam finivit. Item sancti Lucii Cyrenensis, qui apud Cyrenem primus episcopus a sanctis Apostolis institutus est, quem in Actibus apostolorum beatus Lucas commemorat. In Africa, sanctorum martyrum Heliodori, Venusti, cum aliis septuaginta quinque.

The day before the Nones · 6 May

  • 27

The birthday of blessed John the apostle, when, brought from Ephesus to Rome by order of Domitian, and in the presence of the senate before the Latin Gate, he was put into a jar of boiling oil, but was not in the least harmed. Where the faithful peoples make a festal gathering on the same day to this day. At Antioch, of Saint Evodius, who, as blessed Ignatius writes, was ordained the first bishop there by the Apostles. He ended his life by a glorious martyrdom in the same city. Also of Saint Lucius the Cyrenian, who was appointed the first bishop at Cyrene by the holy Apostles, whom blessed Luke commemorates in the Acts of the apostles. In Africa, of the holy martyrs Heliodorus, Venustus, with seventy-five others.

Nonis. Die 7.

  • 29

Apud Terracinam Campaniae urbem, natalis beatae Domitillae virginis et martyris, quae cum esset Flavii Clementis Consulis, ex sorore neptis, et a sancto Clemente velamine sacro consecrata, persecutione Domitiani, cum aliis plurimis, in insulam Pontiam exilio deportata, longum inibi martyrium duxit. Novissime doctrina, ac miraculis cum convertisset plurimos ad fidem Domini, incenso cubiculo a quodam judice, in quo simul cum suis convirginibus Eufrosina videlicet et Theodora clausa morabatur, cursum gloriosi martyrii sui consummavit. Eodem die, sancti Juvenalis martyris. Nicomediae, passio sanctorum Flavii, Augusti et Augustini fratrum.

On the Nones · 7 May

  • 29

At Terracina, a city of Campania, the birthday of blessed Domitilla, virgin and martyr, who, being the niece, by his sister, of Flavius Clemens the Consul, and consecrated with the sacred veil by Saint Clement, was carried off into exile to the island of Pontia in the persecution of Domitian, with very many others, and there endured a long martyrdom. Last of all, when by her teaching and miracles she had converted very many to the faith of the Lord, the chamber being set on fire by a certain judge, in which she was dwelling shut up together with her fellow virgins, namely Eufrosina and Theodora, she completed the course of her glorious martyrdom. On the same day, of Saint Juvenalis, martyr. At Nicomedia, the passion of Saints Flavius, Augustus and Augustinus, brothers.

VIII Idus. Die 8.

  • 33

Mediolani, natalis sancti Victoris martyris, qui a primaeva aetate Christianus, cum esset in castris imperialibus miles, compellente Maximiano, ut sacrificaret, in confessione Domini fortissime perseverans, primo graviter fustibus caesus, sed Deo protegente, doloris expers, dein liquenti plumbo perfusus, sed nihil penitus laesus, novissime gloriosi martyrii cursum capitis abscisione complevit. Apud Aegyptum, sanctorum Stephani et Victoris.

The eighth day before the Ides · 8 May

  • 33

At Milan, the birthday of Saint Victor, martyr, who, a Christian from his earliest age, when he was a soldier in the imperial camp, and Maximian was compelling him to sacrifice, persevering most bravely in the confession of the Lord, was first grievously beaten with cudgels, but, God protecting him, free of pain; then drenched with molten lead, but not harmed at all; last of all he completed the course of his glorious martyrdom by the cutting off of his head. At Egypt, of Saints Stephen and Victor.

VII Idus. Die 9.

  • 35

Festivitas beati Andreae apostoli, quando sacratissimum ejus corpus una cum ossibus sanctorum Lucae evangelistae, et Timothaei discipuli sancti Pauli, sub Constantio imperatore, Constantinopolim translatum est. Romae, natalis sancti Hermen, cujus apostolus Paulus, ad Romanos scribens, meminit. Hic digne semetipsum Deo sacrificans, acceptabilisque Deo hostia factus, virtutibus clarissime fundatus, coeli regna petivit. Apud Nazanzi oppidum, beati Gregorii episcopi, cognomento Theologi, qui post multa documenta, in loco suo, ut Hieronymus, ab eodem in divinis libris instructus, perhibet, episcopum ordinans, ruri vitam monachi exercuit. In Perside, sanctorum martyrum trecentorum decem. Castro Vindicino, depositio beati confessoris.

The seventh day before the Ides · 9 May

  • 35

The festival of blessed Andrew the apostle, when his most sacred body, together with the bones of Saints Luke the evangelist and Timothy the disciple of Saint Paul, was translated to Constantinople under the emperor Constantius. At Rome, the birthday of Saint Hermes, whom the apostle Paul, writing to the Romans, mentions. He, worthily sacrificing himself to God, and made a victim acceptable to God, most clearly grounded in virtues, sought the kingdoms of heaven. At the town of Nazanzus, of blessed Gregory, bishop, surnamed the Theologian, who after many teachings, ordaining a bishop in his own place, as Jerome, instructed by him in the divine books, reports, practised the life of a monk in the country. In Persis, of the three hundred and ten holy martyrs. At the fort of Vindicinum, the deposition of the blessed confessor.

VI Idus. Die 10

  • 39

Job prophetae. Romae via Latina, natalis sanctorum martyrum Gordiani et Epimachi, quorum prior pro confessione nominis Christi, tempore Juliani, diu plumbatis caeditur, et in ultimo, capite truncatur. Item Romae, beati Calepodii presbyteri, sub Alexandro imperatore, qui fecit eum occidi gladio et corpus trahi per civitatem atque in Tyberim jactari. Decollatus est etiam Palmatius consul, cum uxore et filiis et aliis promiscui sexus quadraginta duobus; cum quibus et Simplicius senator. Quorum capita jussa sunt suspendi per diversas portas urbis Romae, ad exemplum Christianorum. Item Romae via Latina ad centum aulas, natalis sanctorum Quarti et Quinti.

The sixth day before the Ides · 10 May

  • 39

Of Job the prophet. At Rome, on the Latin Way, the birthday of the holy martyrs Gordianus and Epimachus, of whom the former, for the confession of the name of Christ, in the time of Julian, is long beaten with leaded scourges, and at the last is cut off by the head. Also at Rome, of blessed Calepodius, presbyter, under the emperor Alexander, who caused him to be slain with the sword and his body to be dragged through the city and thrown into the Tiber. There was beheaded also Palmatius the consul, with his wife and sons and forty-two others of both sexes; with whom also Simplicius the senator. Whose heads were ordered to be hung up at the various gates of the city of Rome, as an example to the Christians. Also at Rome, on the Latin Way, at the Hundred Halls, the birthday of Saints Quartus and Quintus.

V Idus. Die 11.

  • 43

Romae via Salaria, natalis beati Anthimi presbiteri, qui post virtutum et praedicationis insignia, in Tyberim praecipitatus et ab angelo exinde ereptus, oratorio restitutus est proprio, dehinc capite punitus, victor migravit ad coelos. Viennae, sancti Mamerti episcopi et confessori, qui ob imminentem cladem, solemnes ante Ascensionem Domini Letanias instituit.

The fifth day before the Ides · 11 May

  • 43

At Rome, on the Salarian Way, the birthday of blessed Anthimus, presbyter, who after the distinctions of virtues and of preaching, cast headlong into the Tiber and rescued from it by an angel, was restored to his own oratory; thereafter punished by the loss of his head, he passed as victor to the heavens. At Vienne, of Saint Mamertus, bishop and confessor, who, on account of an impending disaster, instituted the solemn Litanies before the Ascension of the Lord.

IV Idus. Die 12.

  • 45

Romae via Ardeatina, natalis sanctorum martyrum Nerei et Achillei, fratrum, qui primo apud insulam Pontiam longum duxerunt exilium, et postmodum a quodam judice verberibus gravissimis sunt attrectati, deinde cum equuleo et flammis compellerentur ad immolandum, et dicerent se a beato Petro apostolo baptizatos, nulla ratione posse idolis immolare, capite caesi sunt. Item via Aurelia, sancti Pancratii martyris, qui cum esset annorum quatuordecim, sub Diocletiano martyrium capitis detruncatione complevit. Eodem die, sancti Dionisii, patrui ejusdem beati Pancratii, qui cum praemiis caelestibus, carus et dignus Deo, in pace diem vitae hujus clausit. Apud Cyprum, sancti patris nostri Epiphanii Salaminae episcopi.

The fourth day before the Ides · 12 May

  • 45

At Rome, on the Ardeatine Way, the birthday of the holy martyrs Nereus and Achilleus, brothers, who first underwent a long exile on the island of Pontia, and afterwards were handled by a certain judge with most grievous blows; then, when they were being compelled with the rack and flames to sacrifice, and said that they, baptized by blessed Peter the apostle, could by no means sacrifice to idols, they were beheaded. Also on the Aurelian Way, of Saint Pancratius, martyr, who, when he was fourteen years old, under Diocletian, completed his martyrdom by the cutting off of his head. On the same day, of Saint Dionisius, uncle of the same blessed Pancratius, who, with heavenly rewards, dear and worthy to God, closed the day of this life in peace. At Cyprus, of our holy father Epiphanius, bishop of Salamina.

III Idus. Die 13.

  • 49

Natalis sanctae Mariae ad martyres, quando beatus Bonifacius papa ecclesiam in honore semper virginis Mariae et omnium martyrum dedicavit. Item, beati Mucii presbyteri et martyris, qui primo in Amfibuli urbe, multis poenis atque cruciatibus in confessione Christi affectus, postea Byzantium usque perductus, capitali sententia occubuit. Ipso die, sancti Servatii Tungrensis Ecclesiae episcopi et confessoris, ob cujus meritum hominibus demonstrandum, cum tempore hiemis omnia in circuitu nix repleret, numquam sepulchrum ejus operuit, donec industria civium, basilica super ipsum aedificata est.

The third day before the Ides · 13 May

  • 49

The birthday of Saint Mary at the Martyrs, when blessed Boniface the pope dedicated a church in honour of Mary ever virgin and of all the martyrs. Also of blessed Mucius, presbyter and martyr, who, first in the city of Amfibuli afflicted with many pains and torments in the confession of Christ, afterwards brought as far as Byzantium, fell by capital sentence. On this same day, of Saint Servatius, bishop of the Church Tungrensis and confessor; for the showing of whose merit to men, when in the time of winter the snow filled all things round about, it never covered his sepulchre, until, by the diligence of the citizens, a basilica was built over it.

Pridie Idus Die 14.

  • 51

In Gallia civitate Cimela, natalis Pontii martyris, cujus praedicatione et industria, postquam conversi sunt duo Philippi imperatores ad fidem Christi, ipse quoque sub Valeriano et Galieno principibus martyrii palmam adeptus est. In Syria, sanctorum martyrum Victoris et Coronae, sub Antonino imperatore, quae Corona cum esset uxor cujusdam, et coepisset beatificare sanctum Victorem, ob martyrii constantiam, vidit duas coronas de coelo lapsas, unam Victori, et alteram sibi missam. Cumque et hoc cunctis audientibus 53protestaretur, ipsa quidem inter arbores scissa, Victor vero decollatus est. Eodem die, sancti Patris nostri Pachomii, qui cum esset factis apostolicae gratiae insignis, fundatorque Aegypti coenobiorum, scripsit monachorum regulas, quas angelo dictante didicerat.

The day before the Ides · 14 May

  • 51

In the Gauls, in the city of Cimela, the birthday of Pontius, martyr, by whose preaching and diligence, after the two emperors Philip were converted to the faith of Christ, he himself also, under the emperors Valerian and Gallienus, obtained the palm of martyrdom. In Syria, of the holy martyrs Victor and Corona, under the emperor Antoninus; which Corona, since she was the wife of a certain man, and had begun to call Saint Victor blessed on account of the constancy of his martyrdom, saw two crowns fallen from heaven, one sent to Victor and the other to herself. And when she was declaring this also in the hearing of all, 53she indeed was torn apart between trees, but Victor was beheaded. On the same day, of our holy Father Pachomius, who, being distinguished by deeds of apostolic grace, and founder of the monasteries of Egypt, wrote rules for monks, which he had learned at an angel's dictation.

Idibus. Die 15.

  • 55

Natalis sanctorum confessorum Torquati, Tesifontis, Secundi, Indalecii, Cecilii, Esicii, Eufrasii, qui Romae a sanctis apostolis ordinati, et ad praedicandum verbum Dei, ad Hispanias tunc adhuc gentili errore implicatas, directi sunt. Cumque diversis urbibus evangelizassent et innumeras multitudines Christi fidei subjugassent, Torquatus Acci, Tesifon Vergii, Secundus Abulae, Indalecius Urci, Cecilius Heliberri, Esicius Carcesi, Eufrasius Eliturgi quieverunt. Apud insulam Chium, beati Isidori martyris, in cujus basilica exstat puteus, in quo et fertur injectus, de cujus aqua infirmi saepius potati salvantur. Lamosacum, passio sanctorum Petri et Andreae, Pauli et Dionisiae.

On the Ides · 15 May

  • 55

The birthday of the holy confessors Torquatus, Tesifon, Secundus, Indalecius, Cecilius, Esicius, Eufrasius, who were ordained at Rome by the holy apostles, and sent to preach the word of God to the Spains, at that time still entangled in the error of the gentiles. And when they had evangelized in divers cities and had subjected innumerable multitudes to the faith of Christ, Torquatus at Acci, Tesifon at Vergium, Secundus at Abula, Indalecius at Urci, Cecilius at Heliberri, Esicius at Carcesi, Eufrasius at Eliturgi, rested. At the island of Chios, of blessed Isidore, martyr, in whose basilica there stands a well, into which also he is said to have been cast, from whose water the sick, often given to drink, are made whole. At Lamosacum, the passion of Saints Peter and Andrew, Paul and Dionisia.

XVII Kal. Die 16.

  • 59

In Isauria provincia, natalis sanctorum Aquilini et Victoriani, quorum gesta habentur. Apud Autisiodorum, beati Peregrini episcopi, primi civitatis ipsius, qui capitali sententia multatus, coronam meruit sempiternam. In pago Forojuliensi, sanctae Maximae virginis, quae multis clara virtutibus, in pace quievit.

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 16 May

  • 59

In the province of Isauria, the birthday of Saints Aquilinus and Victorianus, whose acts are extant. At Auxerre, of blessed Peregrinus, bishop, the first of that city, who, punished by capital sentence, merited an everlasting crown. In the district Forojuliensis, of Saint Maxima, virgin, who, renowned for many virtues, rested in peace.

XVI Kal. Die 17.

  • 61

In Tuscia, sancti Torpetis martyris. Hic magnus in officio Neronis Caesaris primo fuit; sed postea pro fide Christi, jubente eodem, alapis caeditur, verberibus diutissime afficitur, ac bestiis devorandus traditur, sed minime laeditur; tandem vero decollatione martyrium suum III Kal. Maias complevit. Sed tamen ejus festivitas celebrius hic recolitur. Civitate Niveduno, sanctorum Eraclii, Pauli, Aquilini, cum aliis duobus.

The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 17 May

  • 61

In Tuscia, of Saint Torpes, martyr. He was at first great in the service of Nero Caesar; but afterwards, for the faith of Christ, at the order of the same man he is struck with blows of the hand, is afflicted with scourgings for a very long time, and is handed over to be devoured by beasts, but is not in the least harmed; at length, however, he completed his martyrdom by beheading on the third day before the Kalends of May. But nevertheless his festival is more solemnly kept here. In the city of Nivedunum, of Saints Eraclius, Paul, Aquilinus, with two others.

XV Kal. Die 18.

  • 65

In Aegypto, sancti Dioscori lectoris, in quem praeses multa et varia tormenta exercuit, ita ut ungues ejus effoderet et lampadibus ejus latera inflammaret, sed coelestis luminis fulgore territi ceciderunt ministri, novissime laminis ardentibus adustus martyrium consummavit. Ipso die, sancti Felicis episcopi, qui apud Spellatensem urbem, sub Maximiano imperatore, palmam martyrii adeptus est.

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 18 May

  • 65

In Egypt, of Saint Dioscorus, reader, upon whom the governor exercised many and various torments, so that he dug out his nails and set his sides aflame with torches; but the attendants, terrified by the brightness of a heavenly light, fell down; last of all, burned with glowing plates, he consummated his martyrdom. On this same day, of Saint Felix, bishop, who at the city Spellatensis, under the emperor Maximian, obtained the palm of martyrdom.

XIV Kal. Die 19.

  • 67

Romae via Appia, natale sanctorum Caloceri et Parthenii eunuchorum, quorum prior cum esset praepositus cubiculi imperatoris Decii, alter primicerius, nolentes sacrificare idolis, ab eodem occisi sunt. Item Romae, sanctae Potentianae virginis, quae post innumeros agones, post multorum martyrum venerabiliter exhibitas sepulturas, post omnes facultates suas pauperibus pro Christo erogatas, tandem de terris ad coelos migravit. Eodem ipso die, beati Pudentis patris supradictae virginis, qui ab apostolis Christo in baptismo vestitus, innocentem tunicam usque ad coronam vitae immaculatae custodivit et servavit.

The fourteenth day before the Kalends · 19 May

  • 67

At Rome, on the Appian Way, the birthday of Saints Calocerus and Parthenius, eunuchs, of whom the former was chamberlain of the emperor Decius, the other primicerius; refusing to sacrifice to idols, they were slain by the same man. Also at Rome, of Saint Potentiana, virgin, who, after innumerable contests, after the burials of many martyrs reverently performed, after all her means were paid out to the poor for Christ, at length passed from earth to the heavens. On the same very day, of blessed Pudens, father of the aforesaid virgin, who, clothed with Christ in baptism by the apostles, kept and preserved the innocent tunic unto the crown of a spotless life.

XIII Kal. Die 20.

  • 71

Romae via Salaria, natalis sanctae Basillae virginis et martyris, quae cum esset ex regio genere et haberet sponsum illustrissimum, accusata ab eodem quod esset Christiana, decretum est a Galieno Augusto, ut aut sponsum reciperet, aut gladio interiret. Conventa de hoc, et renuens assensum praebere, gladio transverberata est. In Galliis civitate Nemauso, sancti Baudelii martyris, qui comprehensus, cum sacrificare nollet, et in fide Christi immobilis inter verbera et tormenta persisteret, martyrii palmam pretiosa morte percepit. Bituricas, beati Austregisili episcopi et confessoris.

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 20 May

  • 71

At Rome, on the Salarian Way, the birthday of Saint Basilla, virgin and martyr, who, since she was of royal stock and had a most illustrious betrothed, was accused by him of being a Christian, and it was decreed by Gallienus Augustus that she should either receive her betrothed or perish by the sword. Being summoned about this, and refusing to give assent, she was pierced through with the sword. In the Gauls, in the city of Nemausus, of Saint Baudelius, martyr, who, when he was seized and would not sacrifice, and persisted immovable in the faith of Christ amid blows and torments, received the palm of martyrdom by a precious death. At Bourges, of blessed Austregisilus, bishop and confessor.

XII Kal. Die 21.

  • 73

In Mauritania Caesariensi, natalis sanctorum Timothei, Polii et Eutichii diaconi, qui apud praefatam regionem verbum Domini disseminantes, pariter coronari meruerunt. Item apud Caesaream Cappadociae, natalis sanctorum Polyeucti, Victorii et Donati. Cordubae, Secundini S. Secundinus lituram occupat in codice. Quis olim aut quae ejus loco fuit? Nullo tamen scripturarum discrimine. BOUILLART. martyris.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 21 May

  • 73

In Mauritania Caesariensis, the birthday of Saints Timotheus, Polius and Eutichius, deacon, who, sowing the word of the Lord in the aforesaid region, merited to be crowned together. Also at Caesarea of Cappadocia, the birthday of Saints Polyeuctus, Victorius and Donatus. At Cordoba, of Secundinus Saint Secundinus occupies an erasure in the codex. What saint, man or woman, once stood in his place? Yet with no difference in the scripts. Bouillart., martyr.

XI Kal. Die 22.

  • 77

In Africa, natalis sanctorum Casti et Emilii, qui, ut scribit beatus Cyprianus in libro de Lapsis, per ignem passionis martyrium consummaverunt. Apud Corsicam, sanctae Juliae, quae crucis supplicio coronata est. Autisiodoro, Helenae virginis.

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 22 May

  • 77

In Africa, the birthday of Saints Castus and Emilius, who, as blessed Cyprian writes in the book On the Lapsed, consummated their martyrdom through the fire of the passion. At Corsica, of Saint Julia, who was crowned with the punishment of the cross. At Auxerre, of Helena, virgin.

X Kal. Die 23.

  • 79

Apud Lingones, passio sancti Desiderii episcopi. Hic cum plebem suam ab exercitu Wandalorum vexari cerneret, pro ea supplicaturus exiens, statim jugulari est jussus, quique pro ovibus sibi creditis cervicem libenter tetendit, et percussus gladio migravit ad Christum. Passi sunt autem cum eo et alii plures de numero gregis sui, et apud eamdem urbem conditi.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 23 May

  • 79

At Langres, the passion of Saint Desiderius, bishop. He, when he saw his people harried by the army of the Wandals, going out to make supplication for them, was at once ordered to be slain; and he, who willingly stretched out his neck for the sheep entrusted to him, was struck with the sword and passed to Christ. There suffered with him also many others of the number of his flock, and they were buried in the same city.

IX Kal. Die 24.

  • 81

Natalis Manahen, Herodis tetrachae collactanei, doctoris et prophetae sub gratia Novi Testamenti; apud Antiochiam in Christo quiescentis. Item beatissimae Joannae, uxoris Chuzae, procuratoris Herodis, quam commemorant Evangelistae. In Portu Romano, natalis sancti Vincentii martyris. In Galliis civitate Namnetis, beatorum martyrum Donatiani et Rogatiani fratrum, qui pro constantia fidei in carcerem missi, et in 83equulei catasta suspensi ac laniati, deinde lancea militari perfossi, novissime capita eorum praecisa sunt. Apud Histrian, sanctorum martyrum Zoelli, Servilii, Felicis, Silvani et Dioclis.

The ninth day before the Kalends · 24 May

  • 81

The birthday of Manahen, foster-brother of Herod the tetrarch, a teacher and prophet under the grace of the New Testament, resting in Christ at Antioch. Also of most blessed Joanna, wife of Chuza, the steward of Herod, whom the Evangelists commemorate. At Portus Romanus, the birthday of Saint Vincent, martyr. In the Gauls, in the city of Namnetis, of the blessed martyrs Donatianus and Rogatianus, brothers, who for the constancy of their faith were put into prison, and 83hung up on the scaffold of the rack and torn, then pierced through with a soldier's lance; last of all their heads were cut off. At Histria, of the holy martyrs Zoellus, Servilius, Felix, Silvanus and Diocles.

VIII Kal. Die 25.

  • 85

Romae via Numentana, natalis beati Urbani papae et martyris, cujus doctrina, sub persecutione Alexandri, multi martyrio coronati sunt. Mediolani, sancti Dionisii episcopi et confessoris, qui apud Cappadociam pro fide Catholica damnatus exilio, ibidem primo quievit, postea vero reliquias corporis ejus, per sanctum Basilium praefatae urbis episcopum receptas, beatus Ambrosius condigno honore condidit. Apud Mesiam civitate Dorostoro, sanctorum martyrum Passecratis. Valentionis, et aliorum duorum simul coronatorum. In territorio Trecassino, sancti Leonis confessoris.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 25 May

  • 85

At Rome, on the Numentan Way, the birthday of blessed Urbanus, pope and martyr, by whose teaching, under the persecution of Alexander, many were crowned with martyrdom. At Milan, of Saint Dionisius, bishop and confessor, who, condemned to exile in Cappadocia for the Catholic faith, rested there at first; but afterwards the relics of his body, received through Saint Basil, bishop of the aforesaid city, blessed Ambrose laid to rest with fitting honour. At Mesia, in the city of Dorostorum, of the holy martyrs Passecratis. Valentio, and two others crowned together. In the territory of Troyes, of Saint Leo, confessor.

VII Kal. Die 26.

  • 89

Apud Athenas, natalis beati Quadrati episcopi, Apostolorum discipuli, qui persecutione Adriani, Ecclesiam grandi terrore dispersam, fide et industria sua congregans, etiam librum pro Christianae religionis defensione edidit, plenum fidei, et Apostolica doctrina dignum. Romae, sanctorum martyrum Simmitrii presbyteri et aliorum viginti duorum. Ipso item die, sancti Quadrati martyris, in cujus solemnitate sancti Augustini sermones habiti inveniuntur. Tuder Tusciae, natalis sanctorum Felicissimi, Eraclii, Paulini. In territorio Autisiodorensi, passio sancti Prisci martyris, cum ingenti multitudine. In Britanniis, sancti Augustini episcopi et confessoris, qui missus a beato papa Gregorio, primus genti Anglorum Christi Evangelium praedicavit.

The seventh day before the Kalends · 26 May

  • 89

At Athens, the birthday of blessed Quadratus, bishop, disciple of the Apostles, who, in the persecution of Hadrian, gathering together by his faith and diligence the Church scattered by great terror, also published a book in defence of the Christian religion, full of faith and worthy of Apostolic doctrine. At Rome, of the holy martyrs Simmitrius, presbyter, and twenty-two others. On this same day also, of Saint Quadratus, martyr, on whose solemnity sermons of Saint Augustine are found to have been delivered. At Tuder of Tuscia, the birthday of Saints Felicissimus, Eraclius, Paulinus. In the territory of Auxerre, the passion of Saint Priscus, martyr, with a vast multitude. In the Britains, of Saint Augustine, bishop and confessor, who, sent by blessed Pope Gregory, first preached the Gospel of Christ to the nation of the English.

VI Kal. Die 27.

  • 93

Apud Mesiam civitate Dorostorensi, natalis beati Julii, qui, tempore persecutionis, cum esset veteranus et emeritae militiae, comprehensus ab officialibus, judici est oblatus, quo praesente, cum idola execraretur, et nomen Christi constantissime confiteretur, capitali sententia punitus est. In Galliis civitate Arausica, sancti Eutropi episcopi, cujus vitam, illustrem virtutibus ac miraculis, Verus episcopus luculento sermone describit. In territorio Adartensi, sancti Ragnulfi martyris.

The sixth day before the Kalends · 27 May

  • 93

At Mesia, in the city Dorostorensis, the birthday of blessed Julius, who, in the time of persecution, being a veteran and of completed service, was seized by the officials and brought before the judge; in whose presence, when he cursed the idols and most constantly confessed the name of Christ, he was punished by capital sentence. In the Gauls, in the city Arausica, of Saint Eutropius, bishop, whose life, illustrious with virtues and miracles, Verus the bishop describes in a splendid discourse. In the territory Adartensis, of Saint Ragnulfus, martyr.

V Kal. Die 28.

  • 95

Romae, natalis beati Joannis papae, quem refert sanctus Gregorius, ob orthodoxam fidem, primo in custodia tentum, ac deinde ad mortem usque perductum, cum aliis aeque catholicis viris. Parisius civitate, transitus sancti Germani confessoris. Qui beatus ejusdem urbis episcopus, quantae sanctitatis, quantique meriti fuerit, quibus etiam claruerit miraculis, Fortunatus episcopus veraci stylo digessit. In Sardinia, sanctorum Aemilii, Felicis, Priami, Luciani. Eodem die, sancti Charauni, qui apud Carnotum civitatem, capite amputato, martyrium sumpsit.

The fifth day before the Kalends · 28 May

  • 95

At Rome, the birthday of blessed John, pope, whom Saint Gregory relates to have been first held in custody for the orthodox faith, and then brought even to death, with other equally catholic men. In the city of Paris, the passing of Saint Germanus, confessor. Of how great holiness and how great merit this blessed bishop of the same city was, and by what miracles also he shone, Fortunatus the bishop has set forth with a truthful pen. In Sardinia, of Saints Aemilius, Felix, Priamus, Lucianus. On the same day, of Saint Charaunus, who at the city of Carnotum, his head being cut off, took up martyrdom.

IV Kal. Die 29.

  • 99

Apud Iconium civitatem Isauriae, sub Aureliano imperatore, passio sancti Cononis martyris et filii ejus, qui primum ignito ferro superpositi, deinde craticulam prunis suppositis, et oleo super infuso constanter superaverunt. Postea vero tam frixorii quam suspensionis in equuleo, ac ignis penuriam passi, malleo ad extremum ligneo In codice haec omnia : Postea vero tam frixorii quam suspensionis in equuleo ac ignis poenuriam passi, malleo ad extremum ligneo in litura versantur. Eadem tamen exhibent codices omnes. Vetustior igitur haec litura est, quam ullum Usuardini Martyrologii apographum. BOUILLART. manibus eorum contritis, in laudem omnipotentis Dei spiritum emiserunt. Eodem die, sanctorum Sisinnii, Martyrii, atque Alexandri, qui in Anauniae partibus, ut scribit sanctus Paulinus, persequentibus gentilibus viris, martyrii coronam adepti sunt. Treviris, beati Maximini episcopi et confessoris, a quo Athanasius antistes, persecutionem Constantii fugiens, honorifice susceptus est. Romae via Aurelia, natalis sancti Restituti. Via Tiburtina, septem germanorum.

The fourth day before the Kalends · 29 May

  • 99

At Iconium, a city of Isauria, under the emperor Aurelian, the passion of Saint Conon, martyr, and of his son, who, first placed upon red-hot iron, then, with live coals set beneath and oil poured over, constantly overcame the gridiron. But afterwards, having suffered the frying-pan as well as the hanging on the rack, and the want of fire, their hands at the last crushed with a wooden mallet In the codex all these words, But afterwards, having suffered the frying-pan as well as the hanging on the rack, and the want of fire, with a wooden mallet at the last, lie on an erasure. Yet all the codices show the same. This erasure, therefore, is older than any copy of Usuardus's Martyrology. Bouillart., they gave up the spirit in praise of almighty God. On the same day, of Saints Sisinnius, Martyrius, and Alexander, who in the parts of Anaunia, as Saint Paulinus writes, the gentile men persecuting them, obtained the crown of martyrdom. At Trier, of blessed Maximinus, bishop and confessor, by whom the bishop Athanasius, fleeing the persecution of Constantius, was honourably received. At Rome, on the Aurelian Way, the birthday of Saint Restitutus. On the Tiburtine Way, of seven brothers.

III Kal. Die 30.

  • 101

Romae via Aurelia, natalis beati Felicis papae, qui cum annis quinque rexisset Ecclesiam, sub Claudio principe martyrio coronatus est. Turribus Sardiniae, sanctorum martyrum Gabini et Crispoli. Ipso die, sancti Hucberti episcopi et confessoris.

The third day before the Kalends · 30 May

  • 101

At Rome, on the Aurelian Way, the birthday of blessed Felix, pope, who, when he had ruled the Church for five years, was crowned with martyrdom under the emperor Claudius. At Turres of Sardinia, of the holy martyrs Gabinus and Crispolus. On this same day, of Saint Hucbertus, bishop and confessor.

Pridie Kal. Die 31.

  • 105

Romae, sanctae Petronillae virginis, quae post multa miracula sanitatum, cum eam quidam Comes suo vellet conjugio sociare, tridui inducias postulans, jejuniis atque orationibus vacans, tertio die, mox ut Christi Sacramentum accepit, emisit spiritum. Apud urbem Aquilejam, natalis sanctorum martyrum Cantii, Cantiani et Cantianillae fratrum, qui cum ducerent prosapiam de genere Aniciorum, ob Christianae fidei constantiam, una pedagogo suo Proto capite plexi sunt. Turribus Sardiniae, Crescentiani martyris.

The day before the Kalends · 31 May

  • 105

At Rome, of Saint Petronilla, virgin, who, after many miracles of healing, when a certain Count wished to join her to himself in marriage, asking a respite of three days, and giving herself to fastings and prayers, on the third day, as soon as she received the Sacrament of Christ, gave up her spirit. At the city of Aquileia, the birthday of the holy martyrs Cantius, Cantianus and Cantianilla, brothers, who, since they drew their descent from the family of the Anicii, were beheaded for the constancy of the Christian faith, together with their tutor Protus. At Turres of Sardinia, of Crescentianus, martyr.

MENSIS JUNIUS HABET DIES XXX.

  • 109

JUNE HAS 30 DAYS.

  • 109

Kalendis Die 1.

  • 109

Romae, dedicatio sancti Nicomedis presbiteri et martyris. Apud Caesaream Palestinae sub persecutione Maximini, beati Pamphili presbiteri et martyris, cujus Vitam Eusebius Caesariensis episcopus tribus libris comprehendit. Sed et sanctus Hieronymus scribit, quaedam volumina manu ejus exarata se reperisse, quae tanto, inquit, amplector et servo gaudio, ut Cresi opes habere me credam. Augustoduni, sanctorum Reveriani episcopi et Pauli presbiteri, cum aliis decem, qui sub Aureliano principe martyrio coronati sunt. Ipso die, sancti Caprasii abbatis monasterii Lirinensis. [ Addit Bouill.: Ista cryptarum dedicatio San.-Germanensis Ecclesiae ita propria est, ut mirum non sit eam ab iis qui Martyrologium Usuardi exscripserunt, fuisse praetermissam. Sollerio tamen satis est argumenti, longam illam orationem tribuendam Usuardo non esse, quod alii codices fere omnes repugnant, et praesertim Pratensis B, in quo Sirmondus erasam esse testatus est. Haec postrema ratio, fateor, Sollerio debuit nonnihil negotii facessere. Sed in culpa est tamen, cum eam ob causam negat ab Usuardo scriptum esse, quod perfecta scripturae similitudo scriptum ab illo fuisse persuadet. Nodus erat solutu difficilis ? Expectaret aliquandiu, seque sustineret ; percunctaretur per litteras quid apud Usuardum cryptae sint ; cur semel et iterum annuntietur illa dedicatio ; cur illam Pratensis codex B silentio praetereat. Haec erant accurati editoris. Paucis nodum solvo. Ubi nunc in monasterio San-Germanensi exstructum visitur elegantissimum B. Virginis Mariae sacellum, cryptae olim fuerunt, seu altaria subterranea, quorum dedicationis festus dies, Usuardi tempore, Kalendis Junii agebatur. Postea eadem dedicatio celebrata est IV Idus, ut patet ex Prat. codicibus ; quae tandem celebrari desiit, postquam B. Mariae sacellum ab Hugone de Yssiaco, monasterii Pratensis abbate, cryptarum loco erectum est. Jam si quaesierit Sollerius cur illius dedicationis commemoratio in Prat. codice B. deleta fuerit, in promptu est ratio. Destructis altaribus, quod accidit dum in usu erat iste codex, desita est annuntiari illorum dedicatio; hincque factum est ut illam recentiores codices, seu a quadringentis annis exarati, prorsus omitterent. BOUILLART. Parisius, monasterio beati Germani, dedicatio criptarum in honore ac veneratione Dei genitricis Mariae et beati Petri apostoli, sanctorumque martyrum Georgii, Aurelii atque Nathaliae. Ubi principalis ara nomini ejusdem virginis et beati Petri omniumque apostolorum consecrata est: ad caput vero sanctorum martyrum est altare in honore sancti Michaelis beatarumque omnium coelestium virtutum; et ad pedes ipsorum sancti Vincentii, Sebastiani, Christophori, Andochii, Tirsi et Felicis. Est ibi et altare in honore Macharii Dionysii, Rustici et Eleutherii omniumque sanctorum martyrum. Et aliud sancti Marcelli, Genovefae virginis, Savini, Audoeni, Ansberti omniumque confessorum et virginum.

On the Kalends · 1 June

  • 109

At Rome, the dedication of Saint Nicomedes, presbyter and martyr. At Caesarea of Palestine, under the persecution of Maximinus, of blessed Pamphilus, presbyter and martyr, whose Life Eusebius, bishop of Caesarea, set down in three books. And Saint Jerome also writes that he found certain volumes written out by his hand, which, he says, I embrace and keep with such joy that I believe I possess the riches of Cresus. At Augustodunum, of Saints Reverianus, bishop, and Paul, presbyter, with ten others, who were crowned with martyrdom under the emperor Aurelian. On this same day, of Saint Caprasius, abbot of the monastery Lirinensis. [Bouillart adds: This dedication of the crypts is so proper to the Church of Saint-Germain that it is no wonder it was passed over by those who copied out the Martyrology of Usuardus. Yet for Sollerius it is argument enough that that long passage is not to be ascribed to Usuardus, that almost all the other codices are against it, and above all the Pratensis B, in which Sirmond testified that it was erased. This last reason, I confess, ought to have given Sollerius some trouble. But he is at fault all the same when for that cause he denies that it was written by Usuardus, which the perfect likeness of the script persuades was written by him. Was the knot hard to untie? He should have waited a while and held himself back; he should have inquired by letter what the crypts are in Usuardus; why that dedication is announced once and again; why the Pratensis codex B passes it over in silence. These things belonged to an accurate editor. I untie the knot in a few words. Where there is now seen, built in the monastery of Saint-Germain, the most elegant chapel of the Blessed Virgin Mary, there were once crypts, or underground altars, the feast day of whose dedication was kept, in Usuardus's time, on the Kalends of June. Afterwards the same dedication was celebrated on the fourth day before the Ides, as is plain from the Pratensis codices; and at length it ceased to be celebrated, after the chapel of Blessed Mary was raised in the place of the crypts by Hugh of Issy, abbot of the monastery of the Prés. Now if Sollerius should ask why the commemoration of that dedication was deleted in the Pratensis codex B, the reason is ready to hand. When the altars were destroyed, which happened while that codex was in use, their dedication ceased to be announced; and hence it came about that the more recent codices, those written within these four hundred years, omitted it altogether. Bouillart. At Paris, in the monastery of blessed Germanus, the dedication of the crypts in honour and veneration of Mary the Mother of God and of blessed Peter the apostle, and of the holy martyrs Georgius, Aurelius and Nathalia. Where the principal altar is consecrated to the name of the same virgin and of blessed Peter and of all the apostles: and at the head of the holy martyrs there is an altar in honour of Saint Michael and of all the blessed heavenly powers; and at their feet, of Saints Vincentius, Sebastian, Christophorus, Andochius, Tirsus and Felix. There is there also an altar in honour of Macharius Dionysius, Rusticus and Eleutherius and of all the holy martyrs. And another of Saint Marcellus, of Genevieve the virgin, of Savinus, Audoenus, Ansbertus and of all the confessors and virgins.

IV Non. Die 2

  • 113

Romae, natalis beatorum martyrum Marcellini presbiteri et Petri exorcistae, qui multos in carcere ad fidem erudientes, post dura vincula et plurima tormenta decollati sunt sub judice Sereno. Lugduni, sanctorum Fotini, ejusdem urbis episcopi, Zachariae presbyteri, sancti diaconi, Epagati, Maturi, Attali, Albinae, Gratae, cum aliis quadraginta. Hi omnes famuli Christi, sicut in historia ecclesiastica scribitur, pariter coronati sunt. Passa est quoque et sancta Blandina ex eorum collegio, quae primo, secundo et tertio die pulsata cruciatibus, cum non superaretur, quarto verberibus acta, craticulis exusta, et multa alia perpessa, ad ultimum gladio jugulatur.

The fourth day before the Nones · 2 June

  • 113

At Rome, the birthday of the blessed martyrs Marcellinus, presbyter, and Peter, exorcist, who, instructing many in prison unto the faith, after harsh chains and very many torments were beheaded under the judge Serenus. At Lyon, of Saints Fotinus, bishop of the same city, Zacharias, presbyter, Sanctus, deacon, Epagatus, Maturus, Attalus, Albina, Grata, with forty others. All these servants of Christ, as is written in the ecclesiastical history, were crowned together. There suffered also Saint Blandina, of their company, who, beaten with torments on the first, second and third day, when she was not overcome, on the fourth was driven with blows, burned on gridirons, and, having suffered many other things, at the last is slain with the sword.

III Non. Die 3.

  • 117

In Campania, natalis sancti Herasmi episcopi et martyris, hic sub Diocletiano Augusto, primo plumbatis, deinde fustibus graviter maceratus, post resina, sulphure, plumbo, pice, cera oleoque perfusus, illaesus apparuit. Deinde sub Maximiano, diversis atque immanissimis suppliciis cruciatus, tandem, vocante Domino, sancto fine quievit. Apud Aretium civitatem Tusciae, sanctorum martyrum Pergentini et Laurentini fratrum, qui persecutione Decii cum essent pueri, post dura supplicia tolerata et magna miracula ostensa, gladio caesi sunt. Civitate Corduba, beati Isaac monachi, qui cum esset annorum viginti et septem, pro fide Christi gladio necatus est. In territorio Aurelianensium, sancti Lifardi presbiteri.

The third day before the Nones · 3 June

  • 117

In Campania, the birthday of Saint Herasmus, bishop and martyr; he, under Diocletian Augustus, first grievously bruised with leaded scourges, then with cudgels, afterwards drenched with resin, sulphur, lead, pitch, wax and oil, appeared unharmed. Then under Maximian, tormented with divers and most savage punishments, at length, the Lord calling him, he rested in a holy end. At Aretium, a city of Tuscia, of the holy martyrs Pergentinus and Laurentinus, brothers, who, in the persecution of Decius, when they were boys, after harsh punishments endured and great miracles shown, were cut down with the sword. In the city of Cordoba, of blessed Isaac, monk, who, when he was twenty-seven years old, was slain with the sword for the faith of Christ. In the territory of Orléans, of Saint Lifardus, presbyter.

Pridie Non. Die 4.

  • 119

Apud Illiricum civitate Siscia, natalis beati Quirini episcopi, qui pro fide Christi, ut Prudentius scribit, ligato ad manum molari saxo, in flumen praecipitatus, et cum circumstantibus diu collocutus, ne ejus terrentur exemplo, vix precibus ut mergeretur obtinuit. [ Addit Bouillart.: Nihil hic de Castellano queror. Imo in Pratensem codicem videtur hoc loco propensior fuisse quam par erat. Nam quantum ex Sollerii in hunc et sequentem diem observationibus et ex praefatione conjectare possum, non admonitus fuit eruditus ille scriptor, avulsum ex codice Pratensi folium, aliudque ejus loco assutum fuisse. Forte Castellani oculis fucum fecit membrana aeque exstrita, atramentum aeque decolor, forma litterarum haud multum assimilis, ut fere coaevam et eamdem nullo discrimine manum habuerit. Sed firmioribus causa nostra nititur subsidiis, quam ut quidvis dissimulare debeamus, praesertim cum hujusmodi naevus magis illi faveat quod adversetur. Hinc enim reliqui codicis vetustas, elegantia, dignitas magis enitescunt, ut umbrae tabellis addunt decus. Itaque his diebus 3, 4, 5 et 6, codicem arbitrio Sollerii permitto. Corrigat, mutet, addat, detrahat ; nihil ad me attinet BOUILLART. Civitate Niveduno sanctorum Zotici, Attali et Eutici.]

The day before the Nones · 4 June

  • 119

At Illiricum, in the city of Siscia, the birthday of blessed Quirinus, bishop, who, for the faith of Christ, as Prudentius writes, a millstone tied to his hand, was cast headlong into a river, and, having long spoken with those standing round, lest they be terrified by his example, scarcely obtained by prayers that he might be drowned. [Bouillart adds: Here I make no complaint of Castellanus. Nay, in this place he seems to have been more favourable to the Pratensis codex than was fair. For as far as I can conjecture from Sollerius's observations on this and the following day, and from his preface, that learned writer was not advised that a leaf had been torn out of the Pratensis codex and another sewn in its place. Perhaps Castellanus's eyes were deceived by a parchment equally worn, an ink equally faded, a form of letters not much alike, so that he took the hand for nearly coeval and the same, with no difference. But our cause rests on firmer supports than that we should need to hide anything, especially since a blemish of this kind favours it more than it tells against it. For by it the age, elegance and dignity of the rest of the codex shine out the more, as shadows add grace to pictures. So on these days, the 3rd, 4th, 5th and 6th, I leave the codex to Sollerius's judgement. Let him correct, change, add, take away; it is nothing to me. Bouillart. In the city of Nivedunum, of Saints Zoticus, Attalus and Euticus.]

Nonis. Die 5.

  • 123

Apud Aegyptum, natalis sanctorum martyrum Marciani, Nicandri et Apollonii, quorum gesta habentur. Eodem die, passio sancti Bonifacii martyris, sub Diocletiano et Maximiano apud Tarsum civitatem passi, sed Romae in via quae Latina nuncupatur, sepulti. Hic cum diu in stupro misceretur, tandem divino nutu compunctus, non solum, cui subjacebat, vitium deseruit, verum quoque per diversa tormenta ad palmam martyrii ardenter cucurrit. Item sancti Bonifacii episcopi, qui de Britanniis veniens, et fidem Christi gentibus evangelizans, cum maximam multitudinem in Frisia Christianae religioni subjugasset, novissime gladio peremptus, martyrium consummavit cum Eobano episcopo et aliis servis Dei.

On the Nones · 5 June

  • 123

At Egypt, the birthday of the holy martyrs Marcianus, Nicander and Apollonius, whose acts are extant. On the same day, the passion of Saint Boniface, martyr, who suffered under Diocletian and Maximian at the city of Tarsus, but was buried at Rome on the road which is called the Latin. He, when he had long been mingled in fornication, at length pricked at heart by the divine will, not only deserted the vice to which he was subject, but also ran ardently through divers torments to the palm of martyrdom. Also of Saint Boniface, bishop, who, coming from the Britains and evangelizing the faith of Christ to the nations, when he had subjected a very great multitude in Frisia to the Christian religion, last of all slain with the sword, consummated his martyrdom with Eobanus the bishop and other servants of God.

VIII Idus. Die 6.

  • 127

Natalis beati Philippi, qui fuit unus e septem diaconibus. Hic signis et prodigiis inclitus, apud Caesaream requievit, juxta quem tres virgines filiae ipsius prophetissae tumulatae jacent. Apud Tarsum Ciliciae, martyrum viginti, temporibus Diocletiani et Maximiani, qui per diversa supplicia glorificaverunt Dominum in corporibus suis. Romae, sanctorum Artemii cum uxore sua Candida et filia Paulina. Qui Artemius gladio percussus; uxor vero ejus et filia lapidibus sunt obrutae. Civitate Niveduno, sanctorum Amantii et Alexandri.

The eighth day before the Ides · 6 June

  • 127

The birthday of blessed Philip, who was one of the seven deacons. He, renowned for signs and wonders, rested at Caesarea, and beside him lie buried his three virgin daughters, prophetesses. At Tarsus of Cilicia, of twenty martyrs, in the times of Diocletian and Maximian, who through divers punishments glorified the Lord in their bodies. At Rome, of Saints Artemius with his wife Candida and his daughter Paulina. Which Artemius was struck with the sword; but his wife and daughter were overwhelmed with stones. In the city of Nivedunum, of Saints Amantius and Alexander.

VII Idus Die 7.

  • 129

Constantinopoli, natalis beati Pauli, ejusdem civitatis episcopi, qui a Constantio imperatore apud civitatulam quamdam Cappadociae, Cucusam nomine, ob catholicam fidem pulsus exsilio, Arrianorum insidiis crudeliter strangulatus, ad coelestia regna migravit. Item Corduba, sanctorum Petri presbyteri, Aventii, Hieremiae et aliorum trium.

The seventh day before the Ides · 7 June

  • 129

At Constantinople, the birthday of blessed Paul, bishop of the same city, who, driven into exile by the emperor Constantius for the catholic faith, to a certain little city of Cappadocia, Cucusa by name, cruelly strangled by the plots of the Arians, passed to the heavenly kingdoms. Also at Cordoba, of Saints Peter, presbyter, Aventius, Hieremias and three others.

VI Idus. Die 8.

  • 131

In Galliis Suessionis civitate, natalis beati Medardi episcopi Noviomensis, qui quanti fuerit meriti ostensum est, cum in ejus obitu coeli aperti sunt, atque ante eum divina micuerunt luminaria. Eodem die, sancti Gildardi Rothomagensis episcopi. Item Senonis, sancti Eraclii episcopi et confessoris. In Sardinia, sancti Salustiani. Cordubae, sancti Abundii presbyteri et martyris.

The sixth day before the Ides · 8 June

  • 131

In the Gauls, in the city of Suessionis, the birthday of blessed Medard, bishop Noviomensis, of what great merit he was, was shown when at his death the heavens were opened, and divine lights flashed before him. On the same day, of Saint Gildardus, bishop of Rouen. Also at Senones, of Saint Eraclius, bishop and confessor. In Sardinia, of Saint Salustianus. At Cordoba, of Saint Abundius, presbyter and martyr.

V Idus. Die 9.

  • 135

Romae in monte Caelio, natalis sanctorum Primi et Feliciani sub Diocletiano et Maximiano imperatoribus. Hi gloriosissimi martyres longaevam in Domino vitam ducentes, et nunc simul paria, nunc sigillatim diversa et exquisita ferentes tormenta, tandem felicis pugnae cursum gladio animadversi consummaverunt. In Galliis civitate Agenno, passio beati Vincentii levitae et martyris. Item in Scotia, sancti Columbae presbiteri et confessoris, magnae et mirandae virtutis viri. Nicea, sancti Diomedis. Add. Bouill.: [Apud monasterium S. Germani, dedicatio Haec dedicationis mentio minime Usuardina visa est Sollerio, de ea silentibus quamplurimis codicibus, testante Sirmondo expunctam fuisse ex Pratensi B, fatente denique Castellano recentiore manu sibi adjunctam videri. Sed in re quae soli sancti Germani monasterio convenit, quid probat aliorum codicum silentium? Neque quidquam ponderis habet Sirmondi animadversio. Postquam a Nortmannis B. Petri oratorium cum sanctorum Vincentii et Germani basilica destructum fuit et incensum, simul a dedicationis solemnitate cessatum est, atque adeo a facienda illius mentione in codice qui in capitulo legebatur. Quid tandem de Castellani monito dicam? Taedet eamdem membranam, idem atramentum, manum eamdem semper occinere: commentum oculis refellendum relinquo. BOUILLART. oratorii in honore B. Petri apostoli.]

The fifth day before the Ides · 9 June

  • 135

At Rome, on the Caelian hill, the birthday of Saints Primus and Felicianus, under the emperors Diocletian and Maximian. These most glorious martyrs, leading a long life in the Lord, and bearing now the same torments together, now different and exquisite ones severally, at length, put to the sword, consummated the course of their happy fight. In the Gauls, in the city of Agennum, the passion of blessed Vincent, levite and martyr. Also in Scotia, of Saint Columba, presbyter and confessor, a man of great and wonderful virtue. At Nicea, of Saint Diomedes. Bouillart adds: [At the monastery of Saint Germanus, the dedication This mention of the dedication seemed to Sollerius by no means Usuardus's, since very many codices are silent about it, since Sirmond testifies that it was struck out of the Pratensis B, and lastly since Castellanus avows that it seemed to him added in a more recent hand. But in a matter which belongs to the monastery of Saint Germanus alone, what does the silence of other codices prove? Nor has Sirmond's observation any weight. After the oratory of blessed Peter was destroyed and burned by the Northmen along with the basilica of Saints Vincent and Germanus, the solemnity of the dedication ceased at the same time, and so too the making of any mention of it in the codex that was read in chapter. What, lastly, shall I say of Castellanus's advice? It wearies me to be forever harping on the same parchment, the same ink, the same hand: I leave the fiction to be refuted by the eyes. Bouillart. of an oratory in honour of blessed Peter the apostle.]

IV Idus. Die 10

  • 139

Romae, via Aurelia, natalis sanctorum Basilidis, Tripodis, et Madalis sub Aureliano imperatore, praeside Platone, et aliorum viginti martyrum. Eodem die, via Salaria, passio beati Getuli, clarissimi et doctissimi viri sociorumque ejus, Cerealis, Amantii et Primitivi, qui jussu Adriani imperatoris primo caesi, deinde in carcerem reclusi, postremo incendio sunt traditi. Apud Nicomediam, sancti Zachariae. Autisiodoro, sancti Censurii episcopi et confessoris.

[ Addit Bouill. Sollerius hic insigne praebet aequitatis exemplum. Jam observavi nec semel suorum codicum silentium sibi esse pro norma qua Pratensem dijudicat: proinde sanctos huic additos esse recentiore manu, quos in suis minime reperit. Ut vero sibi constet, ad suas partes trahit illa etiam addimenta, quae tribus minimum saeculis Usuardo posteriora esse nemo unquam inficiatus est. Quid injustius? Monitus dubio procul a Castellano fuerat, Landericum recentiore manu fuisse adjectum. An ea causa potuit aut debuit Pratensem inter puros codices non recensere? Credo equidem, qua est severitate, non mitius acturum fuisse cum Pratensi, si quis e nostris SS. Franciscum Assissiatem, Ignatium Loyolitam, Theresiam, aut alios nostrae memoriae sanctos, vacuis spatiis impressisset. BOUILL. Parisius, sancti Landerici episcopi et confessoris. Eodem die apud monasterium S. Germani dedicatio cryptarum in honore beatae Dei genitricis Mariae et beati Michaelis archangeli omniumque coelestium virtutum, et sanctorum apostolorum Petri et Pauli, omniumque apostolorum et evangelistarum, necnon omnium sanctarum virginum. Est ibi et altare Macharii, Dionysii sociorumque ejus et S. Urbani papae et martyris et omnium martyrum. Et aliud SS. Georgii, Aurelii et Praejecti martyrum et SS. confessorum Martini, Nicolai, Marcelli, Ansberti, et sanctarum Caeciliae, Genovefae, Nathaliae et omnium Christi confessorum.]

The fourth day before the Ides · 10 June

  • 139

At Rome, on the Aurelian Way, the birthday of Saints Basilides, Tripos, and Madalis, under the emperor Aurelian, the governor being Plato, and of twenty other martyrs. On the same day, on the Salarian Way, the passion of blessed Getulus, a most illustrious and most learned man, and of his companions Cerealis, Amantius and Primitivus, who by order of the emperor Hadrian were first beaten, then shut up in prison, lastly given over to burning. At Nicomedia, of Saint Zacharias. At Auxerre, of Saint Censurius, bishop and confessor.

[Bouillart adds: Here Sollerius gives a signal example of fairness. I have already observed, and not once only, that the silence of his own codices is for him the rule by which he judges the Pratensis: and that accordingly the saints whom he does not find in his own were added to it in a more recent hand. But to be consistent with himself, he draws over to his side even those additions which no one has ever denied to be at least three centuries later than Usuardus. What could be more unjust? He had been advised, beyond doubt, by Castellanus that Landericus was added in a more recent hand. Could he, or should he, on that account refuse to count the Pratensis among the pure codices? For my part I believe, such is his severity, that he would have dealt no more mildly with the Pratensis if one of ours had set Saints Francis of Assisi, Ignatius Loyola, Teresa, or other saints within our memory into its empty spaces. Bouillart. At Paris, of Saint Landericus, bishop and confessor. On the same day, at the monastery of Saint Germanus, the dedication of the crypts in honour of blessed Mary the Mother of God and of blessed Michael the archangel and of all the heavenly powers, and of the holy apostles Peter and Paul, and of all the apostles and evangelists, and also of all holy virgins. There is there also an altar of Macharius, of Dionysius and his companions, and of Saint Urbanus, pope and martyr, and of all the martyrs. And another of Saints Georgius, Aurelius and Praejectus, martyrs, and of the holy confessors Martin, Nicolaus, Marcellus, Ansbertus, and of Saints Caecilia, Genevieve, Nathalia and all the confessors of Christ.]

III Idus. Die 11.

  • 143

Natalis sancti Barnabae apostoli, qui cum esset Cyprius, cum Paulo gentium apostolus ordinatus est. Hujus corpus tempore Zenonis imperatoris, ipso revelante, repertum est. In Aquileia, passio sanctorum Felicis et Fortunati, sub persecutione Diocletiani et Maximiani, qui cum essent duo germani fratres, equuleo sunt suspensi, atque ardentes lampades circa latera eorum appositae, quibus mox Dei virtute exstinctis, per ventrem ferventi oleo sunt perfusi, atque ad ultimum in confessione Christi capite truncati.

The third day before the Ides · 11 June

  • 143

The birthday of Saint Barnabas the apostle, who, being a Cypriot, was ordained apostle of the gentiles with Paul. His body was found in the time of the emperor Zeno, he himself revealing it. At Aquileia, the passion of Saints Felix and Fortunatus, under the persecution of Diocletian and Maximian, who, being two brothers german, were hung upon the rack, and burning torches were set about their sides; which being presently extinguished by the power of God, they were drenched through the belly with boiling oil, and at the last were cut off by the head in the confession of Christ.

Pridie Idus. Die 12.

  • 145

Mediolani, natalis beati Nazarii martyris, quem Anolinus sub rabie persecutionis, quae per Neronem excitata est, diu maceratum et afflictum in carcere, cum quo sanctissimum puerum Celsum, quem idem ipse nutrierat, gladio feriri jussit. Horum corpora, ut scribit sanctus Paulinus, cum usque ad tempora Honorii et Archadii imperatorum latuissent, beatus Ambrosius, Domino revelante, reperit. Item apud praefatam urbem, sanctorum martyrum Basilidis, Cirini, et Naboris.

The day before the Ides · 12 June

  • 145

At Milan, the birthday of blessed Nazarius, martyr, whom Anolinus, under the fury of the persecution which was stirred up by Nero, long bruised and afflicted in prison, with whom the most holy boy Celsus, whom the same man had brought up, he ordered to be struck with the sword. Their bodies, as Saint Paulinus writes, when they had lain hidden until the times of the emperors Honorius and Arcadius, blessed Ambrose, the Lord revealing them, found. Also at the aforesaid city, of the holy martyrs Basilides, Cirinus, and Nabor.

Idibus. Die 13.

  • 149

Romae via Ardeatina, natalis sanctae Feliculae virginis et martyris, quae nolens nubere, nec idolis immolare, tradita est cuidam vicario, qui fecit eam perseverantem in confessione Christi, post tenebrosam custodiam et famis inopiam, levari in equuleo et sic demum deponi et praecipitari in cloacam. Eodem die, sancti Fandilae presbyteri, qui apud Cordubam civitatem, capite amputato, martyrium sumpsit.

On the Ides · 13 June

  • 149

At Rome, on the Ardeatine Way, the birthday of Saint Felicula, virgin and martyr, who, unwilling to marry, nor to sacrifice to idols, was handed over to a certain vicar, who caused her, persevering in the confession of Christ, after a dark custody and the want of hunger, to be raised on the rack, and so at last to be taken down and cast headlong into a sewer. On the same day, of Saint Fandila, presbyter, who at the city of Cordoba, his head being cut off, took up martyrdom.

XVIII Kal. Die 14.

  • 151

Helisei prophetae, qui apud Samariam Palestinae (ut beatus scribit Hieronymus) situs est, ubi et Abdias propheta quiescit. Apud Caesaream Cappadociae, natalis sancti Basilii episcopi, qui tempore Valentis doctrina et sapientia insignis, omnibusque virtutibus fundatus, mirabiliter effulsit. In territorio Suessionis civitatis, sanctorum martyrum Rufini et Valerii, qui a praeside Rictiovario post illata sibi tormenta jussi sunt decollari. Ipso die, sancti Quintiani presbyteri et confessoris.

The eighteenth day before the Kalends · 14 June

  • 151

Of Heliseus the prophet, who is laid at Samaria of Palestine (as blessed Jerome writes), where also Abdias the prophet rests. At Caesarea of Cappadocia, the birthday of Saint Basil, bishop, who, in the time of Valens, distinguished in doctrine and wisdom, and grounded in all virtues, shone wonderfully. In the territory of the city of Suessionis, of the holy martyrs Rufinus and Valerius, who, after torments inflicted on them, were ordered by the governor Rictiovarus to be beheaded. On this same day, of Saint Quintianus, presbyter and confessor.

XVII Kal. Die 15.

  • 155

In Sicilia, natalis sanctorum Viti, Modesti, et Crescentiae, qui sub Diocletiano imperatore post ollam plumbi ferventis, post bestias et catastas divina virtute superatas, cursum gloriosi certaminis peregerunt [ Interserit Bouillart: De sancti Mercurii mentione, quam ex codicum suorum silentio et litura Pratensis inductam esse autumat Sollerius; ab ejus judicio ad sanos oculos appello. Si vel levissimum scripturae discrimen suspicentur, causam non dico, quin super inductam esse judicent. Additam fuisse verum est, sed ab Usuardo; unde sumptam, non liquet. Illius martyris Acta penes nos olim fuerunt, siquidem in ms. nostrorum codicum indice memorantur n. 461. Sed illa reperire non potui. Forte non restituit is cui mutuo data fuerant, forte etiam sublata furta sunt. De Alexio, margini addito, non est quod Pratensi codici moveat litem, nam nullum est Martyrologium, quod saltem in usu fuerit, quin hujusmodi additamentis infarciatur. Et quid mirum? An oportuisset integrum volumen describi quoties sanctus aliquis in Fastis addebatur? BOUILLART. Civitate Benevento, sancti Mercurii martyris.] Apud Mesiam civitate Dorostoro, sancti Esichii militaris, qui cum beato Julio comprehensus, sub praeside Maximo postea martyrio coronatus est.

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 15 June

  • 155

In Sicily, the birthday of Saints Vitus, Modestus, and Crescentia, who under the emperor Diocletian, after a pot of boiling lead, after beasts and scaffolds overcome by divine power, finished the course of their glorious contest [Bouillart inserts: Of the mention of Saint Mercurius, which Sollerius asserts was brought in, from the silence of his own codices and an erasure in the Pratensis: from his judgement I appeal to sound eyes. If they suspect even the slightest difference in the script, I plead no case against their judging it written in over the erasure. That it was added is true, but by Usuardus; whence it was taken is not clear. The Acts of that martyr were once in our keeping, since they are recorded in the manuscript catalogue of our codices, n. 461. But I have not been able to find them. Perhaps he to whom they had been lent did not return them; perhaps too they were carried off by theft. As for Alexius, added in the margin, there is no reason why he should pick a quarrel with the Pratensis codex, for there is no Martyrology, at least of those that have been in use, which is not stuffed with additions of this kind. And what wonder? Ought a whole volume to have been copied out every time some saint was added to the Fasti? Bouillart. In the city of Beneventum, of Saint Mercurius, martyr.] At Mesia, in the city of Dorostorum, of Saint Esichius, soldier, who, seized with blessed Julius, was afterwards crowned with martyrdom under the governor Maximus.

XVI Kal. Die 16.

  • 159

In Galliis apud urbem Vesuntionem, sanctorum martyrum Ferreoli presbiteri et Ferrutionis diaconi, qui a beato Hireneo episcopo ad praedicandum verbum Dei missi, postea sub Claudio judice excruciati poenis, gladio feriuntur. Antiochiae, natalis sanctorum martyrum Cyrici et Julittae matris ejus, qui post dira verbera et gravia tormenta, martyrii sui cursum obtruncatione capitis impleverunt. The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 16 June

  • 159

In the Gauls, at the city of Vesuntio, of the holy martyrs Ferreolus, presbyter, and Ferrutio, deacon, who, sent by blessed Hireneus the bishop to preach the word of God, afterwards, under the judge Claudius, tormented with punishments, are struck with the sword. At Antioch, the birthday of the holy martyrs Cyricus and Julitta his mother, who, after cruel blows and grievous torments, fulfilled the course of their martyrdom by the cutting off of the head. At Lyon, the deposition of blessed Aurelianus, bishop of Arles. In the city of Namnetis, of Saint Similianus, bishop and confessor.

XV Kal. Die 17.

  • 161

Romae, sanctorum martyrum ducentorum sexaginta duorum, qui positi sunt via Salaria veteri ad clivum Cucumeris. Eodem die, sancti Vulmari confessoris, admirandae sanctitatis et religionis viri. [ Apud Bouillart, 163pro, Eod. die, . . . . . viri: Pro S. Gundulfo Carolinum autographum habet: Eodem die, S. Vulmari confessoris admirandae sanctitatis et religionis viri. Quam commemorationem ex Adone sumptam delevit postea Usuardus, et S. Gundulfum substituit. Utrumque erudite. Nam non hoc die, sed XX Julii S. Vulmarum vita excessisse suo loco dicam; et S. Gundulfi mentio opportunius, quam hoc die, fieri non potuit. Locus est, quem assignavit Henschenius ipse, qui Breviarium Bituricense, tabulas ejusdem ecclesiae, et purissimos cum exaratos manu, tum editos Usuardi codices allegat. Quo tempore, cujus sedis episcopus fuerit sanctus ille Gundulfus, hactenus ignoratum est. Qui translationis et miraculorum historiam scripsit, Gundulfum ait Mediolensem archiepiscopum fuisse. Mettensem episcopum dicit in vita S. Radegundis Venantius Fortunatus. Sed neque Mediolanenses, neque Mettenses suis illum praesulibus annumerant. Neque id exploratum fuit Usuardo, qui sedem appellare non omisisset, si nota ipsi fuisset. BOUILLART. In territorio Bituricensi, sancti Gundulfi episcopi et confessoris.] Aurelianis, sancti Aviti presbyteri et confessoris.

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 17 June

  • 161

At Rome, of the two hundred and sixty-two holy martyrs, who were laid on the old Salarian Way at the Clivus Cucumeris. On the same day, of Saint Vulmarus, confessor, a man of admirable holiness and religion. [In Bouillart, 163instead of Eod. die, . . . . . viri: In place of Saint Gundulfus the Caroline autograph has: 'On the same day, of Saint Vulmarus, confessor, a man of admirable holiness and religion.' This commemoration, taken from Ado, Usuardus afterwards deleted, and substituted Saint Gundulfus. Both learnedly. For I shall say in its place that Saint Vulmarus departed this life not on this day but on 20 July; and the mention of Saint Gundulfus could not have been made more fittingly than on this day. It is the place which Henschenius himself assigned, who cites the Breviary of Bourges, the tables of the same church, and the purest codices of Usuardus, both those written by hand and those printed. At what time, and of what see, that Saint Gundulfus was bishop has not so far been known. He who wrote the history of the translation and the miracles says that Gundulfus was archbishop of Milan. Venantius Fortunatus, in the Life of Saint Radegundis, calls him bishop of Metz. But neither the Milanese nor the people of Metz number him among their prelates. Nor was it ascertained by Usuardus, who would not have omitted to name the see if it had been known to him. Bouillart. In the territory of Bourges, of Saint Gundulfus, bishop and confessor.] At Orléans, of Saint Avitus, presbyter and confessor.
