XIII Kal. Die 19.

  • 169

Mediolani, natalis sanctorum martyrum Gervasii et Protasii fratrum, ex quibus priorem tam diu jussit Astasius comes plumbatis caedi, quousque exhalaret spiritum: sequentem vero fustibus caesum, capite truncari. Horum sepulcra sanctus Ambrosius Domino revelante reperit, et ita incorrupta eorum corpora, ac si eodem die fuissent interempti. Apud Ravennam, sancti Ursicini martyris, qui sub judice Paulino, post nimia tormenta, in confessione Domini immobilis permanens, capitis abscisione martyrium complevit.

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 19 June

  • 169

At Milan, the birthday of the holy martyrs Gervasius and Protasius, brothers, of whom Count Astasius ordered the former to be beaten with leaded scourges so long, until he breathed out his spirit: but the following one, beaten with cudgels, to be cut off by the head. Their sepulchres Saint Ambrose found, the Lord revealing them, and their bodies so incorrupt, as if they had been slain on that same day. At Ravenna, of Saint Ursicinus, martyr, who under the judge Paulinus, after excessive torments, remaining immovable in the confession of the Lord, completed his martyrdom by the cutting off of his head.

XII Kal. Die 20.

  • 173

Romae, depositio sancti Novati fratris Timothei presbyteri, qui ab apostolis in fide eruditi sunt. Horum sorores fuerunt beata Potentiana et Praxedes sacrae virgines Christi. Civitate Thomis sanctorum Pauli et Ciriaci.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 20 June

  • 173

At Rome, the deposition of Saint Novatus, brother of Timotheus the presbyter, who were instructed in the faith by the apostles. Their sisters were blessed Potentiana and Praxedes, holy virgins of Christ. In the city of Thomis, of Saints Paul and Ciriacus.

XI Kal. Die 21.

  • 177

Apud Siciliam civitate Syracusa, natalis sanctorum martyrum Rufini et Martiae. Civitate Caesarea Palestinae, sancti Eusebii episcopi et confessoris viri excellentissimi ingenii et historiographi. [ Bouillart.: Alii alio loco, aliis verbis Eusebium Caesariensem codices annuntiant. Cum igitur nihil hic de Pratensi queratur Sollerius, qua causa illum posthabuit aliis multo recentioribus, qui sic Eusebii commemorationem efferunt: Civitate Caesarea Palestinae, sancti Eusebii episcopi et confessoris, viri excellentissimi ingenii et historiographi? Si Sollerium audias tamen, quo breviora sunt et simpliciora elogia, eo magis Usuardina sunt. At docta haec et erudita Eusebiani elogii brevitas in Pratensi codice servata, falso in aliis confessoris titulo aucta fuerat: et certum erat Pratensem negligere, quoties ab aliis discreparet. Quod si sui codices ut Pratensis, Pratensis autem ut sui codices Eusebium annuntiarent, vah quam triumphasset Sollerius! quantis laudibus Usuardinam simplicitatem commendasset! In Cesarea Palestinae sancti Eusebii istoriographi.] Romae, sanctae Demetriae virginis. Haec cum ante impium Julianum pro fide Christi introduceretur, in confessione spiritum emisit. In pago Madriacensi, sancti Leutfredi confessoris, qui in monumentum eximiae sanctitatis, orationibus suis fontem produxisse fertur de terra. In Africa, sanctorum Quiriaci et Apollinaris.

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 21 June

  • 177

At Sicily, in the city of Syracusa, the birthday of the holy martyrs Rufinus and Martia. In the city of Caesarea of Palestine, of Saint Eusebius, bishop and confessor, a man of most excellent genius and a historiographer. [Bouillart: Some codices announce Eusebius of Caesarea in one place, others in another, and in different words. Since, then, Sollerius makes no complaint here about the Pratensis, for what cause did he rank it below others much more recent, which set out the commemoration of Eusebius thus: 'In the city of Caesarea of Palestine, of Saint Eusebius, bishop and confessor, a man of most excellent genius and a historiographer'? Yet if you listen to Sollerius, the shorter and simpler the eulogies are, the more they are Usuardus's. But this learned and erudite brevity of the eulogy of Eusebius, preserved in the Pratensis codex, had been falsely enlarged in the others with the title of confessor: and it was a settled thing to disregard the Pratensis whenever it differed from the others. But if his codices announced Eusebius as the Pratensis does, and the Pratensis as his codices do, ah, how Sollerius would have triumphed! with what praises he would have commended the simplicity of Usuardus! In Cesarea of Palestine, of Saint Eusebius the historiographer.] At Rome, of Saint Demetria, virgin. She, when she was being brought in before the impious Julian for the faith of Christ, gave up her spirit in the confession. In the district Madriacensis, of Saint Leutfredus, confessor, who, as a monument of his eminent holiness, is said to have brought forth a spring from the earth by his prayers. In Africa, of Saints Quiriacus and Apollinaris.

X Kal. Die 22.

  • 181

In Britannia, sancti Albani martyris, qui tempore Diocletiani in Verolamio civitate, post verbera et tormenta acerba, capite plexus est. Passus est cum illo etiam unus de militibus, eo quod eum ferire jussus, noluerit. Apud Nolam Campaniae, natalis beati Paulini episcopi et confessoris, cujus meminit sanctus Gregorius in libro Dialogorum. Claruit autem non solum eruditione et copiosa vitae sanctitate, sed etiam potentia adversus daemones. Ipso die, depositio sancti Niceae, Romatianae civitatis episcopi.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 22 June

  • 181

In Britain, of Saint Albanus, martyr, who in the time of Diocletian, in the city of Verolamium, after blows and bitter torments, was beheaded. There suffered with him also one of the soldiers, because, ordered to strike him, he was unwilling. At Nola of Campania, the birthday of blessed Paulinus, bishop and confessor, of whom Saint Gregory makes mention in the book of Dialogues. He was renowned not only for learning and abundant holiness of life, but also for power against the demons. On this same day, the deposition of Saint Niceae, bishop of the city Romatiana.

IX Kal. Die 23.

  • 183

Vigilia sancti Joannis Baptistae. Eodem die, sancti Joannis presbyteri, quem impius Julianus, inauditum, via Salaria veteri, ante simulacrum solis decollari praecepit. In Tuscia civitate Utrina, sancti Felicis presbyteri, cujus os tandiu jussit Turtius praefectus lapide contundi, donec emitteret Spiritum. In Britannia, natalis sanctae Ediltrudis virginis, cujus corpus cum undecim annis esset sepultum, incorruptum inventum est.

The ninth day before the Kalends · 23 June

  • 183

The Vigil of Saint John the Baptist. On the same day, of Saint John, presbyter, whom the impious Julian ordered to be beheaded, unheard, on the old Salarian Way, before the image of the sun. In Tuscia, in the city of Utrina, of Saint Felix, presbyter, whose mouth Turtius the prefect ordered to be pounded with a stone so long, until he gave up the Spirit. In Britain, the birthday of Saint Ediltrudis, virgin, whose body, when it had been buried eleven years, was found incorrupt.

VIII Kal. Die 24.

  • 187

Nativitas beati Joannis Baptistae et praecursoris Domini. In territorio Parisiacensi, vico Christoilo, passio sanctorum Agoadi et Gliberti, cum aliis innumeris promiscui sexus. Augustoduno, depositio Simplicii episcopi.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 24 June

  • 187

The Nativity of blessed John the Baptist and forerunner of the Lord. In the territory of Paris, at the village of Christoilum, the passion of Saints Agoadus and Glibertus, with innumerable others of both sexes. At Augustodunum, the deposition of Simplicius, bishop.

VII Kal. Die 25.

  • 189

Apud Pirriberoeam, sancti Sosipatris, discipuli beati Pauli apostoli. In Alexandria, sancti Gallicani martyris: qui cum triumphalibus infulis sublimatus, acceptus esset Augusto Constantino et carus, per ipsius filiam nomine Constantiam, ac Christi martyres Joannem et Paulum, ad fidem conversus est. Hujus sacrae opinionis fama postmodum in toto orbe in tantum excrevit, ut ab Oriente et Occidente venientes, viderent virum ex patricio et consule lavantem pedes pauperum, ponentem mensam, aquam manibus effundentem, languentibus sollicite ministrantem, et caetera sanctae servitutis officia exhibentem. Ipse vero postea cum sacrificare cogeretur sub Juliano, et contemneret, percussus gladio in 191corpore, Christi martyrem fecit. Romae, sanctae Luciae virginis cum aliis viginti duobus. [ Addit Bouillart.: Verum est. Huic articulo subdit Pratensis sancti Eligii translationem. Sed quid hoc in re peccavit? Facta est haec translatio medio septimo saeculo et in Gallicanis ecclesiis praecipue celebrata: ut extra culpam non esset iste codex si eam omisisset; et non omisisse vitio dat Sollerius: quanquam Pratensi consentiunt septem ex codicibus quos vidit, et undecim quos ipse. BOUILLART. Item translatio sancti Eligii, episcopi et confessoris.]

The seventh day before the Kalends · 25 June

  • 189

At Pirriberoea, of Saint Sosipater, disciple of blessed Paul the apostle. At Alexandria, of Saint Gallicanus, martyr: who, when, raised up with triumphal insignia, he was accepted by Constantine Augustus and dear to him, was converted to the faith through his daughter, Constantia by name, and through the martyrs of Christ John and Paul. The fame of his holy repute afterwards grew so much in the whole world, that people coming from the East and the West saw a man, from patrician and consul, washing the feet of the poor, setting the table, pouring water on hands, ministering carefully to the sick, and performing the other offices of holy service. But he himself afterwards, when he was being compelled to sacrifice under Julian, and scorned it, struck with the sword in the 191body, made a martyr of Christ. At Rome, of Saint Lucia, virgin, with twenty-two others. [Bouillart adds: It is true. To this article the Pratensis subjoins the translation of Saint Eligius. But in what did it sin in this? This translation took place in the middle of the seventh century and was celebrated especially in the churches of Gaul: so that that codex would not have been free of blame had it omitted it; and Sollerius imputes it as a fault that it did not omit it: although seven of the codices which he saw agree with the Pratensis, and eleven of those which he himself (saw). Bouillart. Also the translation of Saint Eligius, bishop and confessor.]

VI Kal. Die 26.

  • 193

Romae, natalis sanctorum Joannis et Pauli fratrum, quorum primus praepositus, secundus primicerius Constantiae virginis filiae Constantini fuit, qui postea sub Juliano martyrii palmam gladio caedente perceperunt. In pago Pictavensi, sancti Maxentii presbyteri et confessoris, gloriosi in miraculis. Eodem die, sanctae Perseverandae virginis. [ Apud Bouillart: Romae, natalis sanctorum Joannis et Pauli fratrum, quorum primus praepositus, secundus primicerius Constanciae virginis filiae Constantini fuit, qui postea sub Juliano 195martyrii palmam gladio caedente perceperunt. In Solleriana editione Vigilius hoc loco motus est et ad XXXI diem Januarii retroactus, ubi Sollerius erasum fuisse disputat, et nos confitemur. Sed hoc loco non ab re fuerit lectorem admonere quo alio nomine suspecta Sollerio sit Vigilii commemoratio. Nimirum illi minus arridet longa illa oratio, quae a nativa Usuardi simplicitate omnino abhorret, quamque multo magis refert ista, qua XXXI Januarii usus fuerat: Apud Trientinam urbem, sancti Vigilii episcopi et martyris. Imo brevitas illa et simplicitas ita Sollerium delectat et capit, ut illam pro norma esse velit: Valeat hic, inquit, receptissima nostra regula, videlicet, primigeniam puritatem eo magis elucescere, quo textus brevior fuerit et simplicior. At, Solleri, cur die XXI hujus mensis Eusebii elogium tam breve, tam simplex repudiasti, praetulisti compositum et falsis titulis inflatum? Cur die XXV Pratense sancti Gallicani elogium, quamvis articulum fere totum impleat, Usuardinum esse judicasti? multo brevius et simplicius tuus Tornacensis codex repraesentabat. Cur die XVIII Maii Marci et Marcelliani elogium exscripsisti totum? Quam breve, quam simplex tibi offerebat Pulsanensis codex: Romae, natalis SS. martyrum Marci et Marcelliani. Idem die XV simpliciter nominat Vitum, Modestum et Crescentiam; cur totos tres versus maluisti? Tornacensis codex media fere parte truncat beati Nazarii martyris elogium die XIII. Cur Pratensem aliosque illi praehabendos existimasti? Quid multis moror? Nullam fere toto tuo Martyrologio diem vidi, qua aliud alio brevius et simplicius elogium non afferatur, et qua simul a tua regula non recesseris. Jam tu a me non postulas, ut rationibus approbem Vigilium ab Usuardo ex die XXXI Januarii erasum et in hanc diem justa causa fuisse reservatum. Rem tui fatentur: Diem martyrii, inquiunt, acta nulla exprimunt: sed ecclesia Tridentina festum S. Vigilii celebrat VI Kal. Julii, verosimiliter ut natalem. Talem certe videtur judicasse Florus Lugdunensis, quando suis ad Bedae genuinum Martyrologium additamentis, S. Vigilii elogium addidit. Neque solum ex hoc articulo Vigilium expunxit Pratensis codicis reprehensor, sed et Salvium Engolismensem, cujus loco Perseverandam substituendam esse censuit. Quibus tandem argumentis? Silentio suorum codicum de sancto Salvio, et eorumdem de Perseveranda consensione. Jam dixi non semel utrumque istud argumentum nullius esse roboris: mirandum non esse quod Solleriani codices de cujuspiam sancti mentione consentiant qui deest in Pratensi, aut in illis desint sancti, quos Pratensis exhibet; id inde accidisse, quod omnes fere codices e primo Usuardini Martyrologii exemplo transumpti fuerint, et ab Usuardo deletae commemorationes fuerint, quae sibi minime probabantur; aut additae, quas potiores ducebat. Itaque hinc erasit Perseverandam, quia lectis actis advertit non VI sed VII Kal. hujus mensis obiisse diem. Sic enim Acta: Celebratur depositio ipsius virginis VII Kalendas Julii, etc. Salvium autem subrogavit, quia hunc esse mortis ejus diem Acta testantur: Martyrizati sunt pariter S. Salvius et discipulus ejus cum eo sub Vuinegardo VI Kal. Julii. Sin inficiatur Sollerius ab Usuardo lecta fuisse S. Salvii Acta, quidni Florum et Wandalbertum imitatus fuisset qui ambo hoc die de illo praesule mentionem fecerunt? Hic admoneo Sollerium male oculatum fuisse hoc die ejus inspectorem, qui lituram in Pratensi codice sibi videre visus est. Sana tota est haec pagina, intacta tota a scalpelli acumine. Sed unum est ex paucis illis foliis, quae substituta sunt loco eorum quae Usuardus ipse avulsit: aut quia prae liturarum numero legi non poterant, aut quia scalpelli acies membranam ipsam laceraverat. Cur autem Castellanus scripserit deletum fuisse Salvium, causam hanc fuisse suspicor. In altero post Salvium folio, paulo superius tamen, medius versus scissus est aut sectus, ita ut supera litterarum pars ab infera separetur. Ne vero litterae illae excederent, manus recentior quidem, sed illis tamen temporibus vicina, supra rimam aut scissionem scripsit quod supererat ex S. Marcelli elogio sic: tari viro martyrio coronatus est. Ante autem in superiore versu sunt haec, una cum Anastasio mili... Itaque de folio scripsit quod in altero viderat oculatissimus Castellanus, cujus tamen testimonio fretus noster censor sic in Pratensem codicem declamitat: Jam vero Pratensem illum codicem hic denuo erasorum et superscriptorum, quidni et suppositorum nominum vitio laborare comperit in hisce rebus oculatissimus clar. Castellanus, qui fontem consuluit, etc. Deinde sollicite investigat unde petitus Salvius in nostrum codicem inductus fuerit, suspicaturque e Sangermanis monachis aliquem fuisse, qui hoc additamento sinceram et primigeniam Usuardi simplicitatem interpolaverit. Quibus nugis, ne quid dicam asperius, refellendis tempus non consumam. Dixisse Sollerio dolebit, et pro indictis haberi volet. (BOUILLART.) Apud Trientinam urbem, sancti Vigilii episcopi, qui sub Stilicone consule pro Christi nomine magno lapidum imbre percussus martyrium complevit. In pago Pictavensi, sancti Maxentii presbyteri et confessoris gloriosi in miraculis. In portu Valentianas, sancti Salvii Engolismae civitatis episcopi.]

The sixth day before the Kalends · 26 June

  • 193

At Rome, the birthday of Saints John and Paul, brothers, of whom the first was chamberlain, the second primicerius, of Constantia the virgin, daughter of Constantine; who afterwards under Julian received the palm of martyrdom, the sword cutting them down. In the district of Poitiers, of Saint Maxentius, presbyter and confessor, glorious in miracles. On the same day, of Saint Perseveranda, virgin. [In Bouillart: At Rome, the birthday of Saints John and Paul, brothers, of whom the first was chamberlain, the second primicerius, of Constancia the virgin, daughter of Constantine; who afterwards under Julian 195received the palm of martyrdom, the sword cutting them down. In Sollerius's edition Vigilius has been moved from this place and put back to the 31st day of January, where Sollerius argues that he was erased, and we confess it. But in this place it will not be beside the point to advise the reader on what other ground the commemoration of Vigilius is suspect to Sollerius. Plainly that long passage pleases him less, which is wholly at odds with the native simplicity of Usuardus, a simplicity which the phrase he had used on 31 January reflects far better: 'At the city of Trent, of Saint Vigilius, bishop and martyr.' Nay, that brevity and simplicity so delights and captivates Sollerius that he would have it be the rule: 'Let our most received rule hold here,' he says, 'namely, that the original purity shines out the more, the briefer and simpler the text is.' But, Sollerius, why on the 21st day of this month did you reject the eulogy of Eusebius, so brief, so simple, and prefer one elaborated and puffed up with false titles? Why on the 25th did you judge the Pratensis eulogy of Saint Gallicanus to be Usuardus's, although it fills almost the whole article? Your Tornacensis codex gave it much more briefly and simply. Why on 18 May did you copy out the whole eulogy of Mark and Marcellianus? How brief, how simple a one the Pulsanensis codex offered you: 'At Rome, the birthday of the holy martyrs Mark and Marcellianus.' The same codex on the 15th simply names Vitus, Modestus and Crescentia; why did you prefer three whole lines? The Tornacensis codex cuts short by almost half the eulogy of blessed Nazarius, martyr, on the 13th. Why did you think the Pratensis and others were to be preferred to it? But why dwell on it at length? I have seen hardly a day in your whole Martyrology on which one eulogy briefer and simpler than another is not brought forward, and on which at the same time you have not departed from your rule. You do not now ask of me that I prove by reasons that Vigilius was erased by Usuardus from the 31st day of January and reserved for this day for just cause. Your own people admit the fact: 'No acts,' they say, 'express the day of his martyrdom: but the church of Trent celebrates the feast of Saint Vigilius on the sixth day before the Kalends of July, probably as his birthday.' Such certainly Florus of Lyon seems to have judged it, when in his additions to the genuine Martyrology of Bede he added the eulogy of Saint Vigilius. Nor did the critic of the Pratensis codex strike only Vigilius out of this article, but Salvius of Angoulême as well, in whose place he judged that Perseveranda should be substituted. On what arguments, pray? On the silence of his codices about Saint Salvius, and their agreement about Perseveranda. I have said already, and not once only, that each of those arguments is of no strength: that it is no wonder that Sollerius's codices agree in mentioning some saint who is lacking in the Pratensis, or that saints whom the Pratensis shows are lacking in them; that this came about because almost all the codices were copied from the first exemplar of Usuardus's Martyrology, and because commemorations were deleted by Usuardus which did not at all satisfy him, or added which he held preferable. So he erased Perseveranda from here because, having read her acts, he noticed that she died not on the sixth but on the seventh day before the Kalends of this month. For so the Acts: 'The deposition of the virgin herself is celebrated on the seventh day before the Kalends of July,' etc. And he put Salvius in her place because the Acts testify that this was the day of his death: 'Saint Salvius and his disciple with him were martyred together under Vuinegardus on the sixth day before the Kalends of July.' But if Sollerius denies that the Acts of Saint Salvius were read by Usuardus, why should he not have imitated Florus and Wandalbert, who both made mention of that prelate on this day? Here I advise Sollerius that his inspector was ill-sighted on this day, who seemed to himself to see an erasure in the Pratensis codex. This whole page is sound, wholly untouched by the point of the knife. But it is one of those few leaves which were substituted in place of those which Usuardus himself tore out: either because for the number of erasures they could not be read, or because the edge of the knife had torn the parchment itself. Why Castellanus wrote that Salvius had been deleted, I suspect the cause was this. On the next leaf after Salvius, though a little higher up, the middle of a line is torn or cut, so that the upper part of the letters is separated from the lower. And lest those letters be lost, a more recent hand indeed, but yet near to those times, wrote above the rent or cut what remained of the eulogy of Saint Marcellus, thus: 'tari viro martyrio coronatus est.' Before it, in the line above, are these words: 'una cum Anastasio mili...' So of the one leaf the most sharp-eyed Castellanus wrote what he had seen on the other; and yet, relying on his testimony, our censor declaims thus against the Pratensis codex: 'But now the celebrated Castellanus, most sharp-eyed in these matters, who consulted the source, has found that that Pratensis codex here again labours under the fault of erased and overwritten, and why not also of substituted, names,' etc. Then he inquires anxiously whence Salvius was sought and brought into our codex, and suspects that it was one of the monks of Saint-Germain who interpolated the sincere and original simplicity of Usuardus with this addition. I will not waste time refuting these trifles, not to say anything harsher. Sollerius will be sorry to have said it, and will wish it held unsaid. (Bouillart.) At the Trientina city, of Saint Vigilius, bishop, who under the consul Stilicho, for the name of Christ, struck by a great shower of stones, completed his martyrdom. In the district of Poitiers, of Saint Maxentius, presbyter and confessor, glorious in miracles. At the port of Valentianae, of Saint Salvius, bishop of the city of Engolisma.]

V Kal. Die 27.

  • 199

Apud Galatiam, beati Crescentis, discipuli sancti Pauli apostoli, qui in Gallias transitum faciens, verbo praedicationis, multos ad fidem Christi convertit. Rediens vero ad gentem cui specialiter episcopus fuerat datus, Galatas, usque ad beatum finem vitae suae, in opere Domini confortavit. Apud Tiburtinam urbem Italiae, natalis sanctae Symphorosae cum septem filiis, Crescente, Juliano, Nemesio, Primitivo, Justino, Stacteo, Eugenio, quorum mater sub Adriano principe, ob insuperabilem constantiam, primo palmis caesa, deinde crinibus suspensa, novissime saxo alligato in flumen est praecipitata. Filii autem stipitibus ad trocleas extensi, diverso mortis exitu martyrium compleverunt. Cordubae, sancti Zoili martyris, cujus corpus, cum longo tempore ubinam sepultum fuerit latuisset, venerabili episcopo ejusdem loci, nomine Agapio, ex divina revelatione manifestatum est.

The fifth day before the Kalends · 27 June

  • 199

At Galatia, of blessed Crescens, disciple of Saint Paul the apostle, who, making a passage into the Gauls, converted many to the faith of Christ by the word of preaching. But returning to the nation for which he had been specially given as bishop, he strengthened the Galatians in the work of the Lord until the blessed end of his life. At Tiburtina, a city of Italy, the birthday of Saint Symphorosa with her seven sons, Crescens, Julian, Nemesius, Primitivus, Justinus, Stacteus, Eugenius; of whom the mother, under the emperor Hadrian, on account of her insuperable constancy, first beaten with palms, then hung up by the hair, last of all, a stone tied to her, was cast headlong into a river. But the sons, stretched on stakes to pulleys, completed their martyrdom by divers manners of death. At Cordoba, of Saint Zoilus, martyr, whose body, when it had long lain hidden where it had been buried, was made manifest by divine revelation to the venerable bishop of the same place, Agapius by name.

IV Kal. Die 28.

  • 203

Vigilia apostolorum Petri et Pauli, et natalis beati Leonis pontificis et doctoris. Apud Lugdunum Galliae, sancti Hirenei episcopi et martyris, quem constat beati Policarpi sacerdotis et martyris fuisse discipulum, ut scribit Hieronymus apostolorum temporum vicinum. Postea vero persecutione Severi, cum omni fere civitatis suae populo, glorioso coronatus est martyrio. Alexandriae, sanctorum martyrum Plutarchi, Sereni, Heraclidis, Herois, Potamienae, Marcellae cum aliis tribus, inter quos praecipue emicuit Potamiena virgo, quae primo immensos et innumeros agones pro virginitate desudans, deinde etiam pro martyrio exquisita et inaudita tormenta perpessa, ad ultimum igni consumpta est.

The fourth day before the Kalends · 28 June

  • 203

The Vigil of the apostles Peter and Paul, and the birthday of blessed Leo, pontiff and doctor. At Lyon of Gaul, of Saint Hireneus, bishop and martyr, who, it is agreed, was the disciple of blessed Policarpus, priest and martyr, as Jerome writes, near to the times of the apostles. But afterwards, in the persecution of Severus, with almost all the people of his city, he was crowned with a glorious martyrdom. At Alexandria, of the holy martyrs Plutarchus, Serenus, Heraclides, Heros, Potamiena, Marcella, with three others, among whom Potamiena the virgin shone forth especially, who first sweating through immense and innumerable contests for her virginity, then also, having suffered exquisite and unheard-of torments for martyrdom, at the last was consumed by fire.

III Kal. Die 29.

  • 207

Romae, natalis beatorum apostolorum Petri et Pauli, qui passi sunt sub Nerone Caesare, Basso et Tusco consulibus. Quorum prior in eadem urbe juxta viam Triumphalem totius orbis veneratione celebratur, sequens quoque in via Ostiensi sepultus, pari habetur honore. Eodem die, sancti Marcelli martyris, qui apud castrum Argentomacum pro fide Christi una cum Anastasio militari, capite plexus est [ Bouillart.: cum Anastasio militari viro martyrio coronatus est]. In territorio Senonico, Beatae, virginis.

The third day before the Kalends · 29 June

  • 207

At Rome, the birthday of the blessed apostles Peter and Paul, who suffered under Nero Caesar, in the consulship of Bassus and Tuscus. Of whom the former is celebrated in the same city beside the Triumphal Way with the veneration of the whole world; the following one also, buried on the Ostian Way, is held in equal honour. On the same day, of Saint Marcellus, martyr, who at the fort of Argentomacum, for the faith of Christ, together with Anastasius, a soldier, was beheaded [Bouillart: with Anastasius, a military man, was crowned with martyrdom]. In the territory Senonicus, of Beata, virgin.

Pridie Kal. Die 30.

  • 209

Commemoratio sancti Pauli apostoli, quem cum beato Petro cruce appenso, coelum gladio necatum excepit una [ Addit Bouillart.: Haec verba, non tamen... scribit, a Sollerio praetermissa sunt in sua Usuardi editione, quae tamen vere sunt Usuardina et in autographo pure, simpliciter et prima manu conscripta, nullo liturae vestigio, nullo atramenti discrimine, eadem litterarum forma. Idem Aratoris testimonium refert Dervensis codex, ut non dubium sit quin fuerit ab Usuardo scriptum. Quidquid garrit Sollerius in hunc locum, Usuardum Adonis opinioni adhaesisse; ut istum, sic et illum somniasse utrumque apostolum non evoluto anni tempore, sed uno eodemque Neronis XVI martyrium subiisse; Aratoris sententiam a recentiori aliquo sciolo esse intrusam: haec omnia verba sunt, praetereaque nihil. Hic non quaero utrum ducem sequi debuerit Usuardus, Adonem an Aratorem. Adoni favent Dionysius Corinthius, Bucherii Kalendarium, Epiphanius, Hieronymus, Asterius et concilium Lateranense sub Gelasio: ante Aratorem in eadem sententia fuerant Prudentius, Augustinus, Nilus apud Photium. At si verum est, quod affirmat Lateranense concilium, postremam hanc opinionem ab haereticis fuisse profectam, nescio qua mente dicere potuerit Sollerius hoc esse Adonis somnium, nisi forte somnium illud ad Adonem venerit ex porta cornea, qua veris datur exitus umbris. Non tamen eadem die, sed evoluto anni tempore, ut vir disertus Arator scribit]. Romae, natalis [ Bouillart. om. natalis] beatissimae Lucinae discipulae apostolorum. Lemovicas civitate, sancti Marcialis episcopi, cum duobus presbyteris Alpiniano et Stratocliniano [ Bouillart., Stratocliano], quorum vita miraculorum signis admodum effulsit. Ipsa die, sancti Corsici presbiteri et Leonis subdiaconi. In territorio Vivariensi, sancti Ostiani presbiteri et confessoris.

The day before the Kalends · 30 June

  • 209

The commemoration of Saint Paul the apostle, whom, slain with the sword, heaven received together with blessed Peter hung on the cross [Bouillart adds: These words, 'Not however ... writes', were passed over by Sollerius in his edition of Usuardus; yet they are truly Usuardus's, and in the autograph they are written cleanly, simply and in the first hand, with no trace of erasure, no difference of ink, the same form of letters. The Dervensis codex gives the same testimony of Arator, so that there is no doubt that it was written by Usuardus. Whatever Sollerius chatters on this place, that Usuardus adhered to the opinion of Ado; that like the one, so the other dreamed that both apostles underwent martyrdom not when the time of a year had rolled round, but on one and the same day, in the sixteenth of Nero; that the opinion of Arator was intruded by some more recent smatterer: all these are words, and nothing more. Here I do not ask whether Usuardus ought to have followed Ado or Arator as his guide. On Ado's side are Dionysius of Corinth, Bucherius's Kalendar, Epiphanius, Jerome, Asterius and the Lateran council under Gelasius: before Arator, Prudentius, Augustine, and Nilus in Photius had been of the same opinion. But if it is true, as the Lateran council affirms, that this latter opinion proceeded from heretics, I do not know with what mind Sollerius could say that this is Ado's dream, unless perhaps that dream came to Ado through the gate of horn, by which exit is given to true shades. Not however on the same day, but when the time of a year had rolled round, as the eloquent man Arator writes]. At Rome, the birthday [Bouillart omits natalis] of most blessed Lucina, disciple of the apostles. In the city of Lemovicae, of Saint Marcialis, bishop, with two presbyters, Alpinianus and Stratoclinianus [Bouillart: Stratocliano], whose life shone forth exceedingly with the signs of miracles. On this same day, of Saints Corsicus, presbyter, and Leo, subdeacon. In the territory Vivariensis, of Saint Ostianus, presbyter and confessor.

MENSIS JULIUS. HABET DIES XXXI.

  • 213

JULY HAS 31 DAYS.

  • 213

Kalendis. Die 1.

  • 213

In monte Or, depositio Aaron sacerdotis primi. In territorio Lugdunensi, depositio beati Domitiani abbatis, qui primis illic heremiticam vitam exercuit, et plurimos sibi in Dei servitio aggregans, magnisque virtutibus et gloriosis miraculis valde clarus, collectus est ad patres in senectute bona. Civitate Engolisma, sancti Eparchi monachi et confessoris. Aninsulae monasterio, sancti Carilefi presbiteri. In territorio Remensi, sancti Theodorici presbiteri. In portu Valencianas, sancti Salvi confessoris. [ Apud Bouillart.: Civitate Engolisma, sancti Eparchi confessoris. De tribus ultimis commemorationibus Carolinum autographum sic habet : Aninsulae in monasterio, sancti Carilefi presbiteri. Item in territorio Remensi sancti Theodorici presbiteri. In portu Valentianas, sancti Salvii confessoris. In suo Codice Usuardus, pro Aninsulae in monasterio, scripsit, in pago Cinomannico. Quae correctio non hoc loco tantum, sed etiam pluribus aliis occurrit. Causam hanc esse verisimile est, quod plerisque multo minus nota sint peculiaria quaedam loca, quam regiones, ubi sita sunt. Ut igitur quibus futurum in usu erat martyrologium, illi facilius perspectum haberent quibuscum communis ipsis erat in sanctum quempiam veneratio ; regiones potius ubi sanctorum cultus celebris erat, quam loca designavit. Forte etiam causae huic accessit altera, nempe ut variaret orationem. Jam ante scripserat in territorio Lugdunensi pro peculiari loco, ubi sanctus Domicianus obierat et colebatur : postea scripsit in pago Cinomannico ut eodem modo alia tamen voce denotaret locum, ubi notus erat sanctus Carilefus. Atque sic minus affert fastidii vox territorium, qua in tertia commemoratione uti debebat. Addo hujus generis correctiones non venire in mentem interpolatori. Sane ejusmodi homines addere, demere, transferre soleant ; sed quid lucri est peculiaris loci nomen delere ut generalius regionis nomen indicetur? De sancto Theodorico nihil est, quod in tuenda correctione laborem. Illum presbyterum in Carolino autographo vocaverit ; confessoris titulo donaverit in suo codice, perinde est, et in nugis hujusmodi hariolari non vacat. Salvius autem quam docte hinc erasus fuerit, patet ex iis quae ad diem 26 Junii observavimus. Denique sanctum Leonorium apposite hic memoratum fuisse, res est extra controversiam posita. Leonorius ille est, qui a puero educatus et primis religionis Christianae elementis a sancto Hilduto imbutus, postea episcopus sine sede creatus est, ut Christi nomen quocunque ferre liberius posset. Celebris fuit sexto Ecclesiae saeculo, regnante in Gallia Childeberto, cui acceptissimus erat. De illo plura narrant Usserius in Britannicarum Ecclesiarum antiquitatibus et Cointius in Annalibus Ecclesiasticis Francorum, quae huc referre praesentis non est instituti. In pago Cinomannico sancti Carilefi presbiteri. Item in territorio Remensi, sancti Theodorici confessoris. In Britannia sancti Leonorii confessoris et episcopi.]

On the Kalends · 1 July

  • 213

On Mount Or, the deposition of Aaron, the first priest. In the territory of Lyon, the deposition of blessed Domitianus, abbot, who among the first there practised the hermit's life, and, gathering very many to himself in the service of God, and very renowned for great virtues and glorious miracles, was gathered to his fathers in a good old age. In the city of Engolisma, of Saint Eparchius, monk and confessor. In the monastery of Aninsula, of Saint Carilefus, presbyter. In the territory of Reims, of Saint Theodoricus, presbyter. At the port of Valencianae, of Saint Salvi, confessor. [In Bouillart: In the city of Engolisma, of Saint Eparchius, confessor. Of the last three commemorations the Caroline autograph has thus: In the monastery of Aninsula, of Saint Carilefus, presbyter. Also in the territory of Reims, of Saint Theodoricus, presbyter. At the port of Valentianae, of Saint Salvius, confessor. In his own codex Usuardus, for in the monastery of Aninsula, wrote in the district Cinomannicus. This correction occurs not in this place only but in many others too. It is likely that the cause was this: that to most people certain particular places are much less known than the regions in which they are situated. So that those who were to have the martyrology in use might the more easily perceive with whom they shared a common veneration of some saint, he designated the regions where the saints' cult was celebrated rather than the places. Perhaps too a second cause was added to this one, namely to vary the phrasing. He had already written in the territory of Lyon for the particular place where Saint Domicianus had died and was venerated: afterwards he wrote in the district Cinomannicus, so as to denote in the same manner, yet with another word, the place where Saint Carilefus was known. And so the word territorium, which he had to use in the third commemoration, brings less tedium. I add that corrections of this kind do not come into an interpolator's mind. Such men are indeed wont to add, take away, transpose; but what is gained by deleting the name of a particular place so that the more general name of a region may be shown? Of Saint Theodoricus there is nothing for which I need labour in defending the correction. Whether he called him presbyter in the Caroline autograph and granted him the title of confessor in his own codex is all one, and there is no leisure for divining in trifles of this kind. How learnedly Salvius was erased from here is plain from what we observed at 26 June. Lastly, that Saint Leonorius was fittingly commemorated here is a matter placed beyond controversy. This is the Leonorius who, brought up from a boy and instructed in the first elements of the Christian religion by Saint Hildutus, was afterwards made a bishop without a see, so that he might carry the name of Christ wherever he would the more freely. He was celebrated in the sixth century of the Church, in the reign of Childebert in Gaul, to whom he was most acceptable. More is told of him by Ussher in his Antiquities of the British Churches and by Le Cointe in the Ecclesiastical Annals of the Franks, which it is not the purpose of the present work to relate here. In the district Cinomannicus, of Saint Carilefus, presbyter. Also in the territory of Reims, of Saint Theodoricus, confessor. In Britain, of Saint Leonorius, confessor and bishop.]

VI Non. Die 2.

  • 219

Romae, via Aurelia, natalis sanctorum Processi et Martiniani, qui a beato Petro apostolo baptizati, sub Nerone autem oris contusionem, equuleum, nervos, fustes, flammas, scorpiones perpessi, novissime gladio caesi martyrio coronantur. Ipso die, sanctorum trium militum, qui cum beato Paulo apostolo passi sunt. Item, sanctorum martyrum Aristonis, Crescentiani, Euticiani, Urbani, Vitalis, Justi, Felicissimi, Felicis, Martiae, et Symphorosae, qui omnes apud Campaniam glorioso martyrio coronati sunt. Turonis, depositio sanctae Monegundis religiosae [ Bouillart. Hanc codicis Pratensis lectionem, sanctae Monegundis Deo sacratae, Sollerius optimam esse confitetur. Sed quia Heriniensis et Tornacensis codices religiosam legunt, timuit ne vel in rebus nihili Pratensem antetulisse videretur. Deo sacratae].

The sixth day before the Nones · 2 July

  • 219

At Rome, on the Aurelian Way, the birthday of Saints Processus and Martinianus, who were baptized by blessed Peter the apostle, but under Nero, having endured the bruising of the mouth, the rack, sinews, cudgels, flames and scorpions, at the last cut down with the sword, are crowned with martyrdom. On this same day, of the three holy soldiers who suffered with blessed Paul the apostle. Also, of the holy martyrs Aristo, Crescentianus, Euticianus, Urbanus, Vitalis, Justus, Felicissimus, Felix, Martia and Symphorosa, who were all crowned with glorious martyrdom in Campania. At Tours, the deposition of Saint Monegundis, a religious [Bouillart: This reading of the Pratensis codex, of Saint Monegundis, consecrated to God, Sollerius confesses to be the best. But because the Heriniensis and Tornacensis codices read religiosam ('a religious'), he feared lest he should seem to have preferred the Pratensis even in things of no account. Deo sacratae].

V Non. Die 3.

  • 221

Apud Edessam Mesopotamiae, translatio corporis sancti Thomae apostoli. Ipso die apud Cneocaesaream Ponti, natalis beati Gregorii episcopi et martyris, qui magna signorum, ac miraculorum gloria praeditus, inter caetera quodam tempore, ut ecclesiae faciendae locus sufficeret, montem precibus movit. Civitate Clusina, sanctorum martyrum Hirenei diaconi et Mustiolae nobilis matronae, qui passi sunt sub Aureliano principe. In Laodicia Siriae, sancti Anatholii episcopi, qui non solum religiosis viris, sed et philosophis admiranda scripta dereliquit. Alexandriae, sancti Triphonis et aliorum decem. Item Constantinopoli, sancti Eulogii.

The fifth day before the Nones · 3 July

  • 221

At Edessa of Mesopotamia, the translation of the body of Saint Thomas the apostle. On this same day, at Cneocaesarea of Pontus, the birthday of blessed Gregory, bishop and martyr, who, endowed with the great glory of signs and miracles, among other things on a certain occasion, so that there might be room enough for building a church, moved a mountain by his prayers. In the city Clusina, of the holy martyrs Hireneus, deacon, and Mustiola, a noble matron, who suffered under the emperor Aurelian. In Laodicea of Syria, of Saint Anatholius, bishop, who left behind writings to be admired not only by religious men but also by philosophers. At Alexandria, of Saint Triphon and ten others. Also at Constantinople, of Saint Eulogius.

IV Non. Die 4.

  • 225

Oseae prophetae. Turonis, translatio beati Martini episcopi et confessoris, et ordinatio episcopatus ejus. In Africa, natalis sancti Jocundiani martyris, in mare mersi. Apud Sirmium, sanctorum martyrum Innocentii et Sebastiae, cum aliis triginta. In territorio Bituricae civitatis, sancti Lauriani martyris, cujus caput Hispalim ad Hispanias deportatum est.

The fourth day before the Nones · 4 July

  • 225

Of Oseas the prophet. At Tours, the translation of blessed Martin, bishop and confessor, and the ordination of his episcopate. In Africa, the birthday of Saint Jocundianus, martyr, plunged into the sea. At Sirmium, of the holy martyrs Innocentius and Sebastia, with thirty others. In the territory of the city of Bourges, of Saint Laurianus, martyr, whose head was carried to Seville, to the Spains.

III Non. Die 5.

  • 227

Apud Syriam, natalis sancti Domitii martyris, qui virtutibus suis multa incolis beneficia praestat. Romae, sanctae Zoae martyris, quae dum ad Confessionem Petri apostoli oraret, a persecutoribus arctata et in custodiam obscurissimam trusa, dein a collo et a capillis in arbore suspensa, adhibito subter horribili fumo, in confessione Domini emisit spiritum.

The third day before the Nones · 5 July

  • 227

At Syria, the birthday of Saint Domitius, martyr, who by his virtues bestows many benefits on the inhabitants. At Rome, of Saint Zoa, martyr, who, while she was praying at the Confession of Peter the apostle, was seized by the persecutors and thrust into a most dark prison, then hung upon a tree by the neck and by the hair, a horrible smoke being applied beneath, and gave up the spirit in the confession of the Lord.

Pridie Non. Die 6.

  • 229

Apud Judeam, Isaiae prophetae, qui sectus in duas partes occubuit, sepultusque est sub quercu Rogel, juxta transitum aquarum. Item Octavae apostolorum et primus beati apostoli Pauli ingressus in urbem Romam, anno secundo Neronis imperii. Romae, natalis sancti Tranquillini martyris, qui dum ad beati Pauli Confessionem oraret, tentus a paganis ac lapidatus, martyrium consummavit. In pago Treverensi, sancti Goaris presbyteri et confessoris.

The day before the Nones · 6 July

  • 229

At Judaea, of Isaias the prophet, who, cut into two parts, fell, and was buried under the oak Rogel, beside the crossing of the waters. Also the Octave of the apostles, and the first entry of blessed Paul the apostle into the city of Rome, in the second year of the reign of Nero. At Rome, the birthday of Saint Tranquillinus, martyr, who, while he was praying at the Confession of blessed Paul, was seized by the pagans and stoned, and consummated his martyrdom. In the district of Trier, of Saint Goar, presbyter and confessor.

Nonis. Die 7.

  • 233

Apud Alexandriam, natalis sancti Panteni, viri apostolici et omni sapientia adornati, cujus tantum studii et amoris erga verbum Dei fuit, ut etiam ad praedicandum Christi Evangelium omnibus gentibus, quae in ultimis Orientis secessibus reconduntur, fidei et devotionis calore profectus sit. Romae, sanctorum martyrum Nicostrati, Claudii, Castorii, Victorini, Simphroniani, quos Fabianus judex per decem dies minis et blanditiis agens, cum in nullo penitus posset commovere, jussit eos tertio torqueri, ac postea in mare praecipites dari. [ Addit Bouillart., ex margine cod. Prat.: Eodem die octavae sancti Marcialis episcopi et confessoris.]

On the Nones · 7 July

  • 233

At Alexandria, the birthday of Saint Pantenus, an apostolic man and adorned with all wisdom, whose zeal and love towards the word of God was so great that he even set out, with the warmth of faith and devotion, to preach the Gospel of Christ to all the nations which are hidden away in the farthest recesses of the East. At Rome, of the holy martyrs Nicostratus, Claudius, Castorius, Victorinus and Simphronianus, whom Fabianus the judge, dealing with them for ten days by threats and blandishments, when he could not move them in anything at all, ordered to be tortured a third time, and afterwards to be cast headlong into the sea. [Bouillart adds, from the margin of the Pratensis codex: On the same day, the Octave of Saint Marcialis, bishop and confessor.]

VIII Idus Die 8.

  • 237

Apud Asiam minorem, sancti Aquilae et Priscillae ejus uxoris, de quibus in Actibus apostolorum scribitur. In Palestina, natalis beati Procopii martyris, qui ab Scitopoli ductus Caesaream, ad primam responsionum ejus confidentiam, a judice Fabiano capite caesus est. Eodem die, Carolinum autographum socios adjungit sancto Ciliano. Nulla tamen hic litura in Pratensi : quia folium aliud est, quod nunquam non animadverti, ubi litura non videtur et codex tamen a Carolino autographo discrepat. BOUILLART. sancti Ciliani martyris.

The eighth day before the Ides · 8 July

  • 237

At Asia Minor, of Saints Aquila and Priscilla his wife, of whom it is written in the Acts of the Apostles. In Palestine, the birthday of blessed Procopius, martyr, who, brought from Scitopolis to Caesarea, at the first boldness of his answers was beheaded by the judge Fabianus. On the same day, The Caroline autograph joins companions to Saint Cilianus. Yet there is no erasure here in the Pratensis: because it is another leaf, and I have never failed to observe that there no erasure is seen and yet the codex differs from the Caroline autograph. Bouillart. of Saint Cilianus, martyr.

VII Idus. Die 9.

  • 239

Romae ad Guttam jugiter manantem, natalis sancti Zenonis martyris et aliorum decem millium ducentorum trium. Eodem die, beati Cirilli episcopi, qui flammis injectus, cum illesus evasisset, ac stupore tanti miraculi a judice esset dimissus, rursus pro instanti et alacri praedicatione facta de Christo, ab eodem capite plexus est. In civitate Tyro, passio sanctorum Anatholiae et Audacis sub Decio imperatore, quorum Anatholia Christi virgo, diversis plagarum generibus vexata, novissime gladio transverberata est in oratione. Audax quoque in custodiam datus; nec mora, capitali sententia coronatur. Civitate Martula, sancti Brictii episcopi et confessoris, qui pro confessione Domini postquam est multa perpessus, et ab angelo sancto et beato Petro Apostolo confortatus, item magnas populorum credentium multitudines omnipotenti Deo lucrifaciens, quievit in pace.

The seventh day before the Ides · 9 July

  • 239

At Rome, at the Gutta that flows continually, the birthday of Saint Zeno, martyr, and of ten thousand two hundred and three others. On the same day, of blessed Cirillus, bishop, who, cast into the flames, when he had come out unharmed and had been released by the judge in amazement at so great a miracle, was again, for the urgent and eager preaching he had made of Christ, beheaded by the same man. In the city of Tyre, the passion of Saints Anatholia and Audax under the emperor Decius; of whom Anatholia, the virgin of Christ, harassed with various kinds of blows, was at the last run through with the sword while at prayer. Audax also was given into custody; and without delay he is crowned by capital sentence. In the city of Martula, of Saint Brictius, bishop and confessor, who, after he had suffered many things for the confession of the Lord, and had been strengthened by a holy angel and by blessed Peter the Apostle, gaining likewise great multitudes of believing peoples for almighty God, rested in peace.

VI Idus. Die 10.

  • 243

Romae, septem fratrum filiorum sanctae Felicitatis, id est Januarii, Felicis, Philippi, Silvani, Alexandri, Vitalis, Martialis. E quibus Januarius tempore Antonini, post verbera virgarum et carcerem, ad plumbatas occisus est. Felix et Philippus fustibus mactati. Silvanus praecipitio interemptus. Alexander, Vitalis, et Martialis capitali sententia puniti. In Africa, natalis sanctorum Januarii, Marini, Naboris et Felicis decollatorum. Item Romae, sanctarum virginum Rufinae et Secundae, quae sub persecutione Valeriani et Galiani tormentis subactae, ad ultimum una, capiti illiso gladio, altera, caesa cervice, migraverunt.

The sixth day before the Ides · 10 July

  • 243

At Rome, of the seven brothers, sons of Saint Felicitas, that is, Januarius, Felix, Philip, Silvanus, Alexander, Vitalis and Martialis. Of whom Januarius, in the time of Antoninus, after beatings with rods and prison, was killed under the leaded scourges. Felix and Philip were slain with cudgels. Silvanus was killed by being thrown down from a height. Alexander, Vitalis and Martialis were punished by capital sentence. In Africa, the birthday of Saints Januarius, Marinus, Nabor and Felix, beheaded. Also at Rome, of the holy virgins Rufina and Secunda, who, subdued by torments under the persecution of Valerian and Gallienus, at the last departed, the one with her head struck by the sword, the other with her neck cut.

V Idus. Die 11.

  • 247

Translatio sancti Benedicti abbatis. Apud Armeniam minorem, civitate Nicopoli, natalis sanctorum martyrum Januarii, et Pelagiae, qui equuleo, ungulis, et testarum fragmentis diebus quatuor cruciati, martyrium impleverunt [ Interserit Bouillart.: Cum solus veritatis amor me impulerit, ut hoc quantulumcunque opus susciperem, longe absum ut veritatem occultare velim aut etiam dissimulare, vel mihi incommodam. Itaque ultro fateor litura hunc articulum deturpari ab hac voce cruciati ad finem ; et scripturam quae nunc liturae spatium occupat, alterius esse manus, licet Usuardo aequalis. Non idcirco tamen ausim affirmare, ut Sollerius, Martianam intrusam fuisse. Rem in medio relinquo; quam lector, si velit, definiet auctoritate trium codicum Antuerp.-Maj., Rosweyd., Aquicinct., quos Sollerius Pratensem secutos esse testatur, et trium Pratensium B, D, E, Conchens. et Montisburg., qui lectionem eamdem referunt. BOUILLART. In Mauritania Caesariensi, sanctae Martianae virginis et martyris]. In territorio Senonico, beati Sidronii martyris. Item in territorio Pictavensi, sancti Sabini confessoris.

The fifth day before the Ides · 11 July

  • 247

The translation of Saint Benedict, abbot. At Armenia Minor, in the city of Nicopolis, the birthday of the holy martyrs Januarius and Pelagia, who, tortured for four days with the rack, with hooks and with fragments of potsherds, fulfilled their martyrdom [Bouillart inserts: Since the love of truth alone drove me to undertake this work, such as it is, I am far from wishing to hide the truth or even to disguise it, even when it is inconvenient to me. So I freely confess that this article is disfigured by an erasure from the word 'cruciati' to the end; and that the script which now occupies the space of the erasure is of another hand, though contemporary with Usuardus. Not on that account, however, would I dare to affirm, as Sollerius does, that Martiana was intruded. I leave the matter open; the reader, if he wishes, will settle it by the authority of the three codices Antuerp.-Maj., Rosweyd., Aquicinct., which Sollerius testifies followed the Pratensis, and of the three of the Prés B, D, E, the Conchens. and the Montisburg., which give the same reading. IV Idus. Die 12.

  • 249

Apud Cyprum, beati Nasonis, antiqui Christi discipuli. In Aquileia natalis sancti Hermagorae episcopi, discipuli beati Marci evangelistae, qui inter miracula sanitatum et praedicationis instantiam, ac populorum conversionem, plurima poenarum genera expertus, ad ultimum cum Fortunato archidiacono suo, capitali supplicio perpetuum meruit triumphum. In Caesarea, sancti Dii Sanctus Dius additus hic est ab Usuardo post oblatum Carolo regi primum sui Martyrologii exemplum. De illo sancto nihil in Dervensi codice. The fourth day before the Ides · 12 July

  • 249

At Cyprus, of blessed Nason, an old disciple of Christ. In Aquileia, the birthday of Saint Hermagoras, bishop, disciple of blessed Mark the evangelist, who, amid miracles of healing and earnestness of preaching and the conversion of peoples, having experienced very many kinds of punishments, at the last, with Fortunatus his archdeacon, merited by capital punishment an everlasting triumph. In Caesarea, of Saint Dius Saint Dius was added here by Usuardus after the first copy of his Martyrology had been offered to King Charles. Of that saint there is nothing in the Dervensis codex. The Hieronymian lists and Ado record him. Bouillart..

III Idus. Die 13.

  • 253

Joel et Esdrae prophetarum. Apud Macedoniam, natalis beati Sileae apostoli; qui cum esset unus de primis fratribus, et ab apostolis ad ecclesias gentium destinatus, praedicationis officium, gratia Domini plenus, instanter consummavit, atque in passionibus suis Christum clarificans, postmodum requievit. In Africa, sanctorum confessorum Eugenii Cartaginensis episcopi, fide ac virtutibus gloriosi, et universi cleri Ecclesiae ejusdem, qui caede inediaque macerati, fere quingenti vel eo amplius, inter quos quamplurimi erant lectores infantuli, gaudentes in Domino, procul exilio crudeli extrusi sunt. Erant autem in eis nobilissimi, archidiaconus nomine Salutaris, et Muritta secundus in officio ministrorum, qui tertio confessores effecti, gloriosae in Christo perseverantiae titulo illustrati sunt. In Britannia minori, sancti Turiani episcopi et confessoris, mirae simplicitatis et innocentiae viri.

The third day before the Ides · 13 July

  • 253

Of Joel and Esdras the prophets. At Macedonia, the birthday of blessed Silas the apostle; who, being one of the first brethren, and appointed by the apostles to the churches of the Gentiles, earnestly consummated the office of preaching, full of the grace of the Lord, and, glorifying Christ in his sufferings, afterwards rested. In Africa, of the holy confessors Eugenius, bishop of Carthage, glorious in faith and virtues, and of all the clergy of the same Church, who, wasted by slaughter and hunger, about five hundred or more, among whom were very many readers who were little children, rejoicing in the Lord, were driven far off into cruel exile. And there were among them men most noble, an archdeacon named Salutaris, and Muritta, second in the office of the ministers, who, made confessors a third time, were made illustrious by the title of glorious perseverance in Christ. In Lesser Britain, of Saint Turianus, bishop and confessor, a man of wonderful simplicity and innocence.

Pridie Idus. Die 14.

  • 257

Apud Pontum, natalis beati Focae episcopi civitatis Sinopis, qui sub Trajano imperatore, carcerem, vincula, ferrum, ignem etiam pro Christo superavit. Alexandriae, sancti Heracleae antistitis, ob cujus opinionem celeberrimam memorat se Africanus historiographus, ad eum properasse.

The day before the Ides · 14 July

  • 257

At Pontus, the birthday of blessed Focas, bishop of the city of Sinope, who under the emperor Trajan overcame prison, chains, iron, and fire also, for Christ. At Alexandria, of Saint Heracleae, prelate, to whom, on account of his most celebrated repute, Africanus the historiographer records that he hastened.

Idibus. Die 15.

  • 261

Nisibi, natalis sancti Jacobi episcopi, magnae virtutis viri. Hic unus fuit e numero confessorum sub persecutione Maximini, et eorum, qui in Nicena synodo perversitatem Arii, Omousii oppositione damnarunt. Romae in portu, natalis sanctorum Eutropii, Zosimae et Bonosae sororum. Apud Cartaginem, sanctorum martyrum Catulini diaconi, Januarii, Florentii, Juliae et Justae, qui sunt positi in Basilica Fausti. Alexandriae, sanctorum Philippi, Zenonis, Narsei et decem infantum.

On the Ides · 15 July

  • 261

At Nisibis, the birthday of Saint James, bishop, a man of great virtue. He was one of the number of the confessors under the persecution of Maximinus, and of those who in the synod of Nicaea condemned the perversity of Arius by the opposition of the Omousion. At Rome, at the Port, the birthday of Saints Eutropius, Zosima and Bonosa, sisters. At Carthage, of the holy martyrs Catulinus, deacon, Januarius, Florentius, Julia and Justa, who are laid in the Basilica of Faustus. At Alexandria, of Saints Philip, Zeno, Narseus and ten children.

XVII Kal. Die 16.

  • 263

Apud Antiochiam Syriae natalis beati Eustachii episcopi, et confessoris, sub Constantino principe, qui ob catholicam fidem Trajanopolim civitatem Thraciae pulsus exilio, ibidem requievit. In Ostia, passio sancti Hilarii monachi, qui cum nollet sub persecutione Juliani sacrificare, fustibus caesus, martyrium sumpsit.

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 16 July

  • 263

At Antioch of Syria, the birthday of blessed Eustachius, bishop and confessor, under the emperor Constantine, who, for the catholic faith driven into exile to Trajanopolis, a city of Thrace, rested there. In Ostia, the passion of Saint Hilary, monk, who, when he would not sacrifice under the persecution of Julian, beaten with cudgels, took up martyrdom.

XVI Kal. Die 17.

  • 267

In Cartagine, natalis sanctorum martyrum Scillitanorum, id est Sperati, Natali, Cithini, Berturii, Felicis, Aquilini, Letatii, Januariae, Generosae, Bessiae, Donatae et Secundinae, sub Saturnino praefecto, qui post primam confessionem in carcerem missi et in ligno confixi, deinde gladio decollati sunt.

The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 17 July

  • 267

In Carthage, the birthday of the holy Scillitan martyrs, that is, Speratus, Natalis, Cithinus, Berturius, Felix, Aquilinus, Letatius, Januaria, Generosa, Bessia, Donata and Secundina, under the prefect Saturninus, who, after their first confession sent into prison and fastened in the wood, were then beheaded with the sword.

XV Kal. Die 18.

  • 271

Apud Cartaginem, sanctae Guddenes martyris, quae jussu Rufini proconsulis, ob Christi confessionem, quater diversis temporibus equulei extensione vexata, et ungularum horrenda laceratione cruciata, carceris etiam squalore diutissime afflicta, novissime gladio caesa est. Civitate Dorostoro, sancti Emiliani. [ Addit Bouillart., cursivo charactere: In pago Castrense, depositio S. Arnulfi, martyris et episcopi.]

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 18 July

  • 271

At Carthage, of Saint Guddenes, martyr, who, by order of the proconsul Rufinus, for the confession of Christ, was four times, at different seasons, harassed by stretching on the rack, and tortured by the horrible tearing of the hooks, afflicted also for a very long time by the filth of prison, and at the last was cut down with the sword. In the city of Dorostorum, of Saint Emilianus. [Bouillart adds, in cursive type: In the district Castrensis, the deposition of Saint Arnulfus, martyr and bishop.]

XIV Kal. Die 19.

  • 275

Natalis sancti Epafrae, qui a beato Paulo Colossis ordinatus episcopus, clarus virtutibus, ibidem martyrii palmam pro ovibus sibi commendatis virili agone percepit. Apud Thebaidem, sancti patris nostri Arsenii, de quo in verbis seniorum refertur, quia propter redundationem lachrymarum tergendam, sudarium semper in sinu, vel in manu habuerit. In Hispania, civitate Hispali, passio sanctarum Justae et Rufinae, quae a praeside Diogeniano comprehensae, primo equulei extensione et ungularum laniatione vexatae sunt, post carcere, inedia et doloribus afflictae, tandem Justa in carcere spiritum exhalavit, Rufinae cervix confracta in confessione est.

The fourteenth day before the Kalends · 19 July

  • 275

The birthday of Saint Epafras, who, ordained bishop at Colossae by blessed Paul, renowned for virtues, received there the palm of martyrdom for the sheep entrusted to him, in a manly contest. At the Thebaid, of our holy father Arsenius, of whom it is related in the Words of the Elders that, on account of the overflowing of his tears which had to be wiped, he always had a cloth in his bosom or in his hand. In Spain, in the city of Seville, the passion of the holy Justa and Rufina, who, seized by the governor Diogenianus, were first harassed by stretching on the rack and by the tearing of the hooks, afterwards afflicted with prison, hunger and pains; at length Justa breathed out her spirit in prison, and the neck of Rufina was broken in the confession.

XIII Kal. Die 20.

  • 277

Natalis beati Joseph, qui cognominatus est Justus. Hic praedicationis et sanctitatis officio inserviens multamque pro fide Christi persecutionem a Judaeis sustinens, victoriosissimo fine in Judaea quievit. De quo refertur, quod venenum biberit, et nihil ex hoc triste pertulerit, propter Domini fidem. Apud Damascum, sanctorum Sabini, Maximi, Juliani, Macrobii, Cassii, Paulae, cum aliis decem. Civitate Corduba, sancti Pauli diaconi et martyris. [ Bouillart.: . . . . . cum aliis decem. Post Damascenos martyres in Carolino autographo sic : Civitate Corduba sancti Pauli diaconi et martyris. Ipso die sanctae Margaritae virginis. Utraque illa commemoratio nunquam ab Usuardo facta scripto est. Unus duntaxat versus scalpello rasus est ; quod spatium nunquam Paulum et Margaritam continere potuit. Cur ergo Codices omnes, praeter Pratensem, utrumque commemorant ? forte memoriter Margaritam dictavit Usuardus : forte etiam paulo post Usuardum addita est. Nodus inextricabilis, donec ex aliqua Bibliotheca prodeat Codex ipse, qui Carolo regi oblatus fuit. Quod ad sanctum Paulum attinet, difficile dictu cur illum deleverit Usuardus : nam ipsi videntur cordi fuisse sancti ex Hispania, et suum hic Paulus occupasset locum ; si quidem teste domno Eulogio martyre, obierit ille XIII Kal. Augusti, feria 2, an. 851. Quantum conjectura assequi possum, concurrentibus eodem die duobus sanctis, quorum alter longinquae regionis, alter vicinus erat, hunc alteri praeposuit Usuardus. Sancti Vulmari vitam habes parte I tertii saeculi Bened., p. 234. In pago Bononiensi, sancti Vulmari confessoris. Et in margine: Eodem die sanctae Margaritae virginis et martyris.]

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 20 July

  • 277

The birthday of blessed Joseph, who was surnamed Justus. He, serving in the office of preaching and holiness, and sustaining much persecution from the Jews for the faith of Christ, rested in Judaea by a most victorious end. Of whom it is related that he drank poison and suffered nothing harmful from it, on account of his faith in the Lord. At Damascus, of Saints Sabinus, Maximus, Julian, Macrobius, Cassius and Paula, with ten others. In the city of Cordoba, of Saint Paul, deacon and martyr. [Bouillart: . . . . . with ten others. After the martyrs of Damascus the Caroline autograph has thus: In the city of Cordoba, of Saint Paul, deacon and martyr. On this same day, of Saint Margaret, virgin. Neither of those commemorations was ever made in writing by Usuardus. Only one line has been scraped with the knife; and that space could never have held Paul and Margaret. Why, then, do all the codices except the Pratensis commemorate both? Perhaps Usuardus dictated Margaret from memory: perhaps too she was added a little after Usuardus. A knot that cannot be untied, until the very codex which was offered to King Charles comes forth from some library. As for Saint Paul, it is hard to say why Usuardus deleted him: for saints from Spain seem to have been dear to him, and here Paul would have occupied his own place, since, on the testimony of Dom Eulogius, martyr, he died on the thirteenth day before the Kalends of August, on the second feria, in the year 851. As far as I can reach by conjecture, when two saints fell on the same day, of whom one was of a distant region and the other a neighbour, Usuardus put the latter before the other. You have the Life of Saint Vulmarus in part I of the third Benedictine century, p. 234. In the district Bononiensis, of Saint Vulmarus, confessor. And in the margin: On the same day, of Saint Margaret, virgin and martyr.]

XII Kal. Die 21.

  • 281

Danielis prophetae. Romae, sanctae Praxedis virginis. Haec in omni castitate et lege divina erudita, vigiliis et orationibus atque jejuniis assidue vacans, quievit in Christo, sepultaque est juxta suam sororem Potentianam via Salaria. In Galliis civitate Massiliae, natalis sancti Victoris, qui, cum esset miles, et nec militare, nec idolis sacrificare vellet, primo in carcerem trusus, ibique ab angelis visitatus, deinde variis excruciatus poenis, novissime contritus in mola pistoria, martyrium consummavit. Passi sunt cum eo tres milites, Alexander, Felicianus et Longinus. Trecas, sanctae Juliae virginis et martyris.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 21 July

  • 281

Of Daniel the prophet. At Rome, of Saint Praxedes, virgin. She, instructed in all chastity and in the divine law, giving herself constantly to vigils and prayers and fasts, rested in Christ, and was buried beside her sister Potentiana on the Salarian Way. In the Gauls, in the city of Marseille, the birthday of Saint Victor, who, since he was a soldier and would neither serve as a soldier nor sacrifice to idols, was first thrust into prison, and there visited by angels, then tortured with various punishments, and at the last crushed in a baker's mill, consummated his martyrdom. There suffered with him three soldiers, Alexander, Felicianus and Longinus. At Troyes, of Saint Julia, virgin and martyr.

XI Kal. Die 22.

  • 285

Ancira Galaciae, natalis sancti Platonis martyris, cujus gesta habentur. Eodem die, sanctae Mariae Magdalenae et Sinticen beatae, quae Philippis dormit sepulta: de qua apostolus Paulus scribit. In monasterio Fontinella [ Bouillart.: In pago Rothomagensi], depositio sancti Wandregisili confessoris. In territorio Arvernensi, sancti Menelei abbatis.

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 22 July

  • 285

At Ancyra of Galatia, the birthday of Saint Plato, martyr, whose acts are extant. On the same day, of Saint Mary Magdalene, and of blessed Sintice, who sleeps buried at Philippi: of whom the apostle Paul writes. In the monastery Fontinella [Bouillart: In the district of Rouen], the deposition of Saint Wandregisilus, confessor. In the territory Arvernensis, of Saint Meneleus, abbot.

X Kal. Die 23.

  • 289

Natalis beati Apollinaris episcopi, qui ab apostolo Petro Romae ordinatus et Ravennam missus, postea pro fide Christi diversas et multiplices caedes perpessus est. Tandem autem sub Vespasiano Caesare, inter ipsa vicissim succedentia sibi tormenta, gloriosum martyrium consummavit.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 23 July

  • 289

The birthday of blessed Apollinaris, bishop, who, ordained at Rome by the apostle Peter and sent to Ravenna, afterwards suffered for the faith of Christ diverse and manifold beatings. And at length under Vespasian Caesar, amid the very torments succeeding one another by turns, he consummated a glorious martyrdom.

IX Kal. Die 24.

  • 291

Romae via Tiburtina, natalis sancti Vincentii. Apud Emeritam Hispaniae civitatem, sancti Victoris militaris, qui cum duobus fratribus Stercatio et Antinogeno diversis examinatus suppliciis, martyrium consummavit. In Italia civitate Tyro, sanctae Christinae virginis, quae in Christum credens, primo sub duobus judicibus tormenta constanter perferens, novissime per successorem eorum, abscisione linguae et sagittarum confixione, cursum martyrii sui complevit. Eodem die, natalis sanctorum Nicetae et Aquilinae, quae beati Christofori martyris praedicatione ad Christum conversae, martyrii palmam capitis abscisione sumpserunt. Civitate Amiternina, militum octoginta trium. [ Bouillard. add. in margine: Apud Senones sanctorum praesulum Ursicini atque Pavacii.]

The ninth day before the Kalends · 24 July

  • 291

At Rome, on the Tiburtine Way, the birthday of Saint Vincent. At Emerita, a city of Spain, of Saint Victor, a soldier, who with his two brothers Stercatius and Antinogenus, tried by diverse tortures, consummated his martyrdom. In Italy, in the city of Tyre, of Saint Christina, virgin, who, believing in Christ, first under two judges bearing torments steadfastly, at the last through their successor, by the cutting out of her tongue and the piercing of arrows, completed the course of her martyrdom. On the same day, the birthday of Saints Niceta and Aquilina, who, converted to Christ by the preaching of blessed Christopher the martyr, took up the palm of martyrdom by the cutting off of the head. In the city Amiternina, of eighty-three soldiers. [Bouillard adds in the margin: At Senones, of the holy prelates Ursicinus and Pavacius.]

VIII Kal. Die 25.

  • 295

Natalis beati Jacobi apostoli, fratris Joannis evangelistae, qui ab Herode rege decollatus est. Hujus sacratissima ossa ab Hierosolymis ad Hispanias translata, et in ultimis earum finibus condita, celeberrima illarum gentium veneratione excoluntur. In Licia civitate Samon, sancti Christofori, qui virgis ferreis attritus, et a flammis aestuantis incendii, Christi virtute salvatus, ad ultimum sagittarum ictibus confossus, martyrium capitis obtruncatione complevit. Civitate Barcinona, natalis beati Cucufatis martyris, qui pro nomine Domini atrocissima tormentorum genera sub tribus expertus judicibus, novissime gladio victor migravit. Civitate Parisius, translatio sancti Germani episcopi et confessoris.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 25 July

  • 295

The birthday of blessed James the apostle, brother of John the evangelist, who was beheaded by king Herod. His most sacred bones, translated from Jerusalem to the Spains and laid in their farthest borders, are honoured with the most celebrated veneration of those peoples. In Lycia, in the city of Samon, of Saint Christopher, who, worn down with iron rods, and saved by the power of Christ from the flames of a raging fire, at the last pierced through with the strokes of arrows, completed his martyrdom by the cutting off of his head. In the city of Barcelona, the birthday of blessed Cucufas, martyr, who, having experienced under three judges the most atrocious kinds of torments for the name of the Lord, at the last passed away a victor by the sword. In the city of Paris, the translation of Saint Germanus, bishop and confessor.

VII Kal. Die 26.

  • 297

Natalis sancti Herasti, qui Philippis a beato Paulo episcopus relictus, ibidem martyrio coronatus quievit. Romae in portu, sancti Jacincti martyris, qui primo in ignem missus, inde in validissimum torrentem praecipitatus, illaesus evasit: post haec a consulari Leontio gladio percussus, vitam finivit. Via Latina, sanctorum Simpronii, Olimpii, Theodoli, et Exuperiae, qui ignibus conflagrati, ut in gestis sancti Stephani papae legitur, martyrii palmam adepti sunt. [ Addit Bouillart., cursivo charactere: Translatio sancti Marcelli, episcopi et confessoris.]

The seventh day before the Kalends · 26 July

  • 297

The birthday of Saint Herastus, who, left as bishop at Philippi by blessed Paul, having been crowned there with martyrdom, rested. At Rome, at the Port, of Saint Jacinctus, martyr, who, first thrown into the fire, then cast headlong into a most violent torrent, came out unharmed: after this, struck with the sword by the consular Leontius, he ended his life. On the Latin Way, of Saints Simpronius, Olimpius, Theodolus and Exuperia, who, burned up with fires, as is read in the acts of Saint Stephen the pope, obtained the palm of martyrdom. [Bouillart adds, in cursive type: The translation of Saint Marcellus, bishop and confessor.]

VI Kal. Die 27.

  • 301

Apud Ephesum, natalis sanctorum Maximiani, Malchi, Martiani, et aliorum quatuor, quorum gesta habentur. Nicomediae, beatorum martyrum Hermolai presbyteri, Hernempi, et Hermogratis fratrum, qui post multas poenas sibi illatas a Maximiano imperatore, capitali sententia, ob confessionem Christi, puniti sunt. Eodem die in Sicilia, beati Simeonis monachi. Autisiodoro, depositio Etherii episcopi.

The sixth day before the Kalends · 27 July

  • 301

At Ephesus, the birthday of Saints Maximianus, Malchus, Martianus and four others, whose acts are extant. At Nicomedia, of the blessed martyrs Hermolaus, presbyter, Hernempus and Hermogratis, brothers, who, after many punishments inflicted on them by the emperor Maximian, were punished by capital sentence for the confession of Christ. On the same day in Sicily, of blessed Simeon, monk. At Auxerre, the deposition of Etherius, bishop.

V Kal. Die 28.

  • 305

Apud Nicomediam, passio beati Pantaleonis, qui tentus pro fide Christi a Maximiano, equulei poena et lampadum exustione cruciatus, sed inter haec Domino sibi apparente refrigeratus, tandem ictu gladii martyrium consummavit. [ Addit Bouillart. in margine: Eodem die passio sanctorum martyrum Nazarii et Celsi pueri.] In Britannia minori, Dolo monasterio, sancti Samsonis episcopi et confessoris. Eodem die Lugduni, sancti Peregrini presbiteri, cujus beatitudinem miraculorum gloria testatur Nescio quo casu in hunc articulum irrepserint in Dervensi codice Simplicius, Faustinus et Beatrix, quos memorat Pratensis codex in articulo sequente. Nam in isto nulla hic litura est, nec aliud folium. Neque etiam hic bene collocati fuissent. Sic enim Acta apud Monbritium : Tunc beata Beatrix, una cum beatis Crispo et Joanne presbiteris aliisque Christianis levaverunt corpora SS. martyrum Simplicii et Faustini de fluvio (Tiberi, quo, Diocletiano et Maximiano imperatoribus, praecipitati fuerant), et sepelierunt ea ibi in sexto Philippi, IV Kalendas Augustas. Vide etiam Aringhum in Roma subterranea, l. II, c. 20, p. 365 et 366. Si fidem facere possint edita a Monbritio sanctorum illorum Acta, facile esset definire quo anno martyrii laudem adepti sint. Narrant enim Acta illa S. Beatricem, postquam IV Kal. Aug. supremum officium fratribus persolvit, venisse ad matronam quamdam venerabilem nomine Lucinam, moratam esse apud eam per menses septem ; a Lucretio quodam impactam in carcerem, noctu in carcere a perditi illius hominis servis praefocatam, et a Lucina in sexto Philippi, IV Kal. Aug., sepultam fuisse. Anno igitur post suos fratres vita decessit Beatrix. Eodem, ut videtur, anno Lucina, cum poenarum asperitate territa fugeret, admonita fuit a Beatrice non abscederet, in hoc mense datum iri pacem ecclesiis Christi ; hic vero mensis erat Augustus. At mors Maximini, quae contigit mense Augusto anni 312 vel 313, pacem ecclesiis reddidit. Passi sunt igitur Simplicius et Faustinus anno 311 vel 312, Beatrix autem anno 312 vel 313. Tillemontius tom. V, p. 560, suspicatur illud Beatricis vaticinium intelligendum esse de anno 305, quo imperio cessit Diocletianus. At potestne pax appellari, quae cessionem illam consecuta est ? Deinde cessit Diocletianus mense Maio, et nihil in Actis vidi quod mense Maio Lucinam admonitam esse significet. Neque huic conjecturae quidquam officere arbitror prima haec Actorum verba : Temporibus Diocletiani et Maximiani, etc. Nam vaga haec temporis assignandi ratio tam facile de vita amborum aut alterutrius, quam de imperio explicari potest : et id solum si peccarent Acta, nullus dubito quin omnibus probarentur. BOUILLART..

The fifth day before the Kalends · 28 July

  • 305

At Nicomedia, the passion of blessed Pantaleon, who, seized for the faith of Christ by Maximian, tortured by the punishment of the rack and by the burning of torches, but refreshed amid these things by the Lord appearing to him, at length consummated his martyrdom by the stroke of the sword. [Bouillart adds in the margin: On the same day, the passion of the holy martyrs Nazarius and Celsus the boy.] In Lesser Britain, in the monastery of Dolum, of Saint Samson, bishop and confessor. On the same day at Lyon, of Saint Peregrinus, presbyter, whose blessedness the glory of miracles attests By what chance Simplicius, Faustinus and Beatrix crept into this article in the Dervensis codex I do not know; the Pratensis codex records them in the following article. For in the latter there is no erasure here, nor another leaf. Nor would they have been well placed here. For so the Acts in Mombritius: Then blessed Beatrix, together with the blessed presbyters Crispus and John and other Christians, raised the bodies of the holy martyrs Simplicius and Faustinus from the river (the Tiber, into which they had been cast headlong under the emperors Diocletian and Maximian), and buried them there in the sixth of Philip, on the fourth day before the Kalends of August. See also Aringhi in Roma subterranea, book II, ch. 20, pp. 365 and 366. If the Acts of those saints edited by Mombritius could command belief, it would be easy to determine in what year they won the glory of martyrdom. For those Acts relate that Saint Beatrix, after she had paid the last office to her brothers on the fourth day before the Kalends of August, came to a certain venerable matron named Lucina and stayed with her for seven months; that she was thrust into prison by a certain Lucretius, stifled by night in the prison by the slaves of that abandoned man, and buried by Lucina in the sixth of Philip, on the fourth day before the Kalends of August. Beatrix therefore departed this life a year after her brothers. In the same year, as it seems, Lucina, when she was fleeing in terror of the harshness of the punishments, was admonished by Beatrix not to go away, since in this month peace would be given to the churches of Christ; and this month was August. But the death of Maximinus, which happened in the month of August of the year 312 or 313, gave peace back to the churches. Simplicius and Faustinus therefore suffered in the year 311 or 312, and Beatrix in the year 312 or 313. Tillemont, tom. V, p. 560, suspects that that prophecy of Beatrix is to be understood of the year 305, in which Diocletian gave up the empire. But can that be called peace which followed that abdication? Next, Diocletian abdicated in the month of May, and I have seen nothing in the Acts to signify that Lucina was admonished in the month of May. Nor do I think that these first words of the Acts stand at all in the way of this conjecture: In the times of Diocletian and Maximian, etc. For this loose way of assigning the time can as easily be explained of the life of both or of either as of their reign: and if the Acts erred in this alone, I have no doubt that they would be approved by all. Bouillart..

IV Kal. Die 29.

  • 309

Romae via Aurelia, natalis beati Felicis papae, qui a Constantio Augusto, ob catholicae fidei defensionem a sede sua dejectus et gladio necatus, gloriosissime occubuit. Item via Portuensi, sanctorum martyrum Simplicii, Faustini et Beatricis, temporibus Diocletiani, quorum duo post multa ac diversa supplicia, jussi sunt capitalem subire sententiam. Beatrix vero, soror eorum, in Christi confessione praefocata est in carcere. Praeter ista verba, civitate Trecas, tota S. Lupi commemoratio liturae superinducta est ; cujus tamen liturae nec in Dervensi, nec in Sollerianis codicibus, index ullus, ullum vestigium. Quae res Pratensem codicem omni interpolationis suspicione liberat.
