Hinc enim effici ne Sollarius quidem negaverit lituram illam esse quovis alio exemplo antiquiorem, atque adeo ab auctore ipso factam. Fuerint igitur caeterae correctiones eadem scriptae manu ac ista, certum erit correctiones omnes ab ipso auctore scriptas. An vero omnes ejusdem sint manus, oculorum esto judicium. BOUILLART. Civitate Trecas, sancti Lupi confessoris, qui cum beato Germano in Britanniam directus venit. Hic quinquaginta duobus annis sacerdotio venerabiliter functus est.

The fourth day before the Kalends · 29 July

  • 309

At Rome, on the Aurelian Way, the birthday of blessed Felix, pope, who, cast down from his see by Constantius Augustus for the defence of the catholic faith and slain with the sword, fell most gloriously. Also on the Portuensian Way, of the holy martyrs Simplicius, Faustinus and Beatrix, in the times of Diocletian, of whom the two, after many and diverse tortures, were ordered to undergo capital sentence. But Beatrix, their sister, was stifled in prison in the confession of Christ. Apart from these words, 'in the city of Troyes', the whole commemoration of Saint Lupus has been written over an erasure; yet of that erasure there is no sign, no trace, either in the Dervensis or in Sollerius's codices. Which fact frees the Pratensis codex from all suspicion of interpolation. For not even Sollerius would deny that it follows from this that that erasure is older than any other copy, and so was made by the author himself. If, then, the other corrections were written in the same hand as this one, it will be certain that all the corrections were written by the author himself. Whether they are all of the same hand, let the eyes be the judge. Bouillart. In the city of Troyes, of Saint Lupus, confessor, who, sent into Britain with blessed Germanus, came there. He discharged the priesthood venerably for fifty-two years.

III Kal. Die 30.

  • 313

Romae, natalis sanctorum Abdon et Sennes subregulorum, qui cum in Cordula civitate Persarum, vincula paterentur a Decio, ad ultimum duxit eos Romam catenis obligatos et diversis poenis maceratos, ubi primo plumbatis caesi, et deinde gladio interfecti sunt. Apud Africam civitate Lucernaria, sanctarum virginum Maximae, Donatillae et Secundae, quae persecutione Galieni, aceto et felle primo potatae, deinde plagis acerrimis caesae, et equulei extensione cruciatae, postmodum etiam craticulis exustae, necnon ad bestias projectae, sed ab his intactae, novissime gladio jugulatae sunt.

The third day before the Kalends · 30 July

  • 313

At Rome, the birthday of Saints Abdon and Sennes, under-kings, who, when in Cordula, a city of the Persians, they were suffering chains from Decius, at the last he brought them to Rome bound with chains and wasted by diverse punishments, where they were first beaten with leaded scourges, and then slain with the sword. At Africa, in the city of Lucernaria, of the holy virgins Maxima, Donatilla and Secunda, who, in the persecution of Gallienus, first given vinegar and gall to drink, then beaten with most bitter blows, and tortured by stretching on the rack, afterwards also burned on gridirons, and moreover thrown to the beasts, but untouched by them, at the last had their throats cut with the sword.

Pridie Kal. Die 31.

  • 315

Apud Caesaream, passio Fabii martyris, qui cum ferre vexilla praesidialia recusaret, primum diebus aliquot in carcerem trusus, deinde interrogatus semel et iterum, in Christi confessione perdurat: et mox a judice capitali sententia condemnatur. Ravennae, transitus sancti Germani Autisiodorensis episcopi, genere, fide et doctrina, atque miraculorum gloria clarissimi. Civitate Sinnada, sanctorum Democriti, Secundi et Dionisii.

The day before the Kalends · 31 July

  • 315

At Caesarea, the passion of Fabius, martyr, who, since he refused to bear the governor's standards, was first thrust into prison for some days, then, questioned once and again, he persists in the confession of Christ: and forthwith he is condemned by the judge by capital sentence. At Ravenna, the passing of Saint Germanus, bishop of Auxerre, most renowned in birth, faith and doctrine, and in the glory of miracles. In the city of Sinnada, of Saints Democritus, Secundus and Dionisius.

MENSIS AUGUSTUS. HABET DIES XXXI.

  • 319

AUGUST HAS 31 DAYS.

  • 319

Kalendis. Die 1.

Antiochiae, natalis sanctorum Machabaeorum. Romae, ad sanctum Petrum ad vincula. Apud Italiam civitate Vercelli, beati Eusebii episcopi, qui ob confessionem fidei a Constantio Scitopolim, et inde Cappadociam relegatus, post ad ecclesiam suam reversus, novissime persequentibus Arianis, martyrium passus est. Item Romae, passio sanctarum virginum Spei, Fidei et Charitatis et matris earum Sapientiae: quae sub Adriano principe martyrii coronam adeptae sunt. Via Latina, sanctorum martyrum Boni presbyteri, Fausti, Mauri, cum aliis septem, qui in Stephani papae Actibus describuntur. In Hispaniis civitate Gerunda, natalis sancti Felicis, quem jussit judex Dacianus, post illata tormentorum genera, ungulis detrahi, ac deinde usque ad ossa laniari, et tamdiu vulnera vulneribus instaurari, usque dum invictum Christo spiritum reddidit. Apud Arabiam, civitate Philadelphia, sanctorum martyrum Cirilli, Aquilae, Petri, Domiciani, Rufi, Menandri, una die coronatorum. In territorio Parisiensi, Justini martyris. In pago Lisuino, sancti Nemesii confessoris.

On the Kalends · 1 August

At Antioch, the birthday of the holy Maccabees. At Rome, at Saint Peter at the Chains. At Italy, in the city of Vercelli, of blessed Eusebius, bishop, who, for the confession of the faith banished by Constantius to Scitopolis, and thence to Cappadocia, afterwards returned to his church, and at the last, the Arians persecuting him, suffered martyrdom. Also at Rome, the passion of the holy virgins Spes, Fides and Charitas, and of their mother Sapientia: who under the emperor Hadrian obtained the crown of martyrdom. On the Latin Way, of the holy martyrs Bonus, presbyter, Faustus and Maurus, with seven others, who are described in the Acts of Stephen the pope. In the Spains, in the city of Gerona, the birthday of Saint Felix, whom the judge Dacianus, after inflicting kinds of torments, ordered to be scraped with hooks, and then to be torn to the bones, and wounds to be renewed upon wounds so long, until he gave back to Christ his unconquered spirit. At Arabia, in the city of Philadelphia, of the holy martyrs Cirillus, Aquila, Peter, Domicianus, Rufus and Menander, crowned on one day. In the territory of Paris, of Justinus, martyr. In the district Lisuinus, of Saint Nemesius, confessor.

IV Non. Die 2.

Romae in cimiterio Calixti, natalis beati Stephani papae, qui persecutione Valeriani, cum insequeretur ob Christi martyrium, et staret intrepidus ante altaris solemnia, ac jugiter coepta perficeret, in eodem loco, sicut in sua decollatur sede. In provincia Bithinia, urbe Niceae, sanctae Theodotae cum tribus filiis suis, e quibus primogenitum nomine Evodium, Christum fiducialiter confitentem, fecit primum consularis Bithiniae fustibus caedi deinde ipsam cum omnibus filiis igne consumi.

The fourth day before the Nones · 2 August

At Rome, in the cemetery of Calixtus, the birthday of blessed Stephen, pope, who, in the persecution of Valerian, when he was being pursued for the martyrdom of Christ, and was standing undaunted before the solemnities of the altar and continuing steadily with what he had begun, is beheaded in that same place, as it were in his own see. In the province of Bithynia, in the city of Nicaea, of Saint Theodota with her three sons, of whom the consular of Bithynia had the first-born, named Evodius, confessing Christ confidently, first beaten with cudgels, then herself with all her sons consumed by fire.

III Non. Die 3.

Hierosolymis, inventio corporis beatissimi Stephani protomartyris, et sanctorum Gamalielis, Nicomedi et Abibon. Haec septimo Honorii principis anno revelata, atque a beato presbytero Luciano scripta, ad totius Ecclesiae personam, omnibus fere gentibus declaratur. Constantinopoli, Hermelli martyris.

The third day before the Nones · 3 August

At Jerusalem, the finding of the body of the most blessed Stephen the protomartyr, and of Saints Gamaliel, Nicomedi and Abibon. This, revealed in the seventh year of the emperor Honorius, and written up by the blessed presbyter Lucian, is made known, in the person of the whole Church, to almost all nations. At Constantinople, of Hermellus, martyr.

Pridie Non. Die 4.

  • 329

Natalis beati Aristarchi, discipuli sancti Pauli apostoli. Romae via Latina, passio sancti Tertullini martyris, qui sub Aureliano imperatore, post impiam fustium mactationem et ignium circa latera exustionem, ac oris conquassationem, atque equulei extensionem, nervorumque caesionem, data sententia, capitis amputatione martyrium consummavit.

The day before the Nones · 4 August

  • 329

The birthday of blessed Aristarchus, disciple of Saint Paul the apostle. At Rome, on the Latin Way, the passion of Saint Tertullinus, martyr, who, under the emperor Aurelian, after the impious slaughter of cudgels, and the burning of fires about his sides, and the battering of his mouth, and the stretching of the rack, and the cutting of the sinews, sentence being given, consummated his martyrdom by the cutting off of his head.

Nonis. Die 5.

  • 333A

Apud provinciam Retiae civitate Augustana, natalis sanctae Afrae, quae cum esset pagana et meretrix, per doctrinam sancti Narcissi episcopi ad Christum conversa, et cum omni domo sua baptizata, pro confessione Domini igni tradita est. Eodem die, sancti Oswaldi regis Anglorum, cujus Actus commemorat Venerabilis Beda presbyter in gestis ejusdem gentis. Augustudini, beati Cassiani episcopi et confessoris. Catalaunis, sancti Memmii episcopi.

On the Nones · 5 August

  • 333A

At the province of Raetia, in the city Augustana, the birthday of Saint Afra, who, being a pagan and a harlot, converted to Christ through the teaching of Saint Narcissus, bishop, and baptized with all her house, was given over to the fire for the confession of the Lord. On the same day, of Saint Oswald, king of the English, whose Acts the Venerable Bede, presbyter, records in the history of that same people. At Augustudunum, of blessed Cassianus, bishop and confessor. At Catalauni, of Saint Memmius, bishop.

VIII Idus. Die 6.

  • 335

[In monte Thabor transfiguratio Domini Jesu, Bouillart. in margine. ] Romae via Appia, natalis beati Xysti episcopi et martyris. Et in coemeterio Praetextati, sanctorum Felicissimi, et Agapiti diaconorum ejusdem, qui cum eo sub Decio pariter decollati sunt. Decollati sunt cum eo et alii quatuor, id est Januarius, Magnus, Vincentius, et Stephanus, subdiaconi, ut in gestis pontificalibus legitur. Passus est cum eis beatus Quartus, ut scribit sanctus Cyprianus. In Hispaniis civitate Compluto, Justi et Pastoris fratrum, qui cum adhuc 337pueri, litteris imbuerentur, projectis in schola tabulis, sponte ad martyrium concurrerunt. Et mox a Daciano teneri jussi, cum se mutuis exhortationibus constantissime roborarent, extra civitatem producti, et a carnificibus jugulati sunt.

The eighth day before the Ides · 6 August

  • 335

[On Mount Tabor, the transfiguration of the Lord Jesus, Bouillart in the margin.] At Rome, on the Appian Way, the birthday of blessed Xystus, bishop and martyr. And in the cemetery of Praetextatus, of Saints Felicissimus and Agapitus, his deacons, who were beheaded together with him under Decius. There were beheaded with him also four others, that is, Januarius, Magnus, Vincent and Stephen, subdeacons, as is read in the pontifical acts. There suffered with them blessed Quartus, as Saint Cyprian writes. In the Spains, in the city of Complutum, of Justus and Pastor, brothers, who, while they were still 337boys being instructed in letters, threw away their tablets in the school and ran of their own accord to martyrdom. And forthwith ordered by Dacianus to be seized, when they strengthened one another most steadfastly with mutual exhortations, they were led out beyond the city, and had their throats cut by the executioners.

VII Idus. Die 7.

  • 339

Apud Tusciam civitate Aretio, natalis beati Donati episcopi et martyris, sub Juliano imperatore. Qui, ut Gregorius papa in libris Dialogorum meminit, fractum a paganis calicem sanctum ad missam morando restauravit. Romae, sanctorum martyrum Petri, Juliani cum aliis decem et octo. Apud Mediolanum, sancti Faustini, tempore Aurelii Commodi martyrizati

The seventh day before the Ides · 7 August

  • 339

At Tuscia, in the city of Aretium, the birthday of blessed Donatus, bishop and martyr, under the emperor Julian. Who, as Gregory the pope records in the books of the Dialogues, restored, by lingering at mass, the holy chalice broken by the pagans. At Rome, of the holy martyrs Peter and Julian, with eighteen others. At Milan, of Saint Faustinus, martyred in the time of Aurelius Commodus

VI Idus. Die 8.

  • 343

Romae via Ostiensi, natalis beati Ciriaci diaconi et sanctorum martyrum Largi et Smaragdi cum aliis viginti, tempore Diocletiani imperatoris. Locus ubi nunc legitur sancti Severi commemoratio, erasus olim fuit. Item articulus sequens a capite ad calcem totus est scalpello laceratus, tacente Sollerio et correctionem admittente. Quinam olim fuerunt sancti his locis memorati ? quo abierunt, R. P., tui illi antiqui codices, unde sumendam putas primigeniam Usuardi nativamque simplicitatem ? Illos in medium profer, ut soles, ostenta. Nihilne fraudis hic et imposturae latet ? nonne Usuardi symmista aliquis exstitit, qui suum stylum ad Usuardinum accommodandum esse censuerit ? Sed, amoto ludo, fatere tandem lituras et emendationes nihil quidquam nocere Usuardini textus puritati, cum, quae totum codicem descripsit, eadem correxit manus. Apud Viennam Galliae urbem, sancti Severi presbyteri et confessoris, qui, ut ejus gesta testantur, causa Evangelii praedicandi, laboriosam peregrinationem suscepit, et ad praefatam urbem deveniens, ingentem paganorum multitudinem verbo et miraculis ad fidem Christi convertit.

The sixth day before the Ides · 8 August

  • 343

At Rome, on the Ostian Way, the birthday of blessed Ciriacus, deacon, and of the holy martyrs Largus and Smaragdus, with twenty others, in the time of the emperor Diocletian. The place where the commemoration of Saint Severus is now read was once erased. Likewise the following article is torn with the knife from head to foot, Sollerius saying nothing and admitting the correction. Which saints, pray, were once recorded in these places? Where have they gone, Reverend Father, those old codices of yours, from which you think the original and native simplicity of Usuardus is to be drawn? Bring them forward, display them, as you are wont. Is there no fraud and imposture lurking here? Was there not some fellow initiate (symmistes) of Usuardus, who thought his own style must be tempered to Usuardus's? But, jest aside, confess at last that erasures and emendations do no harm whatever to the purity of Usuardus's text, since the same hand that copied out the whole codex corrected it. At Vienne, a city of Gaul, of Saint Severus, presbyter and confessor, who, as his acts testify, for the sake of preaching the Gospel undertook a laborious pilgrimage, and, coming to the aforesaid city, converted a huge multitude of pagans to the faith of Christ by word and miracles.

V Idus. Die 9.

  • 345

Vigilia sancti Laurentii martyris. Apud Coloniam Tusciae, natalis sanctorum Secundiani, Marcelliani, et Veriani, qui tempore Decii, sub consulari Promoto primum caesi sunt, deinde in equuleo suspensi, et ungulis abrasi, atque igne lateribus apposito assati, triumphalem martyrii palmam capite caesi meruerunt. Romae, sancti Romani militis, qui confessione beati Laurentii compunctus, petiit ab eo baptizari, et mox jubente Decio, cum fustibus exhibitus ac decollatus est.

The fifth day before the Ides · 9 August

  • 345

The vigil of Saint Laurence, martyr. At Colonia of Tuscia, the birthday of Saints Secundianus, Marcellianus and Verianus, who, in the time of Decius, under the consular Promotus, were first beaten, then hung upon the rack, and scraped with hooks, and roasted with fire applied to their sides, and, beheaded, merited the triumphal palm of martyrdom. At Rome, of Saint Romanus, a soldier, who, pricked to the heart by the confession of blessed Laurence, asked to be baptized by him, and forthwith, at the command of Decius, was brought forth with cudgels and beheaded.

IV Idus. Die 10.

  • 349

Romae via Tiburtina, natalis beati Laurentii archidiaconi, qui praesente Decio imperatore, post plurima tormenta carceris, verberum diversorum, laminarum ardentium, ad ultimum in craticula ferrea assatus, martyrium complevit. Eodem die Romae, militum centum sexaginta quinque.

The fourth day before the Ides · 10 August

  • 349

At Rome, on the Tiburtine Way, the birthday of blessed Laurence, archdeacon, who, in the presence of the emperor Decius, after very many torments of prison, of diverse scourgings, of burning plates, at the last roasted on an iron gridiron, completed his martyrdom. On the same day at Rome, of one hundred and sixty-five soldiers.

III Idus. Die 11.

  • 351

Romae inter Duas Lauros, natalis sancti Tiburtii, qui sub judice Fabiano, cum nudis plantis super carbones ardentes ingressus, Christum majori confiteretur constantia, duci in tertium ab urbe milliarium atque ibidem gladio animadverti jubetur. Item Romae, sanctae Susannae virginis, quae cum esset nobili prosapia orta et beati Gaii pontificis neptis, martyrii palmam tempore Diocletiani, capitis obtruncatione promeruit. In civitate Camberaco, sancti Gaugerici episcopi et confessoris. Apud castellum Ebroas, sancti Taurini episcopi.

The third day before the Ides · 11 August

  • 351

At Rome, between the Two Laurels, the birthday of Saint Tiburtius, who, under the judge Fabianus, when he had walked with bare soles over burning coals and was confessing Christ with the greater constancy, is ordered to be led to the third milestone from the city and there to be put to the sword. Also at Rome, of Saint Susanna, virgin, who, since she was sprung of noble lineage and was the niece of blessed Gaius the pontiff, merited the palm of martyrdom in the time of Diocletian, by the cutting off of her head. In the city of Camberacum, of Saint Gaugericus, bishop and confessor. At the fort Ebroas, of Saint Taurinus, bishop.

Pridie Idus. Die 12.

  • 353

In Sicilia civitate Cathina, natalis sancti Eupli diaconi, sub Diocletiano et Maximiano Augustis, qui cum diutissime pro confessione Domini torqueretur, tandem martyrii palmam gladio caedente percepit. Apud Augustanam urbem, sanctae Hilariae matris sanctae Afrae martyris, quae cum ad sepulcrum illius excubaret, 355ibidem pro fide Christi a persecutoribus igni tradita est, cum Digna et Eumenia et Euprepia. Passi sunt item eodem die in praefata civitate Quiriacus [ Bouillart.: Pro eodem die passi sunt Romae sub praefecto Pertinace, quae suo codici Usuardus addidit, in Carolino autographo sic erat : Passi sunt item eodem die in praefata urbe. Correctionem ab Usuardo factam esse nemo sanorum oculorum dubitaverit. At unde didicerit eodem die passos illos esse martyres, praefectum Urbis tunc fuisse Pertinacem ; Nimmiam et Julianam aliosque viginti socios Quiriaco, Largioni et Crescentiano adjungendos esse ; omnes denique illos martyres die XII Augusti in coelos migrasse, plane nescio. Nihil horum videre memini in Actis B. Marcelli apud Monbritium, nec uspiam alibi. Sed haec omnia argumento sunt Usuardum ex Adone aliisque Martyrologiis sumpsisse quae pro suis uteretur, cum nihil melius habuit ; multaque legisse, quae nunquam illi legerant. Utinam quae sanctorum illorum martyrum Acta vidit, ea nunc e tenebris prodirent. Nam in iis quae sunt edita, quot versus totidem fere fabulae. BOUILLART. eodem die passi sunt Romae sub praefecto Pertinace Quiriacus, etc.], Largio, Crescentianus, Nimmia, Juliana cum aliis viginti. Item urbe Falari, passio Graciliani et Felicissimae virginis, quorum ora primum pro Christo lapidibus contusa, dehinc gladio percussi, optatam martyrii suscepere coronam. In Siria, sanctorum Macharii et Juliani.

The day before the Ides · 12 August

  • 353

In Sicily, in the city of Cathina, the birthday of Saint Euplus, deacon, under Diocletian and Maximian Augusti, who, when he had been tortured a very long time for the confession of the Lord, at length received the palm of martyrdom by the sword striking. At the city Augustana, of Saint Hilaria, mother of Saint Afra the martyr, who, while she was keeping watch at her tomb, 355was given over to the fire there by the persecutors for the faith of Christ, with Digna and Eumenia and Euprepia. There suffered also on the same day in the aforesaid city Quiriacus [Bouillart: In place of on the same day there suffered at Rome under the prefect Pertinax, which Usuardus added in his own codex, the Caroline autograph had thus: There suffered also on the same day in the aforesaid city. No one with sound eyes would doubt that the correction was made by Usuardus. But whence he learned that those martyrs suffered on the same day, that Pertinax was then prefect of the City; that Nimmia and Juliana and twenty other companions are to be joined to Quiriacus, Largio and Crescentianus; and lastly that all those martyrs passed to heaven on the 12th day of August, I plainly do not know. I do not remember seeing any of these things in the Acts of blessed Marcellus in Mombritius, nor anywhere else. But all these things are proof that Usuardus took from Ado and other Martyrologies what he might use as his own, when he had nothing better; and that he read many things which they had never read. Would that the Acts of those holy martyrs which he saw might now come forth from the darkness. For in those which are published there are almost as many fables as lines. Bouillart. on the same day there suffered at Rome under the prefect Pertinax Quiriacus, etc.], Largio, Crescentianus, Nimmia and Juliana, with twenty others. Also in the city of Falari, the passion of Gracilianus and of Felicissima, virgin, whose mouths were first bruised with stones for Christ, and who then, struck with the sword, received the longed-for crown of martyrdom. In Syria, of Saints Macharius and Julian.

Idibus. Die 13.

  • 357

Romae, beati Ypoliti martyris, qui pro confessionis gloria, jubente Valeriano praeside, ligatus pedes ad colla indomitorum equorum, sic per cardetum et tribulos tractus emisit spiritum. Passa est etiam Concordia nutrix ejus, quae ante ipsum plumbatis caesa martyrizavit: et alii decem et novem de domo ejus, qui simul decollati sunt. Eodem die Forosillae, natalis sancti Cassiani, qui cum adorare noluisset, vocatis pueris, quibus docendo exosus fuerat, data est facultas eum perimendi, quorum quanto infirmior erat manus, tanto graviorem martyrii poenam dilata morte faciebat. Aequali quidem, sed certe non eadem manu addita est huic articulo sancta Radegundis ; quam tamen ut ab Usuardo scriptam admittit Sollerius, nulla interim pristinae liturae facta mentione, quam dissimulat, quoties nihil in suis codicibus occurrit quo Pratensi codici actionem interpolationis intendere valeat. Nec ullum hujus liturae in Dervensi codice, vestigium quamvis omnino duo versus erasi fuerint. Fuerunt igitur erasi ante ullum aliud exemplum. Ergo ab auctore. BOUILLART. Pictavis, sanctae Radegundis reginae, cujus vita virtutibus et miraculis late claruit.

On the Ides · 13 August

  • 357

At Rome, of blessed Ypolitus, martyr, who, for the glory of confession, at the command of the governor Valerian, bound by the feet to the necks of untamed horses, dragged thus through thistles and thorns, gave up the spirit. There suffered also Concordia his nurse, who, beaten with leaded scourges before him, was martyred: and nineteen others of his household, who were beheaded together. On the same day at Forosillae, the birthday of Saint Cassianus, who, when he had refused to worship, the boys were summoned, to whom he had become hateful by teaching, and the power of killing him was given them; and the weaker their hand was, the heavier they made the punishment of his martyrdom, death being delayed. Saint Radegundis was added to this article by a contemporary hand indeed, but certainly not the same; yet Sollerius admits her as written by Usuardus, making no mention meanwhile of the former erasure, which he keeps quiet whenever nothing occurs in his codices by which he might bring a charge of interpolation against the Pratensis codex. Nor is there any trace of this erasure in the Dervensis codex, although two whole lines were erased. They were erased, therefore, before any other copy. Therefore by the author. Bouillart. At Poitiers, of Saint Radegundis, queen, whose life shone far and wide with virtues and miracles.

XIX Kal. Die 14.

  • 361

Vigilia Assumptionis sanctae Mariae. Romae via Appia, natalis beati Eusebii presbyteri et confessoris, qui a Constantio Augusto, ob Catholicae fidei defensionem, in quodam cubiculo domus suae inclusus, ibique in oratione constanter perseverans, dormitionem accepit.

The nineteenth day before the Kalends · 14 August

  • 361

The vigil of the Assumption of Saint Mary. At Rome, on the Appian Way, the birthday of blessed Eusebius, presbyter and confessor, who, for the defence of the Catholic faith, was shut up by Constantius Augustus in a certain chamber of his own house, and there, persevering steadfastly in prayer, received the falling-asleep.

XVIII Kal. Die 15.

  • 365

Dormitio sanctae Dei genitricis Mariae, cujus sacratissimum corpus etsi non invenitur super terram, tamen pia mater Ecclesia venerabilem ejus memoriam sic festivam agit, ut pro conditione carnis, eam migrasse non dubitet. Quo autem illud venerabile Spiritus sancti templum nutu, et consilio divino occultatum sit, plus elegit sobrietas Ecclesiae cum pietate nescire, quam aliquid frivolum et apocriphum, inde tenendo docere. Romae via Appia, sancti Tharsicii acoliti et martyris, qui cum deferret quodam die Christi corporis Sacramenta, et nec vellet ea discutientibus prodere paganis, tam diu est fustibus et lapidibus maceratus ab eis, quousque exhalaret spiritum.

The eighteenth day before the Kalends · 15 August

  • 365

The falling-asleep of Saint Mary, the Mother of God, whose most sacred body, although it is not found upon the earth, yet holy mother Church so keeps her venerable memory as a feast, that she does not doubt that she passed away according to the condition of the flesh. But where that venerable temple of the Holy Spirit has been hidden by the divine will and counsel, the sobriety of the Church has chosen rather, with piety, not to know, than to teach anything frivolous and apocryphal by holding it from that source. At Rome, on the Appian Way, of Saint Tharsicius, acolyte and martyr, who, when on a certain day he was carrying the Sacraments of the body of Christ, and would not betray them to the pagans who were searching him, was so long wasted by them with cudgels and stones, until he breathed out his spirit.

XVII Kal. Die 16.

  • 367

In Nicea Bithiniae, natalis beati Ursacii confessoris, qui sub Licinio persecutore, militia relicta, solitariam vitam agens, tantis claruit virtutibus, ut et daemones expulisse, et ingentem draconem orando interemisse legatur. Apud civitatem Metensium, sancti Arnulfi episcopi, qui sanctitate vitae et miraculorum gratia illustris, heremiticam vitam diligens, beato fine quievit. Romae, sanctae Serenae uxoris quondam Diocletiani Augusti.

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 16 August

  • 367

In Nicaea of Bithynia, the birthday of blessed Ursacius, confessor, who, under the persecutor Licinius, having abandoned military service and leading a solitary life, shone with such virtues that he is read to have both expelled demons and slain a huge dragon by praying. At the city of the Metenses, of Saint Arnulfus, bishop, who, illustrious for holiness of life and the grace of miracles, loving the hermit's life, rested by a blessed end. At Rome, of Saint Serena, once the wife of Diocletian Augustus.

XVI Kal. Die 17.

  • 371

Apud Africam, sanctorum martyrum Liberati abbatis, Bonifacii diaconi, Servii et Rustici subdiaconorum, Rogati et Septimi monachorum, et Maximi pueri; qui persecutione Wandalica jussu Hunerici regis, pro confessione catholicae fidei et unici baptismatis defensione, speciosum cursum certaminis sui, coronante Domino, perfecerunt. In Caesarea Cappadociae, natalis sancti Mammetis martyris, qui passus est imperante Aureliano sub Alexandro praeside. Ipso die Octavae sancti Laurentii martyris.

The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 17 August

  • 371

At Africa, of the holy martyrs Liberatus, abbot, Boniface, deacon, Servius and Rusticus, subdeacons, Rogatus and Septimus, monks, and Maximus, a boy; who, in the Vandal persecution, by order of king Hunericus, for the confession of the catholic faith and the defence of the one baptism, finished the splendid course of their contest, the Lord crowning them. In Caesarea of Cappadocia, the birthday of Saint Mammes, martyr, who suffered while Aurelian was emperor, under the governor Alexander. On this same day, the Octave of Saint Laurence, martyr.

XV Kal. Die 18.

  • 373

Apud Prenestinam civitatem, miliario ab Urbe tricesimo tertio, natalis sancti Agapiti martyris, qui cum esset annorum quindecim, et amore Christi martyrio ferveret, jussu Aurelii imperatoris tentus est, ac primo nervis crudis diutissime caesus: deinde a praeside Antiocho gravia patitur supplicia. Exinde cum ex praecepto imperatoris leonibus subjiceretur, et minime esset laesus, gladio ministrorum coronandus percutitur. Eodem die Romae, beatorum presbiterorum Joannis et Crispi, qui persecutione Diocletiani multa sanctorum corpora officiosissime sepelierunt, quorum meritis et ipsi postmodum sociati, gaudia vitae meruerunt. Via Lavicana, sanctae Helenae matris Constantini imperatoris.

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 18 August

  • 373

At the city of Praeneste, at the thirty-third milestone from the City, the birthday of Saint Agapitus, martyr, who, when he was fifteen years old and burned for martyrdom with the love of Christ, was seized by order of the emperor Aurelius, and first beaten a very long time with raw sinews: then from the governor Antiochus he suffers grievous tortures. Thereupon, when by the command of the emperor he was being exposed to lions, and was not harmed at all, he is struck with the sword of the attendants, to be crowned. On the same day at Rome, of the blessed presbyters John and Crispus, who in the persecution of Diocletian buried very many bodies of the saints most dutifully, and who afterwards, themselves also joined to their merits, merited the joys of life. On the Lavican Way, of Saint Helena, mother of the emperor Constantine.

XIV Kal. Die 19.

  • 377

Natalis sancti Magni, seu sancti Andreae martyris, cum sociis suis duobus milibus quingentis nonaginta et septem. In pago Sigisterico, beati Donati presbiteri, qui ab ipsis infantiae rudimentis mira Dei gratia praeditus, anachoriticam vitam multis annis exegit et miraculorum gloria clarus migravit ad Christum. Romae, sancti Julii Senatoris et martyris, qui jubente Commodo imperatore, tandiu fustibus est caesus, donec emitteret spiritum. In territorio Bituricensi, sancti Mariani confessoris.

The fourteenth day before the Kalends · 19 August

  • 377

The birthday of Saint Magnus, or of Saint Andrew, martyr, with his companions, two thousand five hundred and ninety-seven. In the district Sigistericus, of blessed Donatus, presbyter, who, endowed with the wonderful grace of God from the very rudiments of infancy, led the anchoritic life for many years, and, renowned for the glory of miracles, passed to Christ. At Rome, of Saint Julius, senator and martyr, who, at the command of the emperor Commodus, was beaten with cudgels so long, until he gave up the spirit. In the territory of Bourges, of Saint Marianus, confessor.

XIII Kal. Die 20.

  • 379

Samuelis prophetae, cujus sacra ossa, ut Hieronimus scribit, Augustus Archadius de Judea transtulit Traciam. Eodem die, beati Porphirii hominis Dei, qui sanctum martyrem Agapitum erudivit in fide et doctrina Christi. Herio insula, sancti Philiberti abbatis, qui post militiam temporalem, Christi tyrocinio mancipatus, monasteriorum exstitit fundator devotus. Civitate Corduba, sanctorum monachorum Leovigildi et Christofori, quorum corpora post abscisionem capitis, igni tradita sunt

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 20 August

  • 379

Of Samuel the prophet, whose sacred bones, as Jerome writes, Arcadius Augustus translated from Judaea to Thrace. On the same day, of blessed Porphirius, man of God, who instructed the holy martyr Agapitus in the faith and doctrine of Christ. On the island Herio, of Saint Philibertus, abbot, who, after temporal military service, bound over to the novitiate of Christ, became a devoted founder of monasteries. In the city of Cordoba, of the holy monks Leovigildus and Christopher, whose bodies, after the cutting off of the head, were given over to the fire

XII Kal. Die 21.

  • 383

In territorio civitatis Gavalitanae, passio sancti Privati episcopi et martyris, qui passus est persecutione Valeriani et Galieni. Eodem die, natalis sanctorum martyrum Bonosi et Maximiani, quorum gesta habentur. Et in civitate Salona, sancti Anastasii martyris, qui pro confessione nominis Christi, jubente Aurelio imperatore, interemptus est.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 21 August

  • 383

In the territory of the city Gavalitana, the passion of Saint Privatus, bishop and martyr, who suffered in the persecution of Valerian and Gallienus. On the same day, the birthday of the holy martyrs Bonosus and Maximianus, whose acts are extant. And in the city of Salona, of Saint Anastasius, martyr, who, for the confession of the name of Christ, was slain at the command of the emperor Aurelius.

XI Kal. Die 22.

  • 385

Romae via Ostiensi, natalis beati Timothei martyris, qui tentus ab Urbis praefecto, et longa carceris custodia maceratus, cum sacrificare idolis noluisset, tertio caesus et gravissimis suppliciis attrectatus, ad ultimum decollatus est. Eodem die Augustuduni, sancti Simphoriani, tempore Aurelii imperatoris, qui cum idolis sacrificare nollet, primo verberibus affectus, deinde mancipatus, ad ultimum caeso capite martyrium consummavit. In portu Romano, sanctorum peregrinorum martyrum Martialis, Epicteti, Saturnini, Aprilis et Felicis cum sociis eorum. Item Romae, sancti Antonii martyris, qui, cum se Christianum libere fateretur, jubente Vitellio judice, capitali sententia est damnatus, viaque Aurelia sepultus.

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 22 August

  • 385

At Rome, on the Ostian Way, the birthday of blessed Timotheus, martyr, who, seized by the prefect of the City, and wasted by a long custody in prison, when he had refused to sacrifice to idols, was beaten a third time and handled with most grievous tortures, and at the last was beheaded. On the same day at Augustudunum, of Saint Simphorianus, in the time of the emperor Aurelius, who, since he would not sacrifice to idols, was first afflicted with blows, then handed over, and at the last, his head cut off, consummated his martyrdom. At the Roman Port, of the holy pilgrim martyrs Martialis, Epictetus, Saturninus, Aprilis and Felix, with their companions. Also at Rome, of Saint Antonius, martyr, who, since he confessed himself a Christian freely, at the command of the judge Vitellius was condemned by capital sentence, and was buried on the Aurelian Way.

X Kal. Die 23.

  • 389

Natalis sanctorum martyrum Donati, Restituti, Valeriani, Fructuosae cum aliis duodecim, qui praeclarissimo confessionis honore apud Antiochiam coronati sunt. Eodem die, beati Zachei episcopi, qui quartus a Jacobo, Hierosolimorum ecclesiam clarissime rexit. Apud Liciam civitate Egea, sanctorum Claudii, Asterii et Neonis, qui post acerba tormenta cruci affixi sunt. In portu urbis Romae, sanctorum Ypoliti, Quiriaci et Archillai. Alexandriae, sancti Theonae episcopi venerabilis et vere Deo digni. [ Bouillart. alio ordine, paucis additis: . . . . . coronati sunt, apud Liciam civitate Egea, sanctorum martyrum Claudii, Asterii, Neonis, Multum differt Carolinum autographum. Zacheus qui quarto loco hic, in Dervensi codice ponitur secundo. Claudius ejusque socii, qui secundam in Pratensi, tertiam in Dervensi sedem occupant. Denique sanctus ille Claudius, qui quatuor in Pratensi socios habet, nonnisi duos habet in Dervensi, nempe Asterium et Neonem. Atque ut haec omnia corrigeret Usuardus, in suo codice totos quinque versus oblitteravit. Non dicam eamdem esse manum, id centies iterasse sat est. Videamus quibus causis ad corrigendum adductus fuerit Usuardus. De ordine quo sanctos illos composuit, Sollerius, opinor, auctorem in jus non vocabit. Res est nihili, in qua defendenda religioni fuerit immorari. Neque etiam inficiabitur Pratensis codicis censor, Claudio, Asterio et Neoni recte adjectas fuisse Domninam et Theonillam. Habemus eorum acta proconsularia integra atque germana, ubi quinque illi martyres nominatim ad confitendam fidem vocantur et suppliciis cruciantur ; et in fine, habita est passio haec in civitate Aegea, sub Lysia praeside, X Kal. Sept. Augusto et Aristobolo consulibus. In Carolino autographo non fuerunt haec verba, qui post acerba tormenta cruci affixi sunt, quae tamen Sollerius ut Usuardina suo articulo inseruit. Tres martyres Claudius, Asterius, Neon in Dervensi codice nullo addito nominantur. BOUILLART. Domninae et Theonillae, qui sub Lysia praeside variis poenis excruciati atque interempti sunt. In portu urbis Romae sancti Yppoliti, Quiriaci, et Archillai. Eodem die, beati Zachei episcopi, qui quartus ab Jacobo Jerosolymorum ecclesiam clarissime rexit. Alexandriae, sancti Theonae episcopi venerabilis et vere Deo digni.] Item natalis sanctorum Timothei et Apollinaris, qui apud Remensium urbem consummato martyrio coelestia regna meruerunt. Lugduno Galliae, sanctorum martyrum Minervii et Eleazarii cum filiis octo.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 23 August

  • 389

The birthday of the holy martyrs Donatus, Restitutus, Valerianus and Fructuosa, with twelve others, who were crowned at Antioch with the most illustrious honour of confession. On the same day, of blessed Zacheus, bishop, who, fourth from James, ruled the church of Jerusalem most illustriously. At Lycia, in the city of Egea, of Saints Claudius, Asterius and Neon, who after bitter torments were fastened to the cross. At the Port of the city of Rome, of Saints Ypolitus, Quiriacus and Archillaus. At Alexandria, of Saint Theonas, bishop, venerable and truly worthy of God. [Bouillart, in another order, with a few things added: . . . . . were crowned, at Lycia, in the city of Egea, of the holy martyrs Claudius, Asterius, Neon, The Caroline autograph differs much. Zacheus, who is here in the fourth place, is put second in the Dervensis codex. Claudius and his companions, who occupy the second place in the Pratensis, the third in the Dervensis. Lastly, that Saint Claudius, who has four companions in the Pratensis, has only two in the Dervensis, namely Asterius and Neon. And to correct all these things, Usuardus blotted out five whole lines in his own codex. I will not say that the hand is the same; it is enough to have repeated that a hundred times. Let us see by what causes Usuardus was led to correct. For the order in which he arranged those saints, Sollerius, I think, will not summon the author to court. It is a thing of no account, on which it would be a scruple to dwell in defending it. Nor will the censor of the Pratensis codex deny that Domnina and Theonilla were rightly added to Claudius, Asterius and Neon. We have their proconsular acts, entire and genuine, where those five martyrs are called by name to confess the faith and are tortured with punishments; and at the end, this passion was held in the city of Aegea, under the governor Lysias, on the tenth day before the Kalends of September, Augustus and Aristobolus being consuls. In the Caroline autograph these words were not present, who after bitter torments were fastened to the cross, which Sollerius nevertheless inserted in his article as Usuardus's. The three martyrs Claudius, Asterius, Neon are named in the Dervensis codex with nothing added. Bouillart. Domnina and Theonilla, who under the governor Lysias were tortured with various punishments and slain. At the Port of the city of Rome, of Saint Yppolitus, Quiriacus and Archillaus. On the same day, of blessed Zacheus, bishop, who, fourth from James, ruled the church of Jerusalem most illustriously. At Alexandria, of Saint Theonas, bishop, venerable and truly worthy of God.] Also the birthday of Saints Timotheus and Apollinaris, who, having consummated their martyrdom at the city of Reims, merited the heavenly kingdoms. At Lyon of Gaul, of the holy martyrs Minervius and Eleazarius, with eight sons.

IX Kal. Die 24.

  • 393

Natalis beati Bartholomei apostoli, qui apud Indiam Christi Evangelium praedicans, decollatione martyrium complevit. Hujus apostoli sacratissimum corpus, primum ad insulam Liparis, deinde Beneventum translatum, pia fidelium veneratione honoratur. Eodem die, apud Cartaginem, trecentorum martyrum, qui passi sunt tempore Valeriani et Galieni: denique post supplicia sibi illata, Christum Dei Filium Deum fatentes, in clibanum calcis accensum demersi sunt. Ex quo etiam candidatus ille exercitus beatorum, Massa candida meruit nuncupari. Civitate Rotomago, sancti Audoeni, confessoris, qui saeculum salubriter despiciens, sanctitatis merito, episcopii adeptus est honorem. Nivernis, Patricii abbatis.

The ninth day before the Kalends · 24 August

  • 393

The birthday of blessed Bartholomew the apostle, who, preaching the Gospel of Christ in India, completed his martyrdom by beheading. The most sacred body of this apostle, translated first to the island of Lipari, then to Beneventum, is honoured with the pious veneration of the faithful. On the same day, at Carthage, of three hundred martyrs, who suffered in the time of Valerian and Gallienus: at length, after the tortures inflicted on them, confessing Christ the Son of God to be God, they were plunged into a burning kiln of lime. From which also that white-robed army of the blessed merited to be called the Massa candida, the White Mass. In the city of Rouen, of Saint Audoenus, confessor, who, wholesomely despising the world, by the merit of holiness obtained the honour of the episcopate. At Nevers, of Patricius, abbot.

VIII Kal. Die 25.

  • 397

Romae, natalis sanctorum martyrum Eusebii, Pontiani, Vincentii, et Peregrini, sub Commodo imperatore, qui primo in equuleum levati, nervis quoque distenti, ac deinde fustibus caesi sunt, flammis circa eorum latera appositis, sed cum in laude Christi fidelissimi permanerent, plumbatis usque ad emissionem spiritus mactati sunt. Item Romae, sancti Genesii martyris tempore Diocletiani Augusti, qui fustibus primo crudelissime caesus, equuleo suspensus et ungularum diutissima laceratione vexatus, lampadibus etiam inflammatus, cum in fide persisteret, dicens: Non est rex praeter Christum, pro quo si millies occidar, ipsum mihi de ore, ipsum mihi de corde auferre non poteritis, martyrii coronam capitis obtruncatione promeruit. Eodem die, beati Genesii Arelatensis, qui exceptoris functus officio, cum impia, quibus Christiani puniri jubebantur, edicta nollet excipere, comprehensus atque decollatus, martyrii gloriam proprio cruore baptizatus accepit. In Hispania Dervensis codex: in Hispania civitate Italica. Delevit Usuardus in suo codice, in Hispania, qua de causa, quis dixerit ? an quia de urbe satis nota loquens inutile judicaverit regionem appellare, in qua sita erat ? De S. Geruntio nihil apud martyrologos, qui Usuardum aetate superant. Illius cultum in Hispania dubio procul accepit. Quod antiquius habetur de illo Geruntio, est epigramma Isidori Hispalensis quod manu exaratum legit et suo martyrologio Hispanico intexuit Joannes Tamayus Salazar. In illo epigrammate Geruntius dicitur carceris famisque suppliciis passus sub Nerone. Unde colligit Salazar passum esse circa annum 68, quo vehementius in Hispania saeviebat Neronis crudelitas. BOUILLART. civitate Italica, sancti Gerontii episcopi, in carcere quiescentis.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 25 August

  • 397

At Rome, the birthday of the holy martyrs Eusebius, Pontianus, Vincent and Peregrinus, under the emperor Commodus, who were first raised upon the rack, stretched also with sinews, and then beaten with cudgels, flames being applied about their sides; but when they remained most faithful in the praise of Christ, they were slain with leaded scourges until the giving up of the spirit. Also at Rome, of Saint Genesius, martyr, in the time of Diocletian Augustus, who, first beaten most cruelly with cudgels, hung upon the rack and harassed by a very long tearing of the hooks, set on fire also with torches, when he persisted in the faith, saying: There is no king but Christ, for whom, if I should be slain a thousand times, you will not be able to take him away from my mouth, nor him from my heart, merited the crown of martyrdom by the cutting off of his head. On the same day, of blessed Genesius of Arles, who, discharging the office of a notary, when he would not take down the impious edicts by which Christians were ordered to be punished, was seized and beheaded, and, baptized in his own blood, received the glory of martyrdom. In Spain The Dervensis codex: in Spain, in the city of Italica. Usuardus deleted in Spain in his own codex; for what cause, who could say? Was it because, speaking of a city well enough known, he judged it useless to name the region in which it lay? Of Saint Gerontius there is nothing in the martyrologists who are older than Usuardus. Beyond doubt he learned of his cult in Spain. The oldest thing extant about that Gerontius is an epigram of Isidore of Seville, which Juan Tamayo Salazar read written by hand and wove into his Spanish martyrology. In that epigram Gerontius is said to have suffered the punishments of prison and hunger under Nero. Whence Salazar gathers that he suffered about the year 68, when the cruelty of Nero raged most violently in Spain. Bouillart., in the city of Italica, of Saint Gerontius, bishop, resting in prison.

VII Kal. Die 26.

  • 401

Romae, natalis beati Zephirini papae. Et sanctorum martyrum Hirenei et Abundii, qui in passione beati Laurentii leguntur. Apud Victimilium castrum Italiae, natalis beati Secundi martyris, viri spectabilis et ducis ex legione sanctorum Thebeorum. Item civitate Pergamis, sancti Alexandri, qui et ipse unus exstitit ex legione Thebeorum, et cum nomen Domini Jesu constantissime fateretur, martyrium abscisione capitis complevit. [ Bouillart.: . . . complevit. Deest Carolino autographo S. Aredius ; et ejus loco : Autisiodoro Eleutherii episcopi. Postea Eleutherium hinc erasit Usuardus, lectis, ut conjicere est ; sancti illius praesulis Actis, quae mortem ejus non 26, sed 16 Augusti die consignant. Sextus decimus ille numeratur Autisiodorensis Ecclesiae episcopus, quem obiisse ferunt an. 551 : quod si verum est, falsae sunt notae chronologicae omnes, quibus usus est Anonymus in nova Labbei Bibliotheca, t. I, pag. 419. Quod S. Aredium spectat, Usuardum deceptum fuisse oportet Actis quibusdam illius sancti diversis ab iis quae juris publici fecit nostri Sodalitii praecipuum decus Mabillio, tom. I, Act., pag. 349 : haec enim narrant Aredium IX Kal. Sept. convocatis discipulis, eos de proxima sua morte monuisse ; et hora secunda noctis animam reddidisse. Quae secunda noctis hora si secundam post mediam noctem significet, mortuus fuerit Aredius VIII Kal. Septembris ; sin tantum secundam ab occasu solis indicet, IX Kal. obierit. Testatur Bernardus Guido in Bibliotheca Labbeana, pag. 631, festum S. Aredii agi VIII Kal. Septembris. BOUILLART. In territorio Lemovicensi, Sancti Aredii presbyteri et confessoris.] Autisiodoro, Eleuterii episcopi.

The seventh day before the Kalends · 26 August

  • 401

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Zephirinus, pope. And of the holy martyrs Hireneus and Abundius, who are read of in the passion of blessed Laurence. At the fort Victimilium of Italy, the birthday of blessed Secundus, martyr, a man of rank and a commander from the legion of the holy Thebeans. Also in the city of Pergamis, of Saint Alexander, who himself too was one from the legion of the Thebeans, and, since he confessed the name of the Lord Jesus most steadfastly, completed his martyrdom by the cutting off of his head. [Bouillart: . . . completed. Saint Aredius is lacking in the Caroline autograph; and in his place: At Auxerre, of Eleutherius, bishop. Afterwards Usuardus erased Eleutherius from here, having read, as may be conjectured, the Acts of that holy prelate, which record his death not on the 26th but on the 16th day of August. He is reckoned the sixteenth bishop of the Church of Auxerre, and they say he died in the year 551: if that is true, all the chronological marks which the Anonymous used in Labbe's new Bibliotheca, t. I, p. 419, are false. As regards Saint Aredius, Usuardus must have been deceived by certain Acts of that saint different from those which Mabillon, the chief ornament of our Congregation, made public, tom. I of the Acts, p. 349: for these relate that Aredius, on the ninth day before the Kalends of September, called his disciples together and warned them of his approaching death; and that he gave back his soul at the second hour of the night. If this second hour of the night means the second after midnight, Aredius will have died on the eighth day before the Kalends of September; but if it indicates only the second from sunset, he will have died on the ninth before the Kalends. Bernard Gui testifies, in Labbe's Bibliotheca, p. 631, that the feast of Saint Aredius is kept on the eighth day before the Kalends of September. Bouillart. In the territory Lemovicensis, of Saint Aredius, presbyter and confessor.] At Auxerre, of Eleuterius, bishop.

VI Kal. Die 27.

  • 405

Apud Capuam, natalis sancti Rufi martyris, quem docuit beatus Apollinaris, Petri apostoli discipulus, cum esset idem Rufus patriciae dignitatis. Thomis civitate, sanctorum martyrum Marcellini tribuni et uxoris ejus Manneae, Joannis, Serapionis et Petri. Eodem die, natalis sanctorum Georgii diaconi, Aurelii, Felicis, Nathaliae et Liliosae, quorum primus mirae abstinentiae monachus, ab Hierosolymis Cordubam adveniens, cum reliquis ex eadem urbe claro germine ortis diu optatum sibique a Domino praemonstratum meruit assequi martyrium. Civitate Arelatensi, sancti Caesarii episcopi, mirae sanctitatis et pietatis studii viri. Augustuduni, sancti Siagrii episcopi et confessoris.

The sixth day before the Kalends · 27 August

  • 405

At Capua, the birthday of Saint Rufus, martyr, whom blessed Apollinaris, disciple of Peter the apostle, taught, this same Rufus being of patrician rank. In the city of Thomis, of the holy martyrs Marcellinus, a tribune, and his wife Mannea, John, Serapion and Peter. On the same day, the birthday of Saints Georgius, deacon, Aurelius, Felix, Nathalia and Liliosa, of whom the first, a monk of wonderful abstinence, coming from Jerusalem to Cordoba, merited to attain, with the rest, sprung of noble stock from that same city, the martyrdom long desired and shown to him beforehand by the Lord. In the city of Arles, of Saint Caesarius, bishop, a man of wonderful holiness and zeal for piety. At Augustudunum, of Saint Siagrius, bishop and confessor.

V Kal. Die 28.

  • 407

Romae, natalis beati Hermetis viri illustris, qui, ut in actibus Alexandri papae legitur, primo carcerali custodiae mancipatus, deinde gladio caedente martyrium adeptus est. In Constantinopoli, sancti Alexandri episcopi et confessoris, gloriosi senis, cujus virtute orationis Arius divino judicio condemnatus est. In Africa, depositio sancti Augustini episcopi, qui primo de sua civitate propter barbaros Sardiniam translatus, nuper a rege Longobardorum Ticinis relatus, et honorifice conditus est. Ipso die, natalis sancti Juliani martyris, qui tempore persecutionis, hortatu beati Ferreoli, ex Viennensi urbe, clandestino discessu, ad territorium urbis Arvernae commigravit, ubi tandem desecto gutture, morte horribili necatus est. Apud Sanctonas, sancti Vibiani episcopi et confessoris.

The fifth day before the Kalends · 28 August

  • 407

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Hermes, an illustrious man, who, as is read in the acts of Alexander the pope, was first committed to custody in prison, then obtained martyrdom by the sword striking. In Constantinople, of Saint Alexander, bishop and confessor, a glorious old man, by the power of whose prayer Arius was condemned by the judgment of God. In Africa, the deposition of Saint Augustine, bishop, who, first translated from his own city to Sardinia on account of the barbarians, was lately brought back to Ticinum by the king of the Lombards, and honourably laid to rest. On this same day, the birthday of Saint Julian, martyr, who, in the time of persecution, at the urging of blessed Ferreolus, removed by a secret departure from the city of Vienne to the territory of the city Arverna, where at length, his throat cut, he was killed by a horrible death. At Sanctonas, of Saint Vibianus, bishop and confessor.

IV Kal. Die 29.

  • 411

Decollatio, vel potius inventio capitis, beatissimi Joannis Baptistae. Siquidem decollationem ejus circa solemnitatem Paschalem evenisse ex Evangelica comprobatur lectione, quae tamen hic festiva recolitur, quando caput ejusdem secundo repertum est in Emessa civitate, atque in ecclesia conditum. Romae, natalis sanctae et illustrissimae Sabinae martyris, quae sub Adriano imperatore gladio percussa, martyrii palmam adepta est. In pago Trecassino, sanctae Sabinae virginis, quae pro Christi amore laboriosissima peregrinatione suscepta, etiam virtutibus ac miraculis gloriosa, quievit in pace. Hic lubens incastigatum dimitterem Sollerium. Etsi enim nonnihil vitii reprehenderit in Pratensi codice, nihilominus tamen illum inter puros annumerare dignatus est. Qua de re illi habeo gratiam. Sed Castellani testimonium vel in levissimis rebus infirmare non alienum fuerit. Scripsit oculatissimus ille inspector lituram in Pratensi esse a Parisius dep. usque ad finem. Hoc verum est. Deinde nunc in litura scriptum ositio Mederici presbyteri et monachi, etc. Id vero pernego: post dep. proxime sequitur sancti Mederici et monachi: Denique scriptum olim fuisse Mederici episcopi, ut criminatur Sollerius, id falsissimum est: cum Dervensis codex Medericum non episcopum vocet, sed presbyterum duntaxat. Neque etiam episcopus unquam fuit; sed primum monachus, dein abbas alterius ex duobus monasteriis, quae tunc temporis Augustoduni erant ; tandem Parisiis, quo causa orationis venerat, vitam morte commutavit die quarto Kalendarum Septembrium. Haec ex Actis ejus, quae Mabilio noster edidit p. 1, tertii saeculi p. 10. Antequam hinc discedo, nonnihil difficultatis patior, unde se Sollerius expediat velim. Qua in litura S. Medericus, in eadem versatur S. Candida. Eadem utriusque correctionis scriptura est. Tamen de sancta Candida Sollerius sic: Certe Usuardinam esse satis evincunt codices nostri. Quod igitur de Mederico additum fuit, aeque Usuardinum est. Jam Sollerium rogo veniat ipse, docturus quid discriminis intersit hujus correctionis inter et aliarum correctionum scripturam. Nam nec ego, nec ullus ex hujus urbis eruditis ullum videmus. BOUILLART. Parisius, depositio [ Bouillart. add. sancti] Mederici presbyteri. Ipso die, sanctae Candidae virginis.

The fourth day before the Kalends · 29 August

  • 411

The beheading, or rather the finding of the head, of the most blessed John the Baptist. For his beheading is proved from the Gospel reading to have happened about the Paschal solemnity; it is nevertheless kept here as a feast, when the head of the same was found a second time in the city of Emessa, and laid to rest in a church. At Rome, the birthday of the holy and most illustrious Sabina, martyr, who under the emperor Hadrian, struck with the sword, obtained the palm of martyrdom. In the district of Troyes, of Saint Sabina, virgin, who, having undertaken a most laborious pilgrimage for the love of Christ, glorious also for virtues and miracles, rested in peace. Here I would gladly let Sollerius go uncorrected. For although he found some fault in the Pratensis codex, he nevertheless deigned to number it among the pure. For which I thank him. But it will not be out of place to weaken the testimony of Castellanus even in the slightest matters. That most sharp-eyed inspector wrote that there is an erasure in the Pratensis from Parisius dep. to the end. This is true. Next, that there is now written on the erasure ositio Mederici presbyteri et monachi, etc. This I flatly deny: after dep. there follows next sancti Mederici et monachi. Lastly, that Mederici episcopi was once written there, as Sollerius charges, is most false: since the Dervensis codex calls Medericus not bishop but presbyter only. Nor was he ever a bishop; but first a monk, then abbot of one of the two monasteries which were at Autun at that time; at length at Paris, whither he had come for the sake of prayer, he exchanged life for death on the fourth day before the Kalends of September. These things are from his Acts, which our Mabillon edited, part I of the third century, p. 10. Before I leave here, I suffer some difficulty from which I should like Sollerius to extricate himself. On the same erasure on which Saint Medericus stands, Saint Candida stands too. The script of both corrections is the same. Yet of Saint Candida Sollerius says thus: Certainly our codices sufficiently prove it to be Usuardus's. What was added of Medericus, therefore, is equally Usuardus's. Now I ask Sollerius to come himself, to teach what difference there is between the script of this correction and that of the other corrections. For neither I nor any of the learned men of this city see any. Bouillart. At Paris, the deposition [Bouillart adds sancti] of Medericus, presbyter. On this same day, of Saint Candida, virgin.

III Kal. Die 30.

  • 415

Romae via Ostiensi, passio beati Felicis presbiteri, sub Diocletiano et Maximiano imperatoribus, qui post equulei vexationem, data sententia, cum ad decollandum duceretur, obvius ei fuit quidam Christianus; hic dum se Christianum sponte profiteretur, mox cum eodem pariter decollatus est. Hujus nomen ignorantes 417Christiani, Adauctum eum appellaverunt, quod sancto Felici auctus sit ad coronam. Item Romae, sanctae Gaudentiae virginis. [ Addit Bouillart.: Falsus est et fefellit Sollerium Castellanus, cum scripsit sanctum Agilum non fuisse prima manu signatum. BOUILLART. In pago Meldensi, sancti Agili confessoris.]

The third day before the Kalends · 30 August

  • 415

At Rome, on the Ostian Way, the passion of blessed Felix, presbyter, under Diocletian and Maximian, emperors, who, after the torment of the rack, sentence being given, when he was being led to be beheaded, there met him a certain Christian; this man, while he confessed of his own accord that he was a Christian, was forthwith beheaded together with the same. Not knowing his name, 417the Christians called him Adauctus, because he was added to Saint Felix for the crown. Also at Rome, of Saint Gaudentia, virgin. [Bouillart adds: Castellanus was deceived, and deceived Sollerius, when he wrote that Saint Agilus was not entered in the first hand. Bouillart. In the district Meldensis, of Saint Agilus, confessor.]

Pridie Kal. Die 31.

  • 419

Treveris, natalis sancti Paulini episcopi et confessoris, qui a Constantio imperatore ob Catholicam fidem exilio relegatus, et usque ad mortem, etiam extra Christianum nomen, mutando exilia fatigatus, ad ultimum apud Frigiam defunctus, beatae passionis coronam percepit a Domino. Eodem die, apud Athenas, beati Aristidis, fide, sapientiaque clarissimi, qui Adriano principi de religione Christiana libros obtulit, et quod Christus Jesus solus esset Deus, praesente ipso imperatore, luculentissime peroravit. Autisiodoro, sancti Optati episcopi et confessoris.

The day before the Kalends · 31 August

  • 419

At Trier, the birthday of Saint Paulinus, bishop and confessor, who, banished into exile by the emperor Constantius for the Catholic faith, and worn out until death by the changing of exiles, even beyond the Christian name, at the last dying in Phrygia, received from the Lord the crown of a blessed passion. On the same day, at Athens, of blessed Aristides, most renowned in faith and wisdom, who offered to the emperor Hadrian books on the Christian religion, and, in the presence of the emperor himself, pleaded most brilliantly that Christ Jesus alone is God. At Auxerre, of Saint Optatus, bishop and confessor.

MENSIS SEPTEMBER HABET DIES XXX.

  • 423

SEPTEMBER HAS 30 DAYS.

  • 423

Kalendis. Die 1.

  • 423

Jesu Nave et Gedeon prophetarum. Item natalis beatissimae Annae prophetissae, cujus sanctitatem Evangelicus sermo prodit. Apud Capuam via Aquaria, sancti Prisci martyris, qui fuit unus de antiquis Christi discipulis. Remis, depositio sancti Sixti, episcopi primi civitatis ipsius. Senonis, beati Lupi episcopi et confessoris, de quo refertur, quia quodam die, dum astaret praesente clero sacris altaribus, lapsa est coelitus gemma in ejus sancto calice. Cenomannis, sancti Victoris episcopi. [ Addit Bouillart., charactere Italico: Antiqua huic articulo Egidium supposuit manus, et ita antiqua, ut obscurum sit discrimen. Sed tamen aliam a prima esse Sollerius noverat, et nos ultro confitemur. Cur ergo tam levem ob causam purorum albo Pratensem codicem eradit ? BOUILLART. Eodem die natalis sancti Egidii abbatis.]

On the Kalends · 1 September

  • 423

Of Jesus Nave and Gedeon the prophets. Also the birthday of the most blessed Anna the prophetess, whose holiness the Gospel's word makes known. At Capua, on the Aquarian Way, of Saint Priscus, martyr, who was one of the ancient disciples of Christ. At Reims, the deposition of Saint Sixtus, first bishop of that city. At Senones, of blessed Lupus, bishop and confessor, of whom it is related that on a certain day, while he was standing at the sacred altars with the clergy present, a gem fell from heaven into his holy chalice. At Cenomanni, of Saint Victor, bishop. [Bouillart adds, in italic type: An ancient hand set Egidius under this article, and so ancient that the difference is obscure. Yet Sollerius knew that it was other than the first, and we freely confess it. Why then, for so slight a cause, does he erase the Pratensis codex from the roll of the pure? Bouillart. On the same day, the birthday of Saint Egidius, abbot.]

IV Non. Die 2.

  • 427

Lugduni Galliae, natalis sancti Justi episcopi et confessoris, qui in heremum secedens, cum aliquot annis proximam angelis egisset vitam, et dignus adesset finis tantis laboribus, regnum scandere coelorum beato lucro promeruit. Hujus sanctum corpusculum cum ossibus beati Viatoris ejus ministri, postea Lugdunum relatum est. Eodem die, apud praefatam urbem, sancti Helpidii episcopi et confessoris. In Appamia, sancti Antonini martyris.

The fourth day before the Nones · 2 September

  • 427

At Lyon of Gaul, the birthday of Saint Justus, bishop and confessor, who, withdrawing into the desert, when for some years he had led a life next to the angels, and a worthy end was at hand for such great labours, merited by a blessed gain to ascend to the kingdom of the heavens. His holy little body, with the bones of blessed Viator his minister, was afterwards brought back to Lyon. On the same day, at the aforesaid city, of Saint Helpidius, bishop and confessor. In Appamia, of Saint Antoninus, martyr.

III Non. Die 3.

  • 431

Natalis sanctae Feben, de qua beatus Apostolus Romanis scribit. Romae, passio beatae Serapiae virginis sub Adriano principe, quae cum esset tradita duobus lascivis viris, et minime posset illudi, nec postmodum ardentibus lampadibus inflammari, jussu praesidis fustibus est caesa, sicque gladio decollata. Hujus dies passionis quarto Kalendas Augusti, sepulturae vero ejusdem in praesenti habetur, quando et memoria ipsius martyrii celebrior agitur. In Capua, sanctorum martyrum Antonini pueri, annorum viginti, et Aristei episcopi, quorum gesta habentur. [ Addit Bouillart: Solita sua aequitate noster censor purum esse non censuit Pratensem codicem propter additam praesenti articulo S. Gregorii ordinationem, quamvis Scripturarum differentia se modo non in oculos ingerat. BOUILLART. Romae ordinatio sancti Gregorii papae.]

The third day before the Nones · 3 September

  • 431

The birthday of Saint Febe, of whom the blessed Apostle writes to the Romans. At Rome, the passion of blessed Serapia, virgin, under the emperor Hadrian, who, when she had been handed over to two lustful men and could not at all be abused, nor afterwards be set on fire with burning torches, was beaten with cudgels at the command of the governor, and so beheaded with the sword. The day of her passion is kept on the fourth day before the Kalends of August, but that of her burial on the present day, when also the memory of her martyrdom is celebrated more solemnly. In Capua, of the holy martyrs Antoninus, a boy of twenty years, and Aristeus, bishop, whose acts are extant. [Bouillart adds: With his usual fairness our censor did not judge the Pratensis codex pure, on account of the ordination of Saint Gregory added to the present article, although the difference of the scripts does not force itself upon the eyes. Bouillart. At Rome, the ordination of Saint Gregory, pope.]

Pridie Non. Die 4.

  • 435

Moysi prophetae. Apud Anciram Galatiae, natalis sanctorum martyrum trium puerorum Rufini, Silvani et Vitalici. Cabilonis, sancti Marcelli martyris, qui a praeside Prisco, cum ad profanum convivium fuisset invitatus, et ejusmodi exsecrans epulas, omnes qui aderant, cur idolis deservirent, libera imprecatione corriperet, inaudito crudelitatis genere defodi eum cingulo tenus erectum praeses jussit, sicque sanctus Dei martyr, tertio die in Dei timore et laudibus perseverans, incontaminatum reddidit spiritum. Ipso die, sanctorum Magni et Casti.

The day before the Nones · 4 September

  • 435

Of Moyses the prophet. At Ancyra of Galatia, the birthday of the holy martyrs, three boys, Rufinus, Silvanus and Vitalicus. At Cabilonis, of Saint Marcellus, martyr, who, when he had been invited by the governor Priscus to a profane banquet, and, execrating feasts of that kind, rebuked with free reproach all who were present, why they were slaves to idols, the governor ordered him, by an unheard-of kind of cruelty, to be buried standing upright up to the waist; and so the holy martyr of God, persevering on the third day in the fear and praises of God, gave back an undefiled spirit. On this same day, of Saints Magnus and Castus.

Nonis Die 5.

  • 439

Romae, beati Victorini martyris, qui sanctitate et miraculis praeclarus, sacerdotium Amiterninae urbis, totius populi electione adipiscitur. Inde postmodum sub imperatore Nerva, jussus est suspendi capite deorsum, in loco ubi putentes et sulphureae emanant aquae, quod cum per triduum pro nomine Christi passus fuisset, gloriose coronatus, victor migravit ad Dominum. Item Romae in Portu, natalis sancti Herculani. Capuae, sanctorum martyrum Quinti, Arcontii et Donati. Eodem die [ Apud Bouillart., In Carolino autographo : Eodem die sancti Bertini abbatis : quod cum contra morem nostri esset, totum versum delevit, minutiorique litterarum forma instauravit, ut esset spatium, quo locum, ubi potissimum Bertinus colebatur, designaret. Apposite autem designatus fuit locus ille his verbis in pago Tarvenensi. Tunc enim monasterium quod primus rexit, aut cujus fabricae supremam Bertinus imposuit manum, a Tarvenna aliquot leucis distabat. Nunc autem muris Audomaropolis inclusum est. BOUILLART. in pago Tarvenensi, pro Eodem die], sancti Bertini abbatis.

On the Nones · 5 September

  • 439

At Rome, of blessed Victorinus, martyr, who, illustrious for holiness and miracles, obtains the priesthood of the city Amiternina by the election of the whole people. Then afterwards, under the emperor Nerva, he was ordered to be hung head downward, in a place where stinking and sulphurous waters flow out; and when he had suffered this for three days for the name of Christ, gloriously crowned, he passed as victor to the Lord. Also at Rome, in Portus, the birthday of Saint Herculanus. At Capua, of the holy martyrs Quintus, Arcontius and Donatus. On the same day [In Bouillart, In the Caroline autograph: On the same day, of Saint Bertinus, abbot: and since this was against the custom of our author, he deleted the whole line and restored it in a smaller form of letters, so that there might be room in which to designate the place where Bertinus was chiefly venerated. And that place was fittingly designated by these words, in the district Tarvenensis. For at that time the monastery which Bertinus first ruled, or on whose building he put the finishing hand, stood some leagues distant from Tarvenna. But now it is enclosed within the walls of Saint-Omer. Bouillart. in the district Tarvenensis, instead of Eodem die], of Saint Bertinus, abbot.

VIII Idus. Die 6.

  • 441

Zachariae prophetae, qui de Chaldea senex in patriam reversus, juxta Aggeum prophetam jacet conditus. In Africa, beatorum confessorum et episcoporum, Donatiani, Presidii, Mansueti, Germani, Fuscoli, qui pro assertione catholicae veritatis dirissime caesi et exilio eliminati sunt. Inter quos etiam episcopus, nomine Letus, strenuus atque doctissimus vir, post diuturnos carceris squalores, incendio concrematus est. Item Cappadociae urbe Reaten, sancti Cottidi diaconi. [ Bouillart. add. in margine: Non e margine in textum irrepsit haec commemoratio: Senones sanctorum Germanorum, etc., ut ex ambiguis indiciis conjecit Sollerius: sed etiam num in margine legitur et quidem superiori. BOUILLART. Senones, sanctorum germanorum Sanctiani et Beatae, et sancti Augustini episcopi.]

The eighth day before the Ides · 6 September

  • 441

Of Zacharias the prophet, who, returning as an old man from Chaldea into his homeland, lies buried beside Aggeus the prophet. In Africa, of the blessed confessors and bishops Donatianus, Presidius, Mansuetus, Germanus, Fuscolus, who for the assertion of the catholic truth were most cruelly beaten and driven out into exile. Among whom also a bishop, Letus by name, an energetic and most learned man, after long-lasting filth of prison, was burned up by fire. Also in Cappadocia, in the city Reaten, of Saint Cottidi, deacon. [Bouillart adds in the margin: This commemoration, 'At Senones, of the holy siblings', etc., did not creep from the margin into the text, as Sollerius conjectured from ambiguous indications: but it is read even now in the margin, and indeed the upper one. Bouillart. At Senones, of the holy siblings Sanctianus and Beata, and of Saint Augustine, bishop.]

VII Idus. Die 7.

  • 445

Apud Nicomediam, natalis beatissimi Joannis martyris. Hic nimio calore fidei ignitus, librum in quo continebantur crudelia edicta adversus Christianos, injecta manu detraxit, ac discerpsit, cumque hoc relatum esset Diocletiano et Maximiano Augustis, in eadem urbe constitutis, nimia crudelitate saevientes, omnia suppliciorum genera jusserunt expendi in eum, quae vir nobilissimus cum tanta alacritate vultus ac spiritus pertulit, ut nec tristis quidem pro his videri potuisset. Civitate Aurelianis, depositio sancti Evurtii confessoris, qui ut gesta testantur, primo sanctae Romanae Ecclesiae subdiaconus, dein divino munere per columbam est designatus pontifex praefatae urbis. In territorio Augustudunensi, sanctae Reginae virginis, quae sub proconsule Olibrio, equulei, carceris ac lampadarum perpessa supplicia, tandem capitali sententia finiri est jussa. Item in territorio Parisiacensi, sancti Chlodoaldi presbyteri, In Carolino autographo, sancti Chlodoaldi presbyteri et confessoris. Non debuit Sollerius hac in re Pratensem codicem suis anteferre, qui nihil nisi primigenium admittere volebat: nam id elogii additum ab Usuardo fuit. BOUILLART. tam generis quam mentis nobilitate conspicui.

The seventh day before the Ides · 7 September

  • 445

At Nicomedia, the birthday of the most blessed John, martyr. He, kindled by an exceeding heat of faith, laid his hand on the book in which the cruel edicts against the Christians were contained, pulled it down, and tore it apart; and when this had been reported to Diocletian and Maximian, the Augusti, who were stationed in the same city, raging with exceeding cruelty they ordered every kind of torment to be spent upon him, which the most noble man bore with such great cheerfulness of face and spirit that he could not even have been seen to be sad on account of them. In the city of Orléans, the deposition of Saint Evurtius, confessor, who, as his acts testify, was first a subdeacon of the holy Roman Church, then by divine gift was designated through a dove as pontiff of the aforesaid city. In the territory Augustudunensis, of Saint Regina, virgin, who, under the proconsul Olibrius, having suffered the torments of the rack, of prison and of lamps, was at length ordered to be ended by capital sentence. Also in the territory of Paris, of Saint Chlodoaldus, presbyter, In the Caroline autograph, 'of Saint Chlodoaldus, presbyter and confessor'. Sollerius, who wished to admit nothing but the original, ought not in this matter to have preferred the Pratensis codex to his own: for that eulogy was added by Usuardus. Bouillart. conspicuous for nobility as much of birth as of mind.

VI Idus. Die 8.

  • 449

Nativitas beatissimae Dei genitricis Mariae perpetuae virginis. Apud Nicomediam, natalis sancti Adriani martyris, cum aliis viginti tribus, qui omnes post multa supplicia martyrium crurifragio consummaverunt, sub Diocletiano Augusto. Alexandriae, sanctorum Ammonis, Theophili, Neotherii et aliorum viginti duorum. Item Antiochiae, Timothei et Fausti.

The sixth day before the Ides · 8 September

  • 449

The nativity of the most blessed Mary, Mother of God, ever virgin. At Nicomedia, the birthday of Saint Adrianus, martyr, with twenty-three others, who all, after many tortures, consummated their martyrdom by the breaking of the legs, under Diocletian Augustus. At Alexandria, of Saints Ammon, Theophilus, Neotherius and twenty-two others. Also at Antioch, of Timotheus and Faustus.

V Idus. Die 9.

  • 451

Apud Nicomediam, passio beatorum martyrum Dorothei et Gorgonii, qui praesente Diocletiano, cum persecutionem quam Christianis inferebat, detestarentur, jussi sunt primo suspendi et flagris toto corpore laniari: exin visceribus pelle nudatis, aceto et sale perfundi, sicque in craticula assari, atque ad ultimum laqueo necari. Interjecto autem tempore beatus Gorgonius Romam translatus est positusque via Latina. In Sabinis miliario ab Urbe tricesimo, sanctorum Jacincti, Alexandri, et Tiburtii. In territorio Taruanensi, Omnium exemplorum, etiam Dervensis, haec sunt verba : In territorio Tarvennensi. Liturae tamen inscribuntur in Pratensi. Liturae igitur omnibus exemplis antiquior facta igitur ab auctore. BOUILLART. sancti Autmari episcopi et confessoris. In Scotia, Querani abbatis.

The fifth day before the Ides · 9 September

  • 451

At Nicomedia, the passion of the blessed martyrs Dorotheus and Gorgonius, who, in the presence of Diocletian, when they detested the persecution which he was bringing upon the Christians, were ordered first to be hung up and torn with scourges over the whole body: thereafter, their entrails laid bare of the skin, to be drenched with vinegar and salt, and so to be roasted on a gridiron, and at the last to be killed by the noose. But after an interval of time blessed Gorgonius was translated to Rome and laid on the Latin Way. Among the Sabines, at the thirtieth milestone from the City, of Saints Jacinctus, Alexander and Tiburtius. In the territory Taruanensis, In all the copies, the Dervensis too, these are the words: In the territory Tarvennensis. Yet in the Pratensis they are written on an erasure. The erasure, then, is older than all the copies; made, therefore, by the author. Bouillart. of Saint Autmarus, bishop and confessor. In Scotia, of Queranus, abbot.

IV Idus. Die 10.

  • 455

Romae, beati Hilarii papae. Apud Africam, natalis sanctorum episcoporum Nemesiani, Felicis, Lucii: item Felicis, Littei, Poliani, Victoris, Jaderis et Dativi, qui sub Decio et Valeriano, exurgente persecutionis rabie, ad primam confessionis Christi constantiam, graviter fustibus caesi, deinde compedibus vincti, et ad fodienda metalla deputati, gloriosae confessionis agonem consummaverunt. Extat epistola beati Cypriani ad ipsos et de ipsis scripta. Eodem die in Calcedonia, sanctorum Sosthenis et Victoris, qui ob fidei confessionem, sub Prisco consule, carcerem, vincula et bestias superantes, tandem in oratione vocati de coelo, spiritum emiserunt.

The fourth day before the Ides · 10 September

  • 455

At Rome, of blessed Hilary, pope. At Africa, the birthday of the holy bishops Nemesianus, Felix, Lucius: also of Felix, Litteus, Polianus, Victor, Jader and Dativus, who under Decius and Valerian, as the rage of persecution rose up, at the first steadfastness of the confession of Christ were grievously beaten with cudgels, then bound with fetters, and assigned to dig the mines, and consummated the contest of a glorious confession. There is extant a letter of blessed Cyprian written to them and about them. On the same day at Chalcedon, of Saints Sosthenes and Victor, who for the confession of the faith, under the consul Priscus, overcoming prison, chains and beasts, at length, called from heaven in prayer, gave up the spirit.

III Idus. Die 11.

  • 457

Romae via Salaria veteri, natalis sanctorum martyrum Proti et Jacincti eunuchorum, qui sub Galieno imperatore, quod essent Christiani deprehensi, coguntur sacrificare, sed non consentientes, primo dirissime verberantur, tuncque pariter decollantur.

The third day before the Ides · 11 September

  • 457

At Rome, on the old Salarian Way, the birthday of the holy martyrs Protus and Jacinctus, eunuchs, who, under the emperor Gallienus, having been caught because they were Christians, are compelled to sacrifice; but, not consenting, they are first most cruelly beaten, and then are beheaded together.

Pridie Idus Die 12.

  • 461

Apud urbem Ticinum, sanctorum confessorum Syri et Iventii, qui a beato Hermagora ad praefatam urbem directi, primo illic Christi Evangelium praedicantes, et magnis virtutibus ac miraculis coruscantes, etiam vicinas urbes divinis operibus illustrarunt, sicque in pontificali honore, glorioso fine quieverunt in pace.

The day before the Ides · 12 September

  • 461

At the city of Ticinum, of the holy confessors Syrus and Iventius, who, sent by blessed Hermagoras to the aforesaid city, first preaching the Gospel of Christ there, and flashing with great powers and miracles, illuminated the neighbouring cities also with divine works; and so, in pontifical honour, they rested with a glorious end in peace.

Idibus. Die 13.

  • 463

Apud Aegyptum civitate Alexandria, beati Philippi episcopi, patris sanctae Eugeniae virginis. Hic dignitatem praefecturae deserens, et baptismi gratiam adipiscens, episcopii honorem postmodum est assecutus; quem in oratione constitutum, jussit Perennius praefectus gladio jugulari, tempore Volusiani et Galieni imperatorum.
