Apud Alexandriam, beati Hierii presbiteri, qui in divinis Scripturis nobiliter eruditus, vita purissima, atque ad Christianam philosophiam nudus et penitus expeditus, doctor Ecclesiae incomparabilis fuit, quievit autem Philippi imperatoris tempore. In Galliis civitate Rotenus, sancti Amantii episcopi, cujus vita sanctitate et miraculis exstitit gloriosa. In pago Vilcasino, sancti Clari presbiteri et martyris. Augustuduni, sancti Proculi episcopi.

The day before the Nones · 4 November

  • 655

At Alexandria, of blessed Hierius, presbyter, who, nobly learned in the divine Scriptures, of most pure life, and bare and wholly unencumbered for the Christian philosophy, was an incomparable doctor of the Church; but he rested in the time of the emperor Philip. In the Gauls, in the city of Rotenus, of Saint Amantius, bishop, whose life was glorious in holiness and miracles. In the district Vilcasinus, of Saint Clarus, presbyter and martyr. At Augustudunum, of Saint Proculus, bishop.

Nonis. Die 5.

  • 659

Zachariae prophetae, patris Joannis Baptistae. In Terracina civitate Campaniae, natalis sanctorum Felicis presbiteri et Eusebii monachi, tempore Claudii imperatoris, qui Eusebius cum sepelisset sanctos martyres, Julianum et Caesarium, et converteret multos ad fidem, quos Felix presbiter baptizabat, tenti sunt ambo et ad forum judicis ducti, nec superati; inde carcere reclusi, et nocte eadem cum sacrificare nollent, decollati sunt. In territorio Aurelianensi, sancti Laeti presbiteri.

On the Nones · 5 November

  • 659

Of Zacharias the prophet, father of John the Baptist. In Terracina, a city of Campania, the birthday of Saints Felix, presbyter, and Eusebius, monk, in the time of the emperor Claudius; which Eusebius, when he had buried the holy martyrs Julian and Caesarius, and was converting many to the faith, whom Felix the presbyter baptized, both were seized and led to the judge's forum, and were not overcome; then shut up in prison, and in the same night, when they would not sacrifice, they were beheaded. In the territory of Orléans, of Saint Laetus, presbyter.

VIII Idus. Die 6.

  • 661

Toniza Africae, natalis sancti Felicis. In hujus solemnitate quemdam psalmum beatus Augustinus videtur exponere ad populum. In Oriente civitate Theopoli, sanctorum decem martyrum, qui sub Sarracenis passi leguntur in gestis sanctorum quadraginta. In Frigia, sancti Attici. [ Addit Bouillart, ad oram textus: In pago Lemovicensi Nobiliaco, natalis Sancti Leonardi confessoris.]

The eighth day before the Ides · 6 November

  • 661

At Toniza of Africa, the birthday of Saint Felix. On whose solemnity blessed Augustine appears to expound a certain psalm to the people. In the East, in the city of Theopolis, of ten holy martyrs, who are read in the acts of the forty saints to have suffered under the Saracens. In Phrygia, of Saint Atticus. [Bouillart adds, at the edge of the text: In the district Lemovicensis, at Nobiliacum, the birthday of Saint Leonard, confessor.]

VII Idus. Die 7.

  • 665

In Alexandria beati Achillae episcopi, qui eruditione, fide, conversatione et moribus insignis fuit. Apud Perusinam, Italiae urbem Hic multa movet de Herculano Sollerius, cujus Herculani elogium Usuardo ascribendum esse merito autumat, consentientibus probatissimis quibusque codicibus, cum exaratis manu, tum excusis; quia tamen non in textu Pratensis codicis, sed in margine elogium istud manu recentiore scriptum legitur, hinc orta est acuto censori dubitatio, utra sit auctoris manus, seu, ut ipse loquitur, utra manus autographa sit. Quam dubitationem ita tollit, ut existimet nec priorem nec posteriorem manum Usuardinam esse, et ita judicaturum quisquis, deposito partium studio, hoc et alia Pratensis codicis vitia expenderit. Contestor eruditos omnes, an paucula quaedam in margine aut vacuo versus spatio manu recentiore addita (quod unum est Pratensis codicis vitium, si tamen vitium est) eo quemquam inclinare possint, ut autographum dubitet esse martyrologium, quod et chronologicis notis et litterarum forma constat nono saeculo et Usuardi aetate conscriptum; quod in Sangermana abbatia compositum est, et in usum ejusdem abbatiae concinnatum ab ejusdem abbatiae monacho; quod per trecentos aut eos amplius annos publice in capitulo lectum est; quod studiosissime servatum est in bibliotheca nunquam expilata, nunquam ambusta, saltem a nono saeculo? Quid in hoc litteraturae genere certum esse potest, si hoc non est? De Herculani elogio orae codicis addito jam in epistola exposui qui acciderit, ut codici autographo deesset, et tamen in omnibus exemplis reperiatur. BOUILLART., natalis sancti Herculani episcopi, cujus corpus, post abscisionem capitis, ita unitum et sanum die quadragesimo, ut scribit beatus Gregorius papa, repertum est, ac si nulla hoc incisio ferri tetigisset. Eodem die, beati Amaranti martyris, qui apud Albigensem urbem, exacto agonis fidelis cursu, sepultus, vivit in gloria. In Frisia, depositio sancti Willibrordi episcopi.

The seventh day before the Ides · 7 November

  • 665

In Alexandria, of blessed Achillas, bishop, who was distinguished in learning, faith, way of life and morals. At Perusina, a city of Italy Here Sollerius stirs up much about Herculanus, whose eulogy he rightly holds is to be ascribed to Usuardus, all the most approved codices agreeing, both those written by hand and the printed; but because that eulogy is read not in the text of the Pratensis codex but in the margin, written in a more recent hand, hence arose in the sharp censor a doubt which is the author's hand, or, as he himself puts it, which hand is the autograph. This doubt he removes in such a way that he thinks neither the earlier nor the later hand is Usuardus's, and that whoever, laying aside partisanship, weighs this and the other faults of the Pratensis codex will so judge. I call all the learned to witness whether a very few things added in a more recent hand in the margin or in the empty space of a line (which is the one fault of the Pratensis codex, if indeed it is a fault) could incline anyone to doubt that a martyrology is the autograph which, both by its chronological marks and by the form of its letters, was plainly written in the ninth century and in Usuardus's age; which was composed in the abbey of Saint-Germain, and arranged for the use of the same abbey by a monk of the same abbey; which for three hundred years or more was read publicly in chapter; which has been most carefully preserved in a library never plundered, never burned, at least since the ninth century? What can be certain in this kind of learning, if this is not? Of the eulogy of Herculanus added on the edge of the codex, I have already explained in my letter how it happened that it was lacking in the autograph codex, and yet is found in all the copies. Bouillart., the birthday of Saint Herculanus, bishop, whose body, after the cutting off of the head, was found on the fortieth day so united and sound, as blessed Gregory the pope writes, as if no incision of iron had touched it. On the same day, of blessed Amarantus, martyr, who, at the city Albigensis, having finished the course of the faithful contest, buried, lives in glory. In Frisia, the deposition of Saint Willibrord, bishop.

VI Idus. Die 8.

  • 669

Romae, via Lavicana, passio sanctorum Claudii, Nicostrati, Simproniani, Castorii et Simplicii Quinque illi martyres in litura versantur; nec tamen a Pratensi ulla exempla discrepant. Rogo cuinam ascribenda sit correctio. BOUILLART., qui primo in carcerem trusi, deinde scorpionibus gravissime sunt caesi, sed cum a fide Christi mutari non possent, jussi sunt a Diocletiano in medio mari praecipites dari. Item via Lavicana, natalis quatuor coronatorum Severi, Severiani, Carpofori et Victorini, qui jubente Diocletiano ictibus plumbatarum usque ad mortem caesi sunt. Horum nomina cum minime potuissent repperiri, quae Domino revelante interjectis annis postea ostensa sunt, statutum est ut anniversaria dies ipsorum sub nominibus supradictorum quinque martyrum recoleretur.

The sixth day before the Ides · 8 November

  • 669

At Rome, on the Lavican Way, the passion of Saints Claudius, Nicostratus, Simpronianus, Castorius and Simplicius Those five martyrs lie on an erasure; and yet no copies differ from the Pratensis. I ask to whom the correction is to be ascribed. Bouillart., who were first thrust into prison, then most grievously beaten with scorpions, but when they could not be changed from the faith of Christ, they were ordered by Diocletian to be thrown headlong into the middle of the sea. Also on the Lavican Way, the birthday of the four crowned ones, Severus, Severianus, Carpoforus and Victorinus, who at the command of Diocletian were beaten to death with blows of leaded scourges. Since their names could by no means be found, which, the Lord revealing them, were afterwards shown when years had intervened, it was established that their anniversary day should be kept under the names of the five martyrs above-mentioned.

V Idus. Die 9.

  • 673

Apud Amasiam civitatem Marmaritanorum, natalis sancti Theodori martyris, qui temporibus Maximiani et Maximini imperatorum, pro confessione Christianae fidei fortiter caesus ac in carcerem missus, dein, Domino Salvatore sibi apparente et monente, ut constanter et viriliter ageret, relevatus est. Novissime postquam equuleo suspensus et ungulis est corrosus, ita ut ejus interiora nuda apparerent, ardentibus traditur ignibus. Eodem die civitate Bituricas, sancti Ursicini confessoris, qui Romae ordinatus a successoribus apostolorum, primus eidem urbi destinatur episcopus.

The fifth day before the Ides · 9 November

  • 673

At Amasia, a city of the Marmaritani, the birthday of Saint Theodore, martyr, who in the times of the emperors Maximian and Maximinus, for the confession of the Christian faith, was severely beaten and sent into prison; then, the Lord Saviour appearing to him and admonishing him to act constantly and manfully, he was relieved. At the last, after he had been hung upon the rack and gnawed with claws, so that his inward parts appeared bare, he is given over to burning fires. On the same day, in the city of Bourges, of Saint Ursicinus, confessor, who, ordained at Rome by the successors of the apostles, is appointed the first bishop to that same city.

IV Idus. Die 10.

  • 677

Natalis beati Martini papae, qui ob fidem catholicam ab imperatore Constantino haeretico de Ecclesia raptus, ac perductus Constantinopolim, relegatus apud Cersonam Liciae provinciae, ibidem vitam finivit, multis in eodem loco virtutum signis usque hodie refulgens. In territorio Agatensi, sanctorum martyrum Tiberii, Modesti et Florentiae, qui tempore Diocletiani variis tormentis cruciati, martyrium compleverunt. Antiochiae, sanctorum Demetrii episcopi, Aniani diaconi, Eustosii et aliorum viginti.

The fourth day before the Ides · 10 November

  • 677

The birthday of blessed Martin, pope, who, for the catholic faith, was seized from the Church by the heretical emperor Constantine, and brought to Constantinople, and banished to Cersona of the province of Licia, ended his life there, shining in the same place to this day with many signs of virtues. In the territory Agatensis, of the holy martyrs Tiberius, Modestus and Florentia, who in the time of Diocletian, tortured with various torments, completed their martyrdom. At Antioch, of Saints Demetrius, bishop, Anianus, deacon, Eustosius and twenty others.

III Idus Die 11.

  • 681

In Galliis, Turonis civitate, natalis beati Martini episcopi et confessoris, cujus vita tantis extitit miraculis gloriosa, ut trium mortuorum meruerit esse suscitator. In Scithia metropoli Frigiae Salutariae, passio sancti Mennae martyris, qui persecutione Diocletiani, postquam studuit caelesti regi secreta conversatione in heremo militare, procedens ad publicum, ac se Christianum libera voce declarans, primo diris cruciatibus examinatus, novissime fixis in oratione genibus, et Domino Jesu Christo gratias agens, gladio caesus est. Item Lugduni, sancti Verani episcopi, cujus vita fidei et virtutum meritis illustris fuit.

The third day before the Ides · 11 November

  • 681

In the Gauls, in the city of Tours, the birthday of blessed Martin, bishop and confessor, whose life was so glorious with miracles that he merited to be the raiser of three dead men. In Scithia, the metropolis of Phrygia Salutaria, the passion of Saint Menna, martyr, who, in the persecution of Diocletian, after he had striven to serve as a soldier to the heavenly King by a hidden way of life in the desert, coming forth into public and declaring himself a Christian with free voice, first tried with dire tortures, at the last, his knees fixed in prayer and giving thanks to the Lord Jesus Christ, was slain with the sword. Also at Lyon, of Saint Veranus, bishop, whose life was illustrious for the merits of faith and virtues.

Pridie Idus. Die 12.

  • 685A

Apud provinciam Tarraconensem, civitate Tirassona, beati Emiliani presbiteri et confessoris, cujus admirabilem vitam Braulio Caesaraugustanus episcopus simplici sermone descripsit. Civitate Agripinensi, depositio sancti Cuniberti episcopi. In territorio Senonico, sancti Paterni martyris. Castello Miliduno, sancti Leonii confessoris.

The day before the Ides · 12 November

  • 685A

At the province of Tarragona, in the city of Tirassona, of blessed Emilianus, presbyter and confessor, whose admirable life Braulio, bishop of Caesaraugusta, described in simple speech. In the city of Cologne, the deposition of Saint Cunibertus, bishop. In the territory Senonicus, of Saint Paternus, martyr. At the castle Milidunum, of Saint Leonius, confessor.

Idibus. Die 13.

  • 689

Ravennae, natalis sanctorum martyrum Valentini, Solutoris et Victoris. In Provincia apud civitatem Aquis, beati Mitrii, clarissimi martyris. In Africa, sanctorum martyrum Archadii, Paschasii, Probi et Euticiani, qui cum in Arrianam perfidiam nullatenus declinare paterentur, primum proscripti, deinde in exilium acti, tum atrocissimis suppliciis excruciati, ad postremum diversis mortibus interempti sunt [ Bouillart., ad oram paginae: Parisius, S. Gendulfi episcopi.]. Turonis, depositio sancti Briccii episcopi. Sic habet hoc loco Pratensis codex. Tria verba haec : Civitate Toleto sancti nihil differunt a reliqua pagina. Duae primae syllabae nominis Eugenii ab eadem sunt manu, sed nigriore atramento. Caeterae litterae ejusdem nominis et antistitis in litura sunt, cum primis quatuor litteris vocis Malbodio. Caetera sana. Suspicor tamen novo atramento suffusas fuisse pristinas litteras ne evanescerent. Conjecturam firmant duae postremae syllabae vocis Aldegundis, et integra vox virginis, quae, quod eodem modo renovatae non sunt, incipiunt minus ac minus oculos ferire. BOUILLART. Civitate Toleto, sancti Eugenii episcopi et confessoris. [ Bouillart., S. Eugenii antistitis. Malbodio monasterio, sanctae Aldegundis virginis.]

On the Ides · 13 November

  • 689

At Ravenna, the birthday of the holy martyrs Valentinus, Solutor and Victor. In the Province, at the city Aquis, of blessed Mitrius, most illustrious martyr. In Africa, of the holy martyrs Archadius, Paschasius, Probus and Euticianus, who, when they would by no means bear to decline into the Arian perfidy, first proscribed, then driven into exile, then tortured with most atrocious punishments, at the last were slain by diverse deaths [Bouillart, at the edge of the page: At Paris, of Saint Gendulfus, bishop.]. At Tours, the deposition of Saint Briccius, bishop. So the Pratensis codex has it in this place. These three words, Civitate Toleto sancti, differ in nothing from the rest of the page. The first two syllables of the name Eugenii are of the same hand, but in blacker ink. The remaining letters of the same name and of antistitis are on an erasure, together with the first four letters of the word Malbodio. The rest is sound. I suspect, however, that the original letters were washed over with new ink lest they fade away. The conjecture is confirmed by the last two syllables of the word Aldegundis, and the whole word virginis, which, because they were not renewed in the same way, are beginning to strike the eyes less and less. Bouillart. In the city of Toledo, of Saint Eugenius, bishop and confessor. [Bouillart: of Saint Eugenius, prelate. At the monastery Malbodium, of Saint Aldegundis, virgin.]

XVIII Kal. Die 14.

  • 693

Apud Thraciam civitate Eraclea, natalis sanctorum martyrum Clementini, Theodoti, et Philomini. Alexandriae, sancti Serapionis, quem persecutores sub Decio principe crudelissimis affecerunt suppliciis, ita ut omnes ei juncturas membrorum prius solventes, de superioribus eum praecipitarent, ac sic Christi martyr efficeretur.

The eighteenth day before the Kalends · 14 November

  • 693

At Thrace, in the city of Eraclea, the birthday of the holy martyrs Clementinus, Theodotus, and Philominus. At Alexandria, of Saint Serapion, whom the persecutors under the emperor Decius afflicted with most cruel punishments, so that, first loosening all the joints of his limbs, they cast him headlong from the heights, and so he was made a martyr of Christ.

XVII Kal. Die 15.

  • 695

Apud Nolam, Campaniae urbem, natalis beati Felicis episcopi, qui a quinto decimo aetatis suae anno, miraculorum gloria insignis fuit, et sub Martiano praeside cum aliis triginta martyrium complevit. Ipso die, sancti Eugenii, qui apud pagum Parisiacensem, consummato martyrii cursu, beatae passionis coronam percepit a Domino. [ Apud Bouillart., in margine: Eodem die, sancti Macuti episcopi et confessoris. Apud monasterium S. Germani, dedicatio ecclesiae in honore S. Johannis Baptistae, sanctique Johannis apostoli et evangelistae et S. Martialis; sanctorum martyrum Laurentii, Sebastiani, Cristophori, necnon SS. confessorum Martini, Gregorii, Benedicti et sanctae Mariae Magdalenae.]

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 15 November

  • 695

At Nola, a city of Campania, the birthday of blessed Felix, bishop, who from the fifteenth year of his age was distinguished by the glory of miracles, and under the governor Martianus completed his martyrdom with thirty others. On this same day, of Saint Eugenius, who, at the district of Paris, the course of martyrdom being consummated, received from the Lord the crown of a blessed passion. [In Bouillart, in the margin: On the same day, of Saint Macutus, bishop and confessor. At the monastery of Saint Germanus, the dedication of the church in honour of Saint John the Baptist, and of Saint John the apostle and evangelist, and of Saint Martialis; of the holy martyrs Laurence, Sebastian, Christopher, and also of the holy confessors Martin, Gregory, Benedict, and of Saint Mary Magdalene.]

XVI Kal. Die 16.

  • 699

Lugduni, natalis sancti Eucherii confessoris, qui ex nobilissimo senatorum ordine, ad religiosam vitam habitumque conversus, diu intra septa speluncae sponte clausus, Christo servivit deinde, apud praefatam urbem, revelante angelo, in pontificali cathedra solemniter collocatus est.

The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 16 November

  • 699

At Lyon, the birthday of Saint Eucherius, confessor, who, from the most noble order of senators, converted to the religious life and habit, long shut up of his own will within the enclosure of a cave, served Christ afterwards, at the aforesaid city, an angel revealing it, was solemnly set in the pontifical chair.

XV Kal. Die 17

  • 703

Apud Alexandriam, beati Dionisii episcopi. Hic in multis saepe confessionibus satis clarus, et pro passionum tormentorumque diversitate magnificus existens, quievit Valeriani et Galieni imperatorum temporibus. Civitate Corduba, passio sanctorum martyrum Aciscli et Victoriae, ubi ob commendationem pretiosae mortis eorum, eodem die rosae ortae divinitus colliguntur. Aurelianis, sancti Aniani episcopi et confessoris cujus mortem in conspectu Domini pretiosam miracula crebra testantur.

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 17 November

  • 703

At Alexandria, of blessed Dionisius, bishop. This man, often quite illustrious in many confessions, and being magnificent for the diversity of passions and torments, rested in the times of the emperors Valerian and Gallienus. In the city of Cordoba, the passion of the holy martyrs Acisclus and Victoria, where, in commendation of their precious death, on the same day roses sprung up by divine power are gathered. At Orléans, of Saint Anianus, bishop and confessor whose death, precious in the sight of the Lord, frequent miracles attest.

XIV Kal. Die 18.

  • 707

Apud Antiochiam, natalis beati Romani, qui temporibus Diocletiani, cum quidam praefectus ecclesiam irrumpere, eamque funditus conaretur evertere, caeteros Christianos hortatus est ut ei contradicerent; unde una cum quodam parvulo, nomine Barala, celebri martyrio coronatus est. Item in eadem urbe, sancti Esichii, qui cum esset miles et praeceptum audisset ut quisquis non sacrificaret idolis, cingulum militiae deponeret, repente cingulum solvit. Ob hanc causam ligato in dextram ejus saxo ingenti, in fluvium praecipitari jussus est.

The fourteenth day before the Kalends · 18 November

  • 707

At Antioch, the birthday of blessed Romanus, who, in the times of Diocletian, when a certain prefect was trying to break into the church and to overturn it from its foundations, exhorted the other Christians to oppose him; whence, together with a certain little child named Barala, he was crowned with a celebrated martyrdom. Also in the same city, of Saint Esichius, who, being a soldier, and having heard the command that whoever did not sacrifice to idols should lay down the belt of military service, at once loosed his belt. For this cause, a huge stone being bound to his right hand, he was ordered to be cast headlong into the river.

XIII Kal. Die 19.

  • 711

Romae, via Appia, natalis sancti Maximi presbyteri et martyris, qui persecutione Maximiani passus, positus est ad sanctum Sixtum. Eodem die, sancti Crispini episcopi, qui civitate Astiagensi, capite amputato, martyrii gloriam adeptus est. Apud Viennam, sanctorum martyrum Severi, Exuperii et Feliciani, quorum corpora post multa annorum curricula, ipsis revelantibus inventa, et a pontifice urbis, clero et populo honorifice sublata, ac condigno honore condita sunt. Item sancti Fausti diaconi, hic grandaevus et plenus dierum confessor perdurans, perfecto martyrio, capitis obtruncatione consummatus est. [ Apud Bouillart., in textu charactere cursivo: Turonis, sancti Oddonis abbatis; et ad oram paginae: Parisius, dedicatio ecclesiae in honore sanctae crucis atque sanctae genitricis Dei Mariae, et sanctorum martyrum Stephani, Vincentii et sancti confessoris Germani.]

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 19 November

  • 711

At Rome, on the Appian Way, the birthday of Saint Maximus, presbyter and martyr, who, having suffered in the persecution of Maximian, was laid at Saint Sixtus. On the same day, of Saint Crispinus, bishop, who in the city Astiagensis, his head being cut off, obtained the glory of martyrdom. At Vienne, of the holy martyrs Severus, Exuperius and Felicianus, whose bodies, after many courses of years, found by their own revelation, and raised with honour by the bishop of the city, the clergy and the people, were also laid up with fitting honour. Also of Saint Faustus, deacon, this man, very aged and full of days, persevering as a confessor, his martyrdom perfected, was consummated by the cutting off of the head. [In Bouillart, in the text, in cursive type: At Tours, of Saint Oddo, abbot; and at the edge of the page: At Paris, the dedication of the church in honour of the holy cross and of the holy Mother of God, Mary, and of the holy martyrs Stephen, Vincent, and of the holy confessor Germanus.]

XII Kal. Die 20.

  • 715

Romae, natalis beati Pontiani papae, qui Maximino adversum ecclesiarum sacerdotes persecutionem commovente, cum Ypolito presbitero Sardiniam deportatus, ubique fustibus mactatus martyrium consummavit. Corpus ejus a beato Fabiano papa relatum, atque in cimiterio Calixti sepultum est. Cabilone, sancti Silvestri episcopi et confessoris. Hic quadragesimo secundo anno sacerdotii sui, plenus dierum atque virtutum migravit ad Dominum. Apud Siciliam civitate Messana, sanctorum Ampeli et Gaii. Ipso die civitate Taurinis, Octavii, Solutoris et Adventoris.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 20 November

  • 715

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Pontianus, pope, who, when Maximinus stirred up a persecution against the priests of the churches, was deported to Sardinia with Ypolitus the presbyter, and, slain with cudgels everywhere, consummated his martyrdom. His body was brought back by blessed Fabian the pope, and buried in the cemetery of Calixtus. At Cabilone, of Saint Silvester, bishop and confessor. This man, in the forty-second year of his priesthood, full of days and of virtues, passed to the Lord. At Sicily, in the city of Messana, of Saints Ampeli and Gaius. On this same day, in the city of Turin, of Octavius, Solutor and Adventor.

XI Kal. Die 21.

  • 719

Natalis beatissimi Rufi, de quo apostolus Paulus ad Romanos scribit. In Italia, monasterio Bobio, depositio sancti Columbani abbatis; qui multorum cenobiorum fundator, et innumerabilium pater extitit monachorum, multisque virtutibus clarus, quievit in senectute bona. Item in Histria, passio sancti Mauri martyris. [ Bouillart.: Natalis beatissimi Rufi, de quo apostolus Paulus ad Romanos scribit. Hic Sollerius primum notat de suo, Pratensem codicem hoc die et duobus sequentibus a puris, imo ab omnibus ferme codicibus recedere. Ego, qui die sequenti nullam in codice lituram videbam, non manum aliam, non aliud attramentum, statim admirari, vertere folium, Sollerianum textum percurrere, scire avidus quid vitio censor Pratensi daret. Nihil a Pratensi diversum. Sed quid animi fuit cum haec legi statim post textum : Undecumque puri sunt Pratensis, Heriniensis, etc. Fateor, vix risum tenere potui. Pergo legere varias lectiones hujus diei XXI : Turbatissima ibi omnia esse oportet, cum testetur Castellanus, ad XXII de sola Caecilia agi : porro XXIII expunctum rursus textum totum, etc. Tum de Columbano, etc., ad XXIV observatur diversa manus ; unde ferme posset conjici eamdem omnino esse quae XIX, XXI, XXII et XXIII, hujus mensis, in textus omnes grassatur. Credit lector haec serio dici, et incipit Pratensi codici timere ex illo Castellani testimonio. Quaeso, oculos conjiciat in alteram e regione paginam, omni timore liberabitur. Ego, inquit Sollerius, in Auctariis, hoc et duobus sequentibus diebus Castellani novissimo testimonio innitendum non putavi. Praeclaram censuram ? Caetera videamus. Primum stomachatur Sollerius hic additam fuisse Mariam Neocorensem : nullo tamen argumento sanctae virginis mortem die I hujus mensis potius quam hoc die consignandum esse. Deinde affirmat Columbanum ab Usuardo hic fuisse memoratum. Fateor satius futurum fuisse Columbanum relinquere quo illum loco primum annuntiaverat. Nam, teste Jona Vitae auctore, sanctus ille abbas hoc die mortem obiit. Sed majorem, quam Adoni, fidem habuit Usuardus Kalendario quod Ludovici Pii tempore conditum fuit pro quibusdam Galliarum ecclesiis, quae ritum Romanum a Carolo Magno praescriptum amplecti vellent ; forte etiam incidit in Gellonense Martyrologium, quod et ipsum non hoc die, sed XXIII hujus mensis sanctum Columbanum memorat. Demetrius et Honorius sumuntur ex Hieronymianis Indicibus, qui undecim alios addunt, saltem in perantiquo nostro codice. BOUILLART. Apud Neocorensem civitatem sanctae Mariae virginis, quae passa est sub Adriano et Antonino principibus. In provincia Histria, passio sancti Mauri martyris. Civitate Hostia sanctorum martyrum Demetrii et Honorii.]

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 21 November

  • 719

The birthday of the most blessed Rufus, of whom the apostle Paul writes to the Romans. In Italy, at the monastery Bobium, the deposition of Saint Columbanus, abbot; who was the founder of many monasteries and the father of innumerable monks, and, illustrious with many virtues, rested in a good old age. Also in Histria, the passion of Saint Maurus, martyr. [Bouillart: The birthday of the most blessed Rufus, of whom the apostle Paul writes to the Romans. Here Sollerius first notes on his own account that the Pratensis codex on this day and the two following departs from the pure, nay from almost all the codices. I, who on the following day saw no erasure in the codex, no other hand, no other ink, at once wondered, turned the leaf, ran through Sollerius's text, eager to know what the censor charged as a fault to the Pratensis. Nothing different from the Pratensis. But what was my state of mind when I read this immediately after the text: Wholly pure are the Pratensis, Heriniensis, etc. I confess I could scarcely hold back my laughter. I go on to read the various readings of this 21st day: All things there must be in the greatest disorder, since Castellanus testifies that at XXII it treats of Caecilia alone: further, that at XXIII the whole text is again struck out, etc. Then of Columbanus, etc.; at XXIV a different hand is observed; whence it could almost be conjectured that it is altogether the same one which on the XIX, XXI, XXII and XXIII of this month runs riot through all the texts. The reader believes that these things are said in earnest, and begins to fear for the Pratensis codex from that testimony of Castellanus. Let him, I pray, cast his eyes on the other page opposite; he will be freed of all fear. I, says Sollerius, did not think that in the Auctaria, on this and the two following days, reliance was to be placed on Castellanus's latest testimony. A splendid censure? Let us see the rest. First Sollerius is vexed that Mary Neocorensis was added here: yet with no argument that the death of the holy virgin is to be recorded on the 1st day of this month rather than on this day. Next he affirms that Columbanus was commemorated here by Usuardus. I confess it would have been better to leave Columbanus in the place where he had first announced him. For, on the testimony of Jonas, author of his Life, that holy abbot died on this day. But Usuardus put more faith than in Ado in the Kalendar which was drawn up in the time of Louis the Pious for certain churches of the Gauls which wished to embrace the Roman rite prescribed by Charlemagne; perhaps too he came upon the Gellone Martyrology, which itself also commemorates Saint Columbanus not on this day but on the 23rd of this month. Demetrius and Honorius are taken from the Hieronymian lists, which add eleven others, at least in our very ancient codex. Bouillart. At the city Neocorensis, of Saint Mary, virgin, who suffered under the emperors Hadrian and Antoninus. In the province of Histria, the passion of Saint Maurus, martyr. In the city of Ostia, of the holy martyrs Demetrius and Honorius.]

X Kal. Die 22.

  • 723

Romae, natalis sanctae Ceciliae virginis, quae sponsum Valerianum et fratrem ejus Tiburtium ad credendum Christo ac martyrium perdocuit, et ipsa deinde martyrizata est, ignem quidem superans, sed ferro occisa. Passa est autem Marci Aurelii et Commodi imperatorum temporibus. Item Romae, sancti Mauri martyris, qui sub Celerino praefecto agonizavit. Augustuduni, sancti Pragmatii episcopi.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 22 November

  • 723

At Rome, the birthday of Saint Cecilia, virgin, who thoroughly taught her betrothed Valerianus and his brother Tiburtius to believe in Christ and to martyrdom, and afterwards was herself martyred, overcoming fire indeed, but slain by the sword. She suffered, moreover, in the times of the emperors Marcus Aurelius and Commodus. Also at Rome, of Saint Maurus, martyr, who contended under the prefect Celerinus. At Augustudunum, of Saint Pragmatius, bishop.

IX Kal. Die 23.

  • 725

Natalis sancti Clementis episcopi, qui, quartus a beato Petro apostolo, Romanae Ecclesiae pontificatum tenuit, et sub persecutione Trajani in mare praecipitatus, martyrio coronatur. Eodem die, sanctae Felicitatis, matris septem filiorum martyrum, quae jubente Antonino decollata est pro Christo. [ Interserit Bouillart.: Pugnat Sollerius Columbanum non fuisse hodie ab Usuardo memoratum. Argumenta duo sunt. Primum ex Adonis auctoritate ducit, quem certus est de Columbano mentionem fecisse medius tertius. Alterum vero est dies mortis Columbani, quem diem esse hujus mensis XXI testatur Ionas, Vitae scriptor. Jam dixi non semel, Usuardus erraverit necne, nihil nostra interesse, propter errata quaedam opus auctori abjudicari non posse : quod tamen ultimum argumentum ita malum judico, ut Sollerii causa bonum esse vellem. De isto articulo nescio quid Sollerium admonuerit Chamillartus, sed in Auctariis video legisse illum aliter atque Castellanus legit. Quod certe non capio, inquit Sollerius, nisi a tempore Sirmondi nova interpolatio accesserit. Tam atrocem accusationem ulciscerer, nisi iratus essem. BOUILLART. In Italia, beati Columbani abbatis, multorum fundatoris coenobiorum]. In pago Hasbanio, sancti Trudonis presbiteri et confessoris. Parisius, depositio beati Severini monachi et solitarii. In civitate Emerita, sanctae Lucretiae virginis.

The ninth day before the Kalends · 23 November

  • 725

The birthday of Saint Clement, bishop, who, fourth from blessed Peter the apostle, held the pontificate of the Roman Church, and, under the persecution of Trajan cast headlong into the sea, is crowned with martyrdom. On the same day, of Saint Felicitas, mother of seven martyred sons, who at the command of Antoninus was beheaded for Christ. [Bouillart inserts: Sollerius contends that Columbanus was not commemorated by Usuardus today. The arguments are two. The first he draws from the authority of Ado, who, he is certain, made mention of Columbanus the day before yesterday. The other is the day of Columbanus's death, which Jonas, the writer of his Life, testifies was the 21st of this month. I have said already, and not once only, that whether Usuardus erred or not is nothing to our purpose, and that a work cannot be taken away from its author on account of certain errors: yet I judge that last argument so bad that for Sollerius's sake I could wish it good. About that article I do not know what Chamillart advised Sollerius, but in the Auctaria I see that he read otherwise than Castellanus read. Which I certainly do not understand, says Sollerius, unless a new interpolation has come in since the time of Sirmond. I would avenge so atrocious an accusation, were I not angry. Bouillart. In Italy, of blessed Columbanus, abbot, founder of many monasteries]. In the district Hasbanius, of Saint Trudo, presbyter and confessor. At Paris, the deposition of blessed Severinus, monk and solitary. In the city of Emerita, of Saint Lucretia, virgin.

VIII Kal. Die 24.

  • 729

Romae, natalis beati Chrisogoni martyris, qui post longa vincula et carceres, pro constantissima Christi confessione toleratos, jubente Diocletiano, capite caesus et in mare projectus, martyrium consummavit. Item Romae. sancti Crescentiani martyris, qui in passione beati Marcelli papae legitur. Castro Blavia, sancti Romani presbiteri et confessoris, cujus sanctitatis praeconium miraculorum gloria declarat. Civitate Corduba, sanctarum virginum Florae et Mariae, quae post diuturnos carceres, pro Christo gladio interemptae sunt. Apud Perusiam Tusciae civitatem sancti Felicissimi.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 24 November

  • 729

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Chrisogonus, martyr, who, after long chains and prisons, endured for the most constant confession of Christ, at the command of Diocletian was beheaded and thrown into the sea, and consummated his martyrdom. Also at Rome. of Saint Crescentianus, martyr, who is read of in the passion of blessed Marcellus the pope. At the fortress Blavia, of Saint Romanus, presbyter and confessor, the proclamation of whose holiness the glory of miracles declares. In the city of Cordoba, of the holy virgins Flora and Mary, who after long imprisonments were slain with the sword for Christ. At Perusia, a city of Tuscia, of Saint Felicissimus.

VII Kal. Die 25.

  • 733

Natalis beati Petri episcopi Alexandriae, qui cum esset omnibus adornatus virtutibus, in Scripturis quoque divinis nulli priorum inferior, et sacerdos et hostia Dei, subito rapitur, atque ex Maximini praecepto capite obtruncatur. Cum quo simul et alii plures ex Aegypto episcopi trucidantur, fere cum clericis et laicis, sexcenti sexaginta. Item Antiochiae, sancti Erasmi martyris. [ Bouillart., ad oram paginae: Apud Alexandriam, passio sanctae Chaterinae virginis et martyris.]

The seventh day before the Kalends · 25 November

  • 733

The birthday of blessed Peter, bishop of Alexandria, who, being adorned with all virtues, in the divine Scriptures also inferior to none of those before him, both priest and victim of God, is suddenly seized, and by the order of Maximinus is cut off by the head. With whom at the same time many other bishops from Egypt are also slaughtered, about, with clerics and laymen, six hundred and sixty. Also at Antioch, of Saint Erasmus, martyr. [Bouillart, at the edge of the page: At Alexandria, the passion of Saint Catherine, virgin and martyr.]

VI Kal. Die 26.

  • 737

Natalis beati Lini papae, qui post Petrum primus, Romanam Ecclesiam tenuit annis duodecim, et martyrio coronatus, sepultus est in Vaticano. Apud Alexandriam, sanctorum martyrum Fausti presbiteri, Dii et Ammonii, qui cum beato Petro ejusdem urbis episcopo, jubente Maximino, trucidati sunt. Augustuduni, sancti Amatoris episcopi.

The sixth day before the Kalends · 26 November

  • 737

The birthday of blessed Linus, pope, who, first after Peter, held the Roman Church for twelve years, and, crowned with martyrdom, was buried in the Vatican. At Alexandria, of the holy martyrs Faustus, presbyter, Dius and Ammonius, who, with blessed Peter, bishop of the same city, at the command of Maximinus were slaughtered. At Augustudunum, of Saint Amator, bishop.

V Kal. Die 27.

  • 741

Apud Bononiensem civitatem, natalis sanctorum martyrum Agricolae et Vitalis, quorum posterior prioris servus fuit antea, nunc consors et collega martyrii. Denique cum cogeretur isdem a persequentibus, ut Christum negaret, et ille amplius profiteretur Dominum Jesum Christum, omnia tormentorum genera exercuerunt in eum. Novissime beatum Agricolam interemerunt, cruci affigentes. In Galliis civitate Regensi, sancti Maximi confessoris, qui a primaeva aetate virtutum omnium gratia praeditus, primum Lirinensis coenobii Pater, deinde Regensis Ecclesiae episcopus, signis et prodigiis inclitus extitit.

The fifth day before the Kalends · 27 November

  • 741

At the city Bononiensis, the birthday of the holy martyrs Agricola and Vitalis, of whom the latter was before the former's slave, now his partner and colleague in martyrdom. Then, when the same was being forced by the persecutors to deny Christ, and he professed the Lord Jesus Christ the more, they exercised every kind of torment upon him. Last of all they slew blessed Agricola, fastening him to a cross. In the Gauls, in the city Regensis, of Saint Maximus, confessor, who, endowed from his earliest age with the grace of all virtues, first Father of the monastery Lirinensis, then bishop of the Church Regensis, stood out renowned for signs and prodigies.

IV Kal. Die 28.

  • 743

Apud Corinthum, natalis beati Sostenes discipuli sancti Pauli, cujus mentionem facit idem Apostolus scribens Corinthiis. Eodem die, apud Africam, sanctorum martyrum Papinii et Mansueti episcoporum, qui 745tempore Wandalicae persecutionis, pro fidei catholicae defensione, candentibus ferri laminis toto corpore adusti, gloriosum agonem consummaverunt. Romae sancti Rufi, quem cum omni domo sua Diocletianus punitum Christo martyrem fecit.

The fourth day before the Kalends · 28 November

  • 743

At Corinth, the birthday of blessed Sostenes, disciple of Saint Paul, whom the same Apostle mentions, writing to the Corinthians. On the same day, at Africa, of the holy martyrs Papinius and Mansuetus, bishops, who, 745in the time of the Vandal persecution, for the defence of the Catholic faith, burned over their whole body with glowing plates of iron, consummated a glorious contest. At Rome, of Saint Rufus, whom Diocletian, having punished him with all his household, made a martyr to Christ.

III Kal. Die 29.

  • 747

Vigilia sancti Andreae apostoli. Romae via Salaria, natalis sancti Saturnini martyris et Sisinni diaconi, sub Maximiano principe, quos in carcere diu maceratos jussit praefectus Urbis in equuleo levari, et attrahi nervis, et fustibus ac scorpionibus caedi. Deinde jussit eis flammas ad latera apponi, et depositos de equuleo capite truncari. Eodem die, apud Tolosam, beati Saturnini episcopi, qui in Capitolio ejusdem urbis a paganis tentus, atque a summa Capitolii arce per omnes gradus praecipitatus, capite colliso, excussoque cerebro et omni corpore dilaniato, dignam Christo animam exhalavit.

The third day before the Kalends · 29 November

  • 747

The vigil of Saint Andrew the apostle. At Rome, on the Salarian Way, the birthday of Saint Saturninus, martyr, and of Sisinnius, deacon, under the emperor Maximian, whom, long wasted in prison, the prefect of the City ordered to be raised upon the rack, and stretched with sinews, and beaten with cudgels and scorpions. Then he ordered flames to be applied to their sides, and, taken down from the rack, their heads to be cut off. On the same day, at Toulouse, of blessed Saturninus, bishop, who, held by the pagans in the Capitol of the same city, and cast headlong from the highest summit of the Capitol down all the steps, his head crushed, his brain dashed out and his whole body torn apart, breathed out a soul worthy of Christ.

Pridie Kal. Die 30.

  • 751

In civitate Patras, provinciae Achaiae, natalis sancti Andreae apostoli, qui etiam apud Scithiam praedicavit. Hic beatissimus, ab Egea proconsule comprehensus, primo in carcere est clausus, ac deinde gravissime caesus, ad ultimum vero in cruce appensus, biduo inibi supervixit. Apud Sanctonas, sancti Trojani episcopi, magnae sanctitatis viri, qui sepultus terris, coelo se vivere multis virtutibus manifestat.

The day before the Kalends · 30 November

  • 751

In the city of Patras, of the province of Achaia, the birthday of Saint Andrew the apostle, who also preached at Scithia. This most blessed man, seized by the proconsul Egea, was first shut up in prison, and then most grievously beaten, but at the last hung upon a cross, survived there two days. At Sanctonas, of Saint Trojanus, bishop, a man of great holiness, who, buried in the earth, shows by many virtues that he lives in heaven.

MENSIS DECEMBER HABET DIES XXXI.

  • 755

DECEMBER HAS 31 DAYS.

  • 755

Kalendis. Die 1.

  • 755

Romae, natalis sanctorum martyrum Crisanti et Dariae virginis, qui post multas, quas pro Christo lucrati fuerant, passiones, jussi sunt a Numeriano imperatore via Salaria in arenario deponi, atque illic viventes terra ac lapidibus obrui. Item Romae, sanctorum martyrum Diodori presbyteri et Mariani diaconi, cum aliis pluribus, qui jubente praefato principe martyrii gloriam celebrarunt. Apud civitatem Moguntiam, natalis beati Albani martyris. Item civitate Noviomo, sancti Eligii episcopi et confessoris, cujus vitam admirandam multiplex signorum numerus commendat. Ipso die, sanctae Nathaliae, uxoris sancti Adriani martyris. Civitate Narnia, sancti Proculi presbyteri.

On the Kalends · 1 December

  • 755

At Rome, the birthday of the holy martyrs Crisantus and Daria, virgin, who, after many passions which they had won for Christ, were ordered by the emperor Numerian to be put down into a sand-pit on the Salarian Way, and there, living, to be overwhelmed with earth and stones. Also at Rome, of the holy martyrs Diodorus, presbyter, and Marianus, deacon, with many others, who at the command of the aforesaid emperor celebrated the glory of martyrdom. At the city of Mainz, the birthday of blessed Albanus, martyr. Also in the city of Noyon, of Saint Eligius, bishop and confessor, whose admirable life a manifold number of signs commends. On this same day, of Saint Nathalia, wife of Saint Adrianus, martyr. In the city of Narni, of Saint Proculus, presbyter.

IV Non. Die 2.

  • 759

Natalis sanctorum Veri et Securi fratrum, qui apud Africam martyrio coronati sunt. Romae, passio sanctae Vivianae martyris, quae, jubente Juliano imperatore sacrilego, tamdiu plumbatis caesa est donec spiritum redderet.

The fourth day before the Nones · 2 December

  • 759

The birthday of Saints Verus and Securus, brothers, who were crowned with martyrdom at Africa. At Rome, the passion of Saint Viviana, martyr, who, at the command of Julian the sacrilegious emperor, was beaten with leaded scourges so long, until she gave back her spirit.

III Non. Die 3.

  • 761

Romae, natalis sanctorum martyrum Claudii tribuni et uxoris ejus Hilariae, ac filiorum Jasonis et Mauri, cum septuaginta militibus, ex quibus Claudium jussit Numerianus imperator, cum ingenti saxo alligatum, in medio mari praecipitem dari, milites vero et filios ipsius Claudii, capitali sententia puniri. Tingi metropoli, passio sancti Cassiani, qui exceptoris diu gerens officium, tandem caelitus inspiratus, execrabile duxit Christianorum neci deservire cruentus, unde renuntians eidem officio, sub Christiana confessione, triumphum meruit obtinere martyrii.

The third day before the Nones · 3 December

  • 761

At Rome, the birthday of the holy martyrs Claudius, a tribune, and his wife Hilaria, and their sons Jason and Maurus, with seventy soldiers, of whom the emperor Numerian ordered Claudius, bound to a huge stone, to be given headlong into the middle of the sea, but the soldiers and the sons of Claudius himself to be punished by capital sentence. At Tingis, the metropolis, the passion of Saint Cassianus, who, long discharging the office of a recorder, at length inspired from heaven, held it execrable to serve, bloodstained, the slaughter of Christians; whence, renouncing that same office, under the Christian confession he merited to obtain the triumph of martyrdom.

Pridie Non. Die 4.

  • 765

Apud Pontum, beati Meletii episcopi et confessoris, qui cum esset eruditionum praerogativa magnificus, virtus tamen animi et vitae sinceritas longe in eo magnificentior extitit. Item, Alexandriae, sancti Clementis presbyteri, qui in divinarum eruditionum scholis quam maxime floruit. [ Addit Bouillart. in textu: In pago Bituricensi, sancti Syrani Syrani commemoratio a prima manu sine ulla litura, addita tamen, sed ab Usuardo. Notus est ille Syranus aut Sigirannus. Ejus gesta Labbeus in Bibliotheca et Mabillio in Actis Benedictinis juris publici fecerunt. BOUILLART. abbatis. Et in margine: Floriaco monasterio, translatio sanctissimi confessoris Christi Benedicti abbatis.]

The day before the Nones · 4 December

  • 765

At Pontus, of blessed Meletius, bishop and confessor, who, though he was magnificent by the prerogative of learning, yet the virtue of his mind and the sincerity of his life stood out far more magnificent in him. Also, at Alexandria, of Saint Clement, presbyter, who flourished as greatly as could be in the schools of divine learning. [Bouillart adds, in the text: In the district of Bourges, of Saint Syranus The commemoration of Syranus is in the first hand, with no erasure, added nonetheless, but by Usuardus. That Syranus, or Sigirannus, is well known. Labbe in his Bibliotheca and Mabillon in the Benedictine Acts have made his deeds public. Bouillart., abbot. And in the margin: At the monastery Floriacum, the translation of the most holy confessor of Christ, Benedict, abbot.]

Nonis. Die 5.

  • 769

In Africa, apud Coloniam Thebestinam, natalis sanctae Crispinae martyris, quae temporibus Diocletiani et Maximiani cum sacrificare noluisset, jussu Anolini proconsulis decollata est. Eodem die, in Italia, sancti Dalmacii martyris. Civitate Treveris, sancti Nicecii episcopi, totius sanctitatis viri. Item civitate Thagora, sanctorum Julii et Potamiae.

On the Nones · 5 December

  • 769

In Africa, at the colony Thebestina, the birthday of Saint Crispina, martyr, who, in the times of Diocletian and Maximian, when she would not sacrifice, was beheaded by order of the proconsul Anolinus. On the same day, in Italy, of Saint Dalmacius, martyr. In the city of Trier, of Saint Nicecius, bishop, a man of all holiness. Also in the city of Thagora, of Saints Julius and Potamia.

VIII Idus. Die 6.

  • 771

Natalis beati Nicolai episcopi Mirorum Liciae, de quo inter plura miraculorum insignia, illud memorabile fertur quod imperatorem Constantinum ab interitu quorumdam, longe constitutus, ad misericordiam et monitis per visum deflexit et minis. In Africa, sanctarum Dionisiae, Dativae, Leontiae et religiosi viri nomine Tertii et Emiliani et Bonifacii cum aliis tribus, qui omnes pro confessione fidei catholicae, gravissimis in innumeris suppliciis excruciati, confessorum Christi numero sociari meruerunt.

The eighth day before the Ides · 6 December

  • 771

The birthday of blessed Nicholas, bishop of Myra in Lycia, of whom, among many marks of miracles, this memorable thing is related: that, being set far off, he turned the emperor Constantine from the destruction of certain men to mercy, both by admonitions through a vision and by threats. In Africa, of Saints Dionisia, Dativa, Leontia, and a religious man named Tertius, and Emilianus and Boniface, with three others, who all, for the confession of the Catholic faith, tortured with most grievous, in innumerable, punishments, merited to be joined to the number of the confessors of Christ.

VII Idus. Die 7.

  • 775

Apud Alexandriam, natalis beati Agathonis martyris, sub Decio imperatore, qui cum esset militaris et prohiberet quosdam volentes illudere mortuis cadaveribus martyrum, clamor repente totius vulgi adversus eum extollitur. Oblatus autem judici, cum in confessione persisteret, capite pro pietate damnatus est. Civitate Sanctonas, sancti Martini abbatis, ad cujus tumulum creberrime divinitus fiunt miracula. In pago Meldicensi, sanctae Farae virginis.

The seventh day before the Ides · 7 December

  • 775

At Alexandria, the birthday of blessed Agatho, martyr, under the emperor Decius, who, when he was a soldier and prevented certain people who wished to mock the dead corpses of the martyrs, a clamour of the whole populace is suddenly raised against him. Offered, moreover, to the judge, when he persisted in the confession, he was condemned to the loss of his head for piety. In the city Sanctonas, of Saint Martin, abbot, at whose tomb miracles very frequently come to pass by divine power. In the district Meldicensis, of Saint Fara, virgin.

VI Idus. Die 8.

  • 777

Romae, natalis beati Euticiani papae, qui rexit Ecclesiam anno uno, et sub Aureliano martyrio coronatus est, sepultus in cimeterio Calisti, qui et ipse per diversa loca trecentos quadraginta duos martyres manu sua sepelivit. Ipso die apud Alexandriam, sancti Macharii martyris, qui tempore Decii, a judice, cum 779Amultis verbis ad negandum suaderetur, et eo majori constantia fidem suam profiteretur, vivus ad ultimum jubetur exuri. [ Bouillart., ad oram paginae: Conceptio beatissimae Dei genitricis virginis Mariae.]

The sixth day before the Ides · 8 December

  • 777

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Euticianus, pope, who ruled the Church one year, and under Aurelian was crowned with martyrdom, buried in the cemetery of Calistus, who also himself buried with his own hand, in various places, three hundred and forty-two martyrs. On this same day, at Alexandria, of Saint Macharius, martyr, who, in the time of Decius, when he was being urged by the judge 779Awith many words to deny, and with so much the greater constancy professed his faith, is at the last ordered to be burned alive. [Bouillart, at the edge of the page: The Conception of the most blessed Mother of God, the Virgin Mary.]

V Idus. Die 9.

  • 781

Natalis sanctae Leochadiae virginis, quae a praefecto Hispaniarum Daciano, apud Toletum dura [ Bouillart, 783dira], carceris custodia macerata Hoc loco folium ex codice avulsum est. Sed qui illud restituit, quia majoribus litteris usus est, hunc articulum imperfectum reliquit et plus media parte truncum. Quae deerant, ex codice Pratensi B. supplevit Sollerius. Laudo factum et imitor. BOUILLART., cum gravissimos beatae Eulaliae et reliquorum martyrum audisset cruciatus, genibus in oratione positis, impollutum Christo spiritum reddidit. Ipso die, sancti Cypriani abbatis Petragorici, magnificae sanctitatis et clarissimi in miraculis viri.

The fifth day before the Ides · 9 December

  • 781

The birthday of Saint Leochadia, virgin, who, by the prefect of the Spains Dacianus, at Toledo, worn out by the harsh [Bouillart, 783dira] custody of prison At this place a leaf has been torn out of the codex. But he who restored it, because he used larger letters, left this article unfinished and cut short by more than half. What was lacking Sollerius supplied from the Pratensis codex B. I praise the deed and imitate it. Bouillart., when she had heard of the most grievous tortures of blessed Eulalia and of the rest of the martyrs, with her knees set in prayer, gave back to Christ an undefiled spirit. On this same day, of Saint Cyprian, abbot of Périgueux, a man of magnificent holiness and most renowned in miracles.

IV Idus. Die 10.

  • 785

Apud civitatem Hispolitanam, sanctorum martyrum Carpofori presbiteri et Abundii diaconi, qui persecutione Diocletiani primo fustibus crudelissime caesi deinde in carcerem negato cibo et potu retrusi, rursum equuleo torti, et post haec diu carcere macerati, novissime gladio percussi sunt. In Hispania civitate Emerita, passio sanctae Eulaliae virginis, quae cum esset annorum tredecim, pro confessione Christi, jussu Daciani praesidis, plurima tormenta perpessa est, novissime in equuleo suspensa et exungulata, faculis ardentibus ex utroque latere appositis, hausto igne spiritum reddidit. Eodem die apud praefatam urbem, sanctae Juliae martyris, quae fuit convirginalis sanctae Eulaliae et comes individua ad passionem properanti.

The fourth day before the Ides · 10 December

  • 785

At the city Hispolitana, of the holy martyrs Carpoforus, presbyter, and Abundius, deacon, who, in the persecution of Diocletian, first beaten most cruelly with cudgels then thrust back into prison with food and drink denied them, again tortured on the rack, and after this long wasted in prison, were at last struck with the sword. In Spain, in the city of Emerita, the passion of Saint Eulalia, virgin, who, when she was thirteen years old, for the confession of Christ, by order of the governor Dacianus, suffered very many torments, at last hung upon the rack and torn with hooks, burning torches being applied on either side, drawing in the fire gave back her spirit. On the same day, at the aforesaid city, of Saint Julia, martyr, who was a fellow virgin of Saint Eulalia and an inseparable companion to her as she hastened to the passion.

III Idus. Die 11.

  • 789

Romae, natalis beati Damasi papae, et passio sancti Trasonis martyris, qui cum esset Christianissimus, jubente Maximino tentus est, ac cum aliis duobus Pontiano et Praetextato, martyrio coronatus. Civitate Ambianis, sanctorum martyrum Victorici et Fusciani. In horum naribus et auribus jussit judex tarincas immitti, et clavis candentibus capita transfigi, deinde oculos evelli, ac postremum per eorum corpora jaculari, sicque una cum sancto Gentiano hospite suo capitibus amputatis migraverunt ad Dominum. In Hispaniis, sancti Euticii Eutychius amissus fuit in Dervensi codice. BOUILLART., cujus gesta habentur.

The third day before the Ides · 11 December

  • 789

At Rome, the birthday of blessed Damasus, pope, and the passion of Saint Traso, martyr, who, since he was most Christian, was seized at the command of Maximinus, and with two others, Pontianus and Praetextatus, crowned with martyrdom. In the city of Amiens, of the holy martyrs Victoricus and Fuscianus. Into the nostrils and ears of these the judge ordered iron spikes to be driven, and their heads to be pierced through with red-hot nails, then their eyes to be torn out, and last of all to shoot through their bodies; and so, together with Saint Gentianus their host, their heads cut off, they passed to the Lord. In the Spains, of Saint Euticius Eutychius was left out in the Dervensis codex. Bouillart., whose acts are extant.

Pridie Idus. Die 12.

  • 793

Apud Narbonam, natalis sancti Pauli confessoris, quem beatus Paulus apostolus ordinatum, eidem urbi destinavit antistitem: quique cum eodem Apostolo ad Hispanias praedicandi gratia pergens, ibidem relictus est, ubi praedicationis officio non segniter impleto, clarus miraculis coronatus quievit. In Alexandria, sanctorum martyrum Epimachii et Alexandri, qui, cum essent multo tempore in vinculis, et diversis suppliciis confecti perdurassent in fide, ignibus ad postremum consumi jubentur. Item Alexandriae, sanctarum mulierum Ammonariae et Mercuriae cum aliis duabus, quae, inauditis tormentorum generibus toleratis et superatis, finem omnium, ferro caedente, suscipiunt. Item sanctorum martyrum Hermogenis, Donati et aliorum viginti duorum. In pago Vinnacensi, sancti Walerici presbyteri et confessoris.

The day before the Ides · 12 December

  • 793

At Narbonne, the birthday of Saint Paul, confessor, whom blessed Paul the apostle, having ordained him, appointed as bishop to that same city: and who, going with the same Apostle to the Spains for the sake of preaching, was left there, where, the office of preaching not slothfully fulfilled, illustrious for miracles, crowned, he rested. In Alexandria, of the holy martyrs Epimachius and Alexander, who, when they had been a long time in chains, and, worn out by various punishments, had persevered in the faith, are ordered at the last to be consumed by fires. Also at Alexandria, of the holy women Ammonaria and Mercuria, with two others, who, having endured and overcome unheard-of kinds of torments, receive the end of all, the sword striking. Also of the holy martyrs Hermogenes, Donatus and twenty-two others. In the district Vinnacensis, of Saint Walericus, presbyter and confessor.

Idibus. Die 13.

  • 797

Apud Siracusam, Siciliae civitatem, natalis sanctae Luciae virginis et martyris, sub persecutione Diocletiani, quam cum lenones corrumpere, jubente Paschasio consulari, vellent, nullatenus eam movere potuerunt, nec funibus additis, aut boum paribus plurimis. Picem, resinam, fervens oleum nil laesa suscepit, sed tandem gladio in viscera merso percussa est. Ipso die, sancti Autberti Cameracensis episcopi.

On the Ides · 13 December

  • 797

At Syracuse, a city of Sicily, the birthday of Saint Lucy, virgin and martyr, under the persecution of Diocletian, whom, when the panders wished, at the command of Paschasius the consular, to corrupt her, they could by no means move, nor with ropes added, or with very many yoke of oxen. Pitch, resin, boiling oil she received, harmed in nothing, but at length, a sword plunged into her vitals, she was struck. On this same day, of Saint Autbertus, bishop of Cambrai.

XIX Kal. Die 14.

  • 801

Apud Cyprum, natalis beati Spiridionis episcopi, qui unus fuit ex illis confessoribus quos Maximianus, dextris oculis effossis, et sinistro poplite succiso, per metalla damnaverat, quique cum propheticam duceret vitam, etiam signorum gloria inclitus fuit. Alexandriae, sanctorum martyrum Heronis, Arsenii, Isidori et Dioscori pueri, quos judex sub persecutione Deciana variis tormentis dilanians, cum pari modo constantia fidei videret armatos, tradi ignibus jubet. Dioscorus vero multipliciter flagellatus. divino nutu ad consolationem fidelium est dimissus. In Antiochia, natalis sanctorum martyrum Drusi, Zosimi, et Theodori. Item Remis, passio sancti Nicasii episcopi et Eutropiae virginis sororis ejusdem.

The nineteenth day before the Kalends · 14 December

  • 801

At Cyprus, the birthday of blessed Spiridion, bishop, who was one of those confessors whom Maximian, their right eyes dug out and the left ham cut, had condemned to the mines; and who, since he led a prophetic life, was also renowned for the glory of signs. At Alexandria, of the holy martyrs Hero, Arsenius, Isidore and Dioscorus, a boy, whom the judge, under the Decian persecution, tearing them with various torments, when he saw them armed alike with the constancy of faith, orders to be given to the fires. But Dioscorus, scourged in many ways. by the divine will was released for the consolation of the faithful. In Antioch, the birthday of the holy martyrs Drusus, Zosimus, and Theodorus. Also at Reims, the passion of Saint Nicasius, bishop, and of Eutropia, virgin, his sister.

XVIII Kal. Die 15.

  • 803

Apud Africam beati Valeriani episcopi et confessoris, qui cum esset annorum plus octoginta, extra civitatem singularis expelli jussus est, et ita praeceptum ut nullus eum neque in domo neque in agro dimitteret habitare; sicque in strata publica multo tempore nudus sub aere jacens, in confessione catholicae veritatis et defensione sanctimonii, cursum beatae vitae complevit. In territorio Aurelianensium, sancti Maximini confessoris.

The eighteenth day before the Kalends · 15 December

  • 803

At Africa, of blessed Valerianus, bishop and confessor, who, when he was more than eighty years old, was ordered to be driven out of the city alone, and it was so commanded that no one should let him dwell, neither in a house nor in a field; and thus, lying a long time naked under the open air on the public road, in the confession of Catholic truth and the defence of holiness, he completed the course of a blessed life. In the territory of Orléans, of Saint Maximinus, confessor.

XVII Kal. Die 16.

  • 807

Trium puerorum, Ananiae, Azariae et Misaelis, quorum corpora apud Babiloniam sub quodam specu sunt posita. In Tuscia, passio sanctae Barbarae virginis, sub Maximiano imperatore. Haec post diram carceris macerationem et nervorum caesionem, ac lampadarum adustionem, mamillarumque praecisionem, atque aliorum tormentorum cruciationem, ad extremum gladio data, martyrium consummavit. Ravennae, sanctorum Valentini. Naulis et Agricolae.

The seventeenth day before the Kalends · 16 December

  • 807

Of the three children, Ananias, Azarias and Misael, whose bodies are laid at Babylon under a certain cave. In Tuscia, the passion of Saint Barbara, virgin, under the emperor Maximian. She, after the dire wasting of prison and the cutting of the sinews, and the burning of lamps, and the cutting off of the breasts, and the torture of other torments, given at the last to the sword, consummated her martyrdom. At Ravenna, of Saints Valentinus. Naulis and Agricola.

XVI Kal. Die 17.

  • 809

Translatio sancti Ignatii episcopi et martyris, qui ab urbe Roma Antiochiam delatus, jacet extra portam Damniticam, in cimiterio ecclesiae. Eodem die, beati Lazari, quem Dominus Jesus in Evangelio legitur resuscitasse a mortuis. Item beatae Marthae, sororis ejus, quorum venerabilem memoriam extructa ecclesia non longe a Bethania, ubi e vicino domus eorum fuit, conservat. In Oriente apud Eleutheropolim civitatem, sanctorum martyrum quinquaginta, qui sub Sarracenis passi sunt.

The sixteenth day before the Kalends · 17 December

  • 809

The translation of Saint Ignatius, bishop and martyr, who, brought from the city of Rome to Antioch, lies outside the gate Damnitica, in the cemetery of the church. On the same day, of blessed Lazarus, whom the Lord Jesus is read in the Gospel to have raised from the dead. Also of blessed Martha, his sister, whose venerable memory a church built not far from Bethany, where close by was their house, preserves. In the East, at the city of Eleutheropolis, of fifty holy martyrs, who suffered under the Saracens.

XV Kal. Die 18.

  • 813

Apud Macedoniam, civitate Philippis, natalis beatorum martyrum Rufi et Zosimi. Hi ex eo numero discipulorum fuerunt, per quos primitiva Ecclesia in Judaeis et Graecis fundata est, de quorum etiam felici agone scribit sanctus Policarpus in epistola ad Philippenses. In Africa, passio sancti Moysetis martyris, Turonis, sancti Gatiani episcopi et confessoris, qui ab urbe Roma eidem civitati primus directus episcopus, multis clarus miraculis quievit in pace.

The fifteenth day before the Kalends · 18 December

  • 813

At Macedonia, in the city of Philippi, the birthday of the blessed martyrs Rufus and Zosimus. These were of the number of the disciples through whom the primitive Church was founded among the Jews and the Greeks, of whose happy contest also Saint Policarpus writes in the epistle to the Philippians. In Africa, the passion of Saint Moyses, martyr, At Tours, of Saint Gatianus, bishop and confessor, who, sent from the city of Rome to that same city as its first bishop, illustrious for many miracles, rested in peace.

XIV Kal. Die 19.

  • 817

Apud Aegyptum, beati Nemesii martyris, qui ab Emiliano judice geminatis suppliciis excruciatus, cum latronibus jussus est incendi, Salvatoris perferens similitudinem, qui una cum latronibus pertulit crucem. Civitate Nicea, sancti Darii martyris. Item Autisiodoro, sancti Gregorii episcopi.

The fourteenth day before the Kalends · 19 December

  • 817

At Egypt, of blessed Nemesius, martyr, who, tortured with redoubled punishments by the judge Emilianus, was ordered to be burned with robbers, bearing the likeness of the Saviour, who together with robbers endured the cross. In the city of Nicaea, of Saint Darius, martyr. Also at Auxerre, of Saint Gregory, bishop.

XIII Kal. Die 20.

  • 819

Apud Alexandriam, beatissimorum martyrum Ammonis, Zenonis, Ptolomei, Ingenii et Theophili, qui tribunalibus adstantes, cum quidam Christianus in suppliciis positus trepidaret, et jam pene ad negandum declinaret, vultu, oculis ac nutibus conabantur illum erigere. Cumque hac de causa clamor totius vulgi in eos prosiliret, prorumpentes in medium, Christianos se esse testantur. Per quorum victoriam, illico Christus gloriosissime triumphavit, suisque constantiam animi dedit. In Thracia civitate Gelduba, sancti Julii martyris. Item in Amphitrea, sancti Liberati.

The thirteenth day before the Kalends · 20 December

  • 819

At Alexandria, of the most blessed martyrs Ammon, Zeno, Ptolomeus, Ingenius and Theophilus, who, standing by the tribunals, when a certain Christian set in the punishments was trembling, and was now almost inclining to deny, were trying with face, eyes and nods to raise him up. And when for this cause the clamour of the whole populace leapt out against them, bursting forth into the midst, they testify that they are Christians. Through whose victory Christ at once most gloriously triumphed, and gave constancy of mind to his own. In Thrace, in the city of Gelduba, of Saint Julius, martyr. Also in Amphitrea, of Saint Liberatus.

XII Kal. Die 21.

  • 823

Natalis beati Thomae apostoli, qui Parthis et Medis Evangelium praedicans, passus est in India, ac non multo post inde translatus ad Edessam urbem. Eodem die in Tuscia, sanctorum Joannis et Festi.

The twelfth day before the Kalends · 21 December

  • 823

The birthday of blessed Thomas the apostle, who, preaching the Gospel to the Parthians and Medes, suffered in India, and not long after was translated thence to the city of Edessa. On the same day, in Tuscia, of Saints John and Festus.

XI Kal. Die 22.

  • 825

Romae via Lavicana inter duas lauros, natalis triginta martyrum, qui omnes uno die persecutione Diocletiani coronati sunt. Alexandriae, beati Ischyrionis martyris. Is cum ad sacrificandum blanditiis atque injuriis cogeretur, et contemneret; praeacuta sude transverberatus per viscera, neci traditur. Civitate Ostia, sanctorum Demetrii, Honorati et Flori. [ Bouillart.: Fateor Basileum hic non bene fuisse ab Usuardo annuntiatum. Nam si Hieronymianos indices secutus est, scribere debuit : Civitate Hostia, SS. Demetrii, Honorati (nam de Floro nulla mentio in pervetusto nostro codice) ; aut si Basileum animus erat commemorare, non Hostiam sed Antiochiam civitatem assignari oportebat. Quaerenti hujus errati causam, in mentem rediit diei XXI Novembris, ubi legeram : Civitate Hostia, sanctorum martyrum Demetrii et Honorii. Statim evolvi nostrum Hieronymiani Martyrologii codicem qui ad aetatem Usuardi proxime accedit, quemque verisimile est ex illo exscriptum esse, quem Usuardus in suo scribendo consuluit. Ibi utroque die, id est XXI Novembris et XXII Decembris, memoratos video eosdem sanctos ex iisdem versibus, Hostiae, SS. Demetrium et Honorium, Antiochiae, sanctum Basileum, hoc uno discrimine, quod XXII Decembris pro Honorius legatur Honoratus. Equidem conjicio ex illa sanctorum urbiumque similitudine errorem fluxisse. Fortasse Usuardus articulum suum clauserat SS. Demetrii et Honorati commemoratione. Postea in memoriam rediens eosdem sanctos XXI die Novembris fuisse consignatos, vitium scriptoris suspicatus delevit quod scripserat (nam falso monuit Castellanus nullam esse lituram), sanctum Basileum subrogavit, et memoriae lapsu pro Antiochia scripsit Hostia. Ut ut est, nihil inde auctoritatis amittit Pratensis codex qui autographi loco habebitur, donec erunt qui ex fonte haurire malint quam rivulos consectari. BOUILLART. Civitate Hostia, sancti Basilei.]

The eleventh day before the Kalends · 22 December

  • 825

At Rome on the Lavican Way between the Two Laurels, the birthday of thirty martyrs, who all on one day, in the persecution of Diocletian, were crowned. At Alexandria, of blessed Ischyrion, martyr. He, when he was being forced to sacrifice by blandishments and injuries, and despised them; pierced through the vitals with a sharpened stake, is handed over to death. In the city of Ostia, of Saints Demetrius, Honoratus and Florus. [Bouillart: I confess that Basileus was not well announced here by Usuardus. For if he followed the Hieronymian lists, he ought to have written: In the city of Ostia, of Saints Demetrius, Honoratus (for of Florus there is no mention in our very old codex); or if he had a mind to commemorate Basileus, it was not Ostia but Antioch that ought to have been assigned as the city. As I sought the cause of this error, the 21st day of November came back to my mind, where I had read: In the city of Ostia, of the holy martyrs Demetrius and Honorius. At once I unrolled our codex of the Hieronymian Martyrology, which comes nearest to the age of Usuardus, and which is likely to have been copied from the one which Usuardus consulted in writing his own. There on both days, that is 21 November and 22 December, I see the same saints recorded in the same lines, at Ostia Saints Demetrius and Honorius, at Antioch Saint Basileus, with this one difference, that on 22 December Honoratus is read for Honorius. For my part I conjecture that the error flowed from that likeness of saints and cities. Perhaps Usuardus had closed his article with the commemoration of Saints Demetrius and Honoratus. Afterwards, recalling that the same saints had been recorded on the 21st day of November, suspecting a scribe's fault he deleted what he had written (for Castellanus falsely advised that there is no erasure), put Saint Basileus in their place, and by a slip of memory wrote Ostia for Antioch. However that may be, the Pratensis codex loses no authority from it, and it will be held in place of the autograph so long as there are those who prefer to draw from the source rather than to chase after the streams. Bouillart. In the city of Ostia, of Saint Basileus.]

X Kal. Die 23.

  • 829

Apud Nicomediam, sanctorum martyrum viginti, quos Diocletiana persecutio gravissimis cruciatos tormentis, martyres Christo fecit, et beatos sanctorum numero in coelis conjunxit. Romae, natalis sanctae Victoriae virginis, sub persecutione Decii imperatoris, quae cum esset desponsata a quodam pagano, et nec nubere vellet, nec sacrificare, post multa facta miracula, inter quae plurimas virgines aggregaverat, percussa est gladio in corde a carnifice, rogatu sui sponsi. Item Romae, sancti Servuli, de quo beatus Gregorius scribit quia a primaeva aetate usque ad finem vitae paraliticus jacebat; ad cujus tumbam miracula creberrime fiunt. Parisius, dedicatio Basilicae in honore sanctae crucis et sancti Vincentii martyris, et depositio Domni Childeberti regis.

The tenth day before the Kalends · 23 December

  • 829

At Nicomedia, of twenty holy martyrs, whom the Diocletianic persecution, tortured with most grievous torments, made martyrs to Christ, and joined them, blessed, to the number of the saints in the heavens. At Rome, the birthday of Saint Victoria, virgin, under the persecution of the emperor Decius, who, when she was betrothed to a certain pagan, and would neither marry nor sacrifice, after many miracles done, among which she had gathered very many virgins, was struck with the sword in the heart by the executioner, at the request of her betrothed. Also at Rome, of Saint Servulus, of whom blessed Gregory writes that from his earliest age to the end of his life he lay paralysed; at whose tomb miracles very frequently come to pass. At Paris, the dedication of the Basilica in honour of the holy cross and of Saint Vincent, martyr, and the deposition of the lord Childebert, the king.

IX Kal. (Hic in editione Bouillartii incipit annus.) Die 24.

  • 833

Vigilia Pratenses mss. omnes, uno excepto, juxta morem antiquum Usuardi nostri aevo usitatum, annum hic incipiunt, quos sequuntur innumeri alii. Martyrologium tamen Sollerii a Circumcisione Domini, id est a Kalendis Januarii, initium ducit. BOUILLART. natalis Domini. Apud Antiochiam Siriae, natalis sanctarum virginum quadraginta, quae sub Deciana persecutione per diversa tormenta martyrium consummaverunt. Apud Spoletum civitatem Tusciae, sancti Gregorii martyris et presbyteri, temporibus Diocletiani et Maximiani imperatorum, qui primo fustibus nodosis caesus, ac deinde post craticulam et carcerem cardis ferreis in genibus percussus, sed et ardentibus lampadibus per latera incensus, tandem decollatus est. Civitate Tripoli, sancti Luciani.

The ninth day before the Kalends (Here, in Bouillart's edition, the year begins.) · 24 December

  • 833

The vigil All the manuscripts of the Prés, one excepted, begin the year here according to the ancient custom in use in the age of our Usuardus, and countless others follow them. Sollerius's Martyrology, however, takes its beginning from the Circumcision of the Lord, that is, from the Kalends of January. Bouillart. of the birthday of the Lord. At Antioch of Syria, the birthday of forty holy virgins, who under the Decian persecution consummated martyrdom through various torments. At Spoleto, a city of Tuscia, of Saint Gregory, martyr and presbyter, in the times of the emperors Diocletian and Maximian, who, first beaten with knotted cudgels, and then, after the gridiron and prison, struck on the knees with iron cards, but also set on fire along his sides with burning lamps, was at length beheaded. In the city of Tripoli, of Saint Lucian.

VIII Kal. Die 25.

  • 835

JESUS CHRISTUS FILIUS DEI in BETHLEEM JUDAE NASCITUR anno Caesaris Augusti quadragesimo secundo [ interserit Bouillart.: Sollerio non placet quod Usuardinus codex Pratensis cum non nullis aliis, inter, anno Caesaris Augusti XLII et olympiadis CXCIII legat, hebdomada, juxta Danielis prophetiam, LXVI. Haec verba ad textum pertinere non existimat, eo quod in quinque codicibus, quos hoc in loco puros censet, minime reperiantur. At hi codices Pratensi inferiores aetate sunt. Uter vero alteri est anteponendus, fons rivulo, an fonti rivulus ? Cur caeteri codices non habeant, nescio. Nam nulla hic litura est in Pratensi. Suspicor primum ab Usuardo fuisse scripta ; sed cum dictavit offerendum regi Martyrologium, hac de re opinionum varietate territum timuisse, ne quid incerti traderet. BOUILLART. ebdomada, juxta Danielis prophetiam, sexagesima sexta.], olympiadis centesimae nonagesimae tertiae. Eodem die, natalis sanctae Anastasiae sub Diocletiano imperatore, quae pro confessione Domini diutina custodia macerata, dein cum ducentis viris et septingentis feminis ad insulas Palmarias delata, ipsa quidem igni, caeteri vero variis interfectionibus martyrium celebrarunt. Romae in cimiterio Aproniani passio beatae Eugeniae virginis, quae tempore Galieni imperatoris, post virtutum insignia, post sacros virginum choros, pro Christo diu agonizans, novissime gladio jugulata est.

The eighth day before the Kalends · 25 December

  • 835

JESUS CHRIST THE SON OF GOD IS BORN in BETHLEHEM OF JUDAH in the forty-second year of Caesar Augustus [Bouillart inserts: It does not please Sollerius that the Usuardine Pratensis codex, with some others, reads between in the year of Caesar Augustus XLII and of the olympiad CXCIII, in the week, according to the prophecy of Daniel, LXVI. He does not think these words belong to the text, because they are not found at all in five codices which in this place he judges pure. But these codices are later in age than the Pratensis. Which is to be put before the other, the source before the stream, or the stream before the source? Why the other codices do not have them, I do not know. For there is no erasure here in the Pratensis. I suspect that they were first written by Usuardus; but that when he dictated the Martyrology to be offered to the king, he was alarmed by the variety of opinions on this matter and feared to hand down anything uncertain. Bouillart. in the sixty-sixth week, according to the prophecy of Daniel.], of the one hundred and ninety-third olympiad. On the same day, the birthday of Saint Anastasia under the emperor Diocletian, who, for the confession of the Lord worn out by long custody, then carried with two hundred men and seven hundred women to the Palmarian islands, she herself indeed by fire, but the rest by various killings, celebrated martyrdom. At Rome, in the cemetery of Apronianus, the passion of blessed Eugenia, virgin, who in the time of the emperor Gallienus, after the marks of virtues, after the sacred choirs of virgins, long contending for Christ, at the last had her throat cut with the sword.

VII Kal. Die 26.

  • 839

Apud Hierosolymam, natalis sancti Stephani protomartyris, qui a Judaeis non longe post passionem Christi lapidatus est, eodem scilicet anno Ascensionis ipsius. Hujus venerabile corpus tempore Honorii principis divina revelatione repertum est. Romae via Appia, depositio sancti Dionisii papae, qui fidei documentis sub Claudio imperatore clarus effulsit. Eodem die sancti Marini clarissimi viri, qui martyrii triumphum sub imperatore Martiano Romae adeptus est.

The seventh day before the Kalends · 26 December

  • 839

At Jerusalem, the birthday of Saint Stephen the protomartyr, who was stoned by the Jews not long after the passion of Christ, namely in the same year as his Ascension. The venerable body of this man was found by divine revelation in the time of the emperor Honorius. At Rome, on the Appian Way, the deposition of Saint Dionisius, pope, who shone out illustrious for the teachings of the faith under the emperor Claudius. On the same day, of Saint Marinus, a most illustrious man, who obtained the triumph of martyrdom at Rome under the emperor Marcian.

VI Kal. Die 27.

  • 843

Apud Ephesum, natalis beati Joannis apostoli et evangelistae, qui post exilii relegationem, post Apocalypsis divinae revelationem, post Evangelii descriptionem, usque ad Trajani principis tempora perseverans, totius Asiae fundavit rexitque ecclesias. Et confectus senio, sexagesimo octavo post passionem Domini anno, aetatis autem suae nonagesimo nono mortuus est, ac juxta eamdem urbem sepultus.
