Alexandriae, sancti Maximi episcopi: qui satis clarus et insignis de titulo confessionis effectus est.

The sixth day before the Kalends · 27 December

  • 843

At Ephesus, the birthday of blessed John, apostle and evangelist, who, after the banishment of exile, after the revelation of the divine Apocalypse, after the writing of the Gospel, persevering until the times of the emperor Trajan, founded and ruled the churches of all Asia. And, worn out with old age, in the sixty-eighth year after the passion of the Lord, but of his own age the ninety-ninth, he died, and was buried near the same city. At Alexandria, of Saint Maximus, bishop: who was made quite illustrious and distinguished by the title of confession.

V Kal. Die 28.

  • 847

Bethleem, natalis sanctorum Innocentium, qui ab Herode rege interfecti sunt. Ancira Galatiae, sanctorum Eutici presbiteri et Domitiani diaconi.

The fifth day before the Kalends · 28 December

  • 847

At Bethlehem, the birthday of the holy Innocents, who were slain by King Herod. At Ancyra of Galatia, of Saints Euticus, presbyter, and Domitianus, deacon.

IV Kal. Die 29.

  • 849

Hierosolimis, David regis. Apud Arelatem, natalis sancti Trophimi, cujus meminit Paulus scribens ad Timotheum, qui ab eodem Apostolo episcopus ordinatus, praefatae urbi primus ob Christi Evangelium praedicandum directus est. Ex cujus fonte, ut papa Zosimus scribit, tota Gallia fidei rivulos accepit. In pago Oximensi, sancti Ebrulfi confessoris. [ Addit Bouillart: Hic sane mirandus Sollerius, qui, rejectis mss. codicibus omnibus etiam antiquis, soli Molanae editioni adhaereat. Quasi vero Pratensis codex, ipso fatente, si non primigenius, saltem antiquissimus, majoris non esset auctoritatis. Neque refert quod sancta Melania in fine articuli adjecta sit, cum legatur in nonnullis codicibus cum ab ipso laudatis, tum in Prat. cod. B. D. E. et Montisburg. Praeterea hoc additamentum non ab aliena, sed a primaria manu exaratum est; qua de causa hoc die Melaniam Usuardus memoraverit, incompertum. Festus ejus dies colitur vulgo XXXI. Quod spectat ad sanctum Thomam Cantuariensem, quem manu Gothica scriptum et sub finem adjectum cl. Castellanus in famosa illa epistola a Sollerio toties laudata asserit, id omnino a veritate alienum. De sancto Thoma ne verbum quidem in Pratensi codice. Jam hinc perspiciat veri studiosus lector quantum in reliquis Castellano credendum sit. BOUILLART. Item Hierosolymis, sanctae Melaniae Deo sacratae.]

The fourth day before the Kalends · 29 December

  • 849

At Jerusalem, of David the king. At Arles, the birthday of Saint Trophimus, whom Paul mentions, writing to Timothy, who, ordained bishop by the same Apostle, was sent as the first to the aforesaid city to preach the Gospel of Christ. From whose fountain, as Pope Zosimus writes, all Gaul received the streams of faith. In the district Oximensis, of Saint Ebrulfus, confessor. [Bouillart adds: Here Sollerius is surely to be wondered at, who, rejecting all the manuscript codices, even the ancient ones, clings to Molanus's edition alone. As if the Pratensis codex, by his own admission, if not the original, at least the most ancient, were not of greater authority. Nor does it matter that Saint Melania was added at the end of the article, since she is read in some codices, both those cited by him and in the Pratensis codices B, D, E and the Montisburg. Besides, this addition was written not by a strange hand but by the principal one; for what cause Usuardus commemorated Melania on this day is not known. Her feast day is commonly kept on the 31st. As regards Saint Thomas of Canterbury, whom the celebrated Castellanus, in that famous letter so often praised by Sollerius, asserts was written in a Gothic hand and added towards the end, that is wholly foreign to the truth. Of Saint Thomas there is not so much as a word in the Pratensis codex. Now from this let the reader who is zealous for the truth see how far Castellanus is to be believed in the rest. Bouillart. Also at Jerusalem, of Saint Melania, consecrated to God.]

III Kal. Die 30.

  • 853

Apud Spoletum, natalis beati Sabini episcopi, Exuperantii et Marcelli diaconorum, et Venustiani cum uxore et filiis, sub Maximiano imperatore. Ex quibus Marcellus et Exuperantius primo equuleo suspensi, dein fustibus graviter macerati, post ungulis abrasi, et laterum adustione assati, martyrium compleverunt. Venustianus autem non multo post, una cum uxore et filiis, gladio necatus. Sanctus vero Sabinus post manuum detruncationem et diutinam carceris religationem, ad mortem usque caesus est. Horum itaque martyrium, licet diverso extiterit tempore, una tamen recolitur die. Alexandriae, sancti Mansueti martyris, cum aliis decem.

The third day before the Kalends · 30 December

  • 853

At Spoleto, the birthday of blessed Sabinus, bishop, of Exuperantius and Marcellus, deacons, and of Venustianus with his wife and sons, under the emperor Maximian. Of whom Marcellus and Exuperantius, first hung upon the rack, then heavily wasted with cudgels, afterwards scraped with hooks, and roasted by the burning of their sides, completed their martyrdom. Venustianus, however, not long after, together with his wife and sons, was slain with the sword. But Saint Sabinus, after the cutting off of his hands and a long binding in prison, was beaten even to death. Their martyrdom, therefore, although it took place at different times, is nevertheless kept on one day. At Alexandria, of Saint Mansuetus, martyr, with ten others.

Pridie Kal. Die 31.

  • 857

Romae, natalis sancti Silvestri papae, cujus actus clari habentur. Apud Senones, beatorum Sabiniani episcopi et Potentiani, qui a pontifice Romano ad praedicandum directi, eamdem metropolim martyrii sui confessione illustrem fecerunt. Prat. codicem cum Antuerp.-Maj. et Rosweyd. excedere contendit Sollerius eo quod post caesa est addant jussu Aureliani principis. Quis non videat haec ab ipso auctore esse descripta, ut tempus sanctae Columbae martyrii accuratius demonstraretur ? Haec verba, jussu Aureliani principis, loco quidem eraso, sed prima scripta sunt manu. Lectionem eamdem habent codices Pratenses B et D, Colbert. B, Victorin. B, Conchensis, Pontisar., Sandionys. et Montisburgensis. Notandum in eodem hoc articulo haec verba, qui a pontifice Romano ad etc. in litura versari, cujus nullum in Dervensi codice vestigium, quaeque adeo ante oblatum Carolo Martyrologium facta fuerat. Duae sunt igitur in isto articulo liturae diversi temporis. Eruditos rogo inspectum veniant an diversa scriptura sit. BOUILLART. Et in eadem urbe, sanctae Columbae virginis: quae superato igne, gladio caesa est [ Addit Bouillart.: jussu Aureliani principis.] Retiariae, sancti Hermetis exorcistae.

Et tandem claudunt extremum festa Decembrem.

AD MAJOREM DEI GLORIAM.

The day before the Kalends · 31 December

  • 857

At Rome, the birthday of Saint Silvester, pope, whose illustrious acts are extant. At Senones, of blessed Sabinianus, bishop, and Potentianus, who, sent by the Roman pontiff to preach, made that same metropolis illustrious by the confession of their martyrdom. Sollerius contends that the Pratensis codex, with the Antuerp.-Maj. and the Rosweyd., goes too far, in that after was cut down they add by order of the emperor Aurelian. Who does not see that these words were written by the author himself, so that the time of Saint Columba's martyrdom might be shown more exactly? These words, by order of the emperor Aurelian, are indeed in an erased place, but written in the first hand. The same reading is had by the codices of the Prés B and D, the Colbert. B, the Victorin. B, the Conchensis, the Pontisar., the Sandionys. and the Montisburgensis. It is to be noted that in this same article these words, who by the Roman pontiff to, etc., lie on an erasure, of which there is no trace in the Dervensis codex, and which therefore had been made before the Martyrology was offered to Charles. There are therefore two erasures of different times in this article. I ask the learned to come and inspect whether the script is different. Bouillart. And in the same city, of Saint Columba, virgin: who, having overcome the fire, was cut down with the sword [Bouillart adds: by order of the emperor Aurelian.] At Retiaria, of Saint Hermes, exorcist.

And at length the feasts close December, the last.

TO THE GREATER GLORY OF GOD.

On this text

Cruces for this work — where the plate is defective or the reading uncertain →

The martyrology of Usuard, monk of Saint-Germain-des-Prés, part two: the calendar from May to December, continuing the first part in Migne’s previous volume. For each day it lists the martyrs and confessors commemorated, with place, date of death, and a line or two on the manner of it. The text is Migne’s reprint of the edition by the Bollandist Jean-Baptiste du Sollier (Sollerius), with the readings of the 1718 Saint-Germain edition of Jacques Bouillart set in square brackets. Only Usuard’s text is translated. On Migne’s pages, most of the space goes to Sollerius’s commentary on each day (his notes, variant readings, observations and supplements from other manuscripts); it is present in Migne and not translated here. Bouillart’s own notes, keyed to the text by a raised letter, were recovered from the printed page and are translated: our digital source does not carry them. Many concern a Saint-Germain manuscript that Bouillart believed to be Usuard’s autograph, and they record, entry by entry, which lines stand on an erasure or in a later hand. Each day is headed as Usuard dates it, by the Roman calendar, with the modern date beside it. Migne’s volume survives in two printings that differ in a handful of letters. Where one prints a misprint and the other the sound word, the sound word is followed.

Du bon, à bon marché — carried to its limit: the good, free.
